## How M0 advances the Sprint Goal

The Sprint Goal has three clauses; M0 owns the third and is the hard gate on the second.

- **"repair the gate-index defect"** — BUG-042 *is* that clause. Three `section(N)` criteria in `readiness-gates.md` name a heading and evaluate a different one. Two of them (`cr.sandbox-paths-declared`, `epic.affected-files-declared`) leave the CR Execution Sandbox and the Epic Affected Files list **gated by nothing**, and the failure presents as a green 8/8 or 12/12 pass, so it reads as "checked and fine" rather than "not checked". Correcting the three indices moves the sprint's declared metric from **9/12 → 12/12**.
- **"that blocks the second one"** — the Task Breakdown surface (STORY-054-06, M2) inserts a `## Task Breakdown` heading into `story.md`, `CR.md` and `Bug.md`. Every insertion shifts positional indices. Landing 054-06 on an uncorrected, unpinned registry means converting a wrong index into a *differently* wrong index with nothing to notice. M0 is what makes M2 a build-break instead of a silent redirect.
- **"a pre-sprint SPIKE charter"** — STORY-054-02 (M1) appends two spike gate blocks to the same file and must rebase on M0's corrections; STORY-054-01 ships `spike.md`, a new template whose own section indices will be authored against the corrected mental model. M0 lands the correct model first.

STORY-054-05 is not a nice-to-have half. **The metric is "criteria resolving to their named heading", not "criteria carrying the right number today."** A test that pins literal integers records the current numbers and asserts nothing about naming; it would go green on a tree where `## Task Breakdown` shifted `sandbox-paths-declared` off Execution Sandbox again, because the integer would still be 6. Only a test that resolves index → heading text and compares that text to an independently-authored statement of intent moves the metric — and keeps it moved. See **§Test shape**, which is normative, not advisory.

## Order

**Strict. No parallelism inside M0. No concurrent worktrees.**

1. **wave1 — BUG-042.** Head of the `readiness-gates.md` merge chain (`042 → 05 → 02 → 06 → 111`, sprint plan §2.2). Three one-line index edits × 2 trees + one prose paragraph.
2. **wave2 — STORY-054-05.** Authored and merged after BUG-042 is on `sprint/S-39`.

**Why not parallel:** both items read `readiness-gates.md`; 054-05's fixture is a statement about the corrected content of the file 042 edits. Cutting both worktrees at once produces a rebase on the sprint's hottest file (5 of 18 items touch it) for zero wall-clock gain — 042 is a ~10-line change.

**Cross-milestone:** M0 blocks M2 entirely (054-06, 054-07) and blocks M1-wave2 (054-02 appends to `readiness-gates.md`). M4's `CR-111` is the tail of the same chain. M1-wave1 (054-01 ‖ 054-04) is surface-disjoint from M0 and may run concurrently with it at the orchestrator's discretion.

**Sequencing decision — 054-05 is authored against the *corrected* tree, not the pre-fix tree.** The sprint plan §2.3 offers both routes ("Author it expecting three known failures, or merge BUG-042 first and author against the corrected tree"). **Take the second.** Rationale and the discipline that replaces Red-first are in STORY-054-05 §Red-phase contract below — the red-ness is relocated into the mutation scenarios, which are red-by-construction and stay red-capable forever, rather than into three transient failures that stop being reproducible the moment 042 merges.

### Required fourth edit — the vocabulary note

The Predicate Vocabulary entry at `readiness-gates.md:27-34` says *"Splits the document body on `## ` heading boundaries (1-indexed)"* and never states that the **printed ordinal is not the index**. That silence is the authoring defect: three criteria were written by people who believed they were counting correctly. Correcting the numbers without correcting the doc ships the same trap to the next author — and EPIC-052 and STORY-054-06 are both queued to add headings.

Append to the `section(<N>)` entry (after the two `Example:` sentences at `:34`), both trees, verbatim:

```markdown
**`N` is a position, not a printed ordinal.** Sections are counted in document order over `## ` headings, so a template whose first heading is `## 0.5 Open Questions` or `## 0. AI Coding Agent Handoff` shifts every later section by one — and unnumbered headings (`## Existing Surfaces`, `## Prior work`, `## Why not simpler?`) consume positions too. `## 3. Execution Sandbox` in `CR.md` is `section(6)`, not `section(3)`. Inserting any `## ` heading into a gated template renumbers every criterion below it. The pinning test (`cleargate-cli/test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts`) enumerates every `section(N)` criterion and asserts it resolves to the heading its id names — it is what turns that renumbering into a build break. Update the fixture there in the same commit as any template heading change.
```

This paragraph is inside a prose section of a file that is never itself gate-evaluated, so it shifts nothing.

### Test scenarios (from Gherkin)

> **AMENDED 2026-08-27 — shape-explicit scenarios.** The previous S1 and S4 asserted the corrected criterion "now FAILS" without saying **which empty shape** was tested. That claim holds only when the subsection *label* is deleted along with the content. QA-Red verified the epic case; the Orchestrator independently verified the CR case, which QA-Red missed — its S1 fixture deleted `**Modify:**` along with the paths, so it exercised the bare shape, not the authoring shape. S1 and S4 are now shape-explicit; **S1b and S4b are added and assert a PASS as known residue, not as a regression.** Mechanism, scope and ownership: **R10**. BUG-042's four edits, file surface, and merge position are **unchanged** — nothing else in this section moved.

BUG-042 §5 specifies the locking test but **assigns it to STORY-054-05** ("This test is also the prerequisite EPIC-054 WS5 depends on"). Do not write two tests. BUG-042's own verification is the reproduction protocol in its §2, executed as a manual QA-Verify step:

**Two empty shapes exist, and the corrected criteria behave differently on each.** Both `affected-files-declared` and `sandbox-paths-declared` carry `≥1 declared-item` thresholds, and `countDeclaredItems` (`readiness-predicates.ts:712-763`) counts a bare bold subsection **label** as a declared item. So:

- **bare shape** — heading present, the `**Affected Files:**` / `**Modify:**` label itself deleted, nothing beneath. Counts **0**. The corrected criterion **fails**.
- **label-bearing shape** — the shipped template's label survives, zero content beneath it. Counts **2** for `epic.md` §4 (`**Affected Files:**` + `**Data Changes:**`) and **1** for `CR.md` §3 (`**Modify:**`). The corrected criterion **passes**.

The label-bearing shape is how humans and agents actually empty a section. **QA-Verify must not certify "fail-open closed" on the strength of a bare-shape fixture, and must not kick back a correct implementation because a label-bearing fixture passes.** Both failure modes are live; the scenario pairs below are what prevent them.

- **S1 — bare `## 3. Execution Sandbox`** (heading present, `**Modify:**` label deleted, zero paths) → `sandbox-paths-declared` now **FAILS**: `section 6 has 0 declared-item (≥1 required)`. Pre-fix this passed 8/8 (BUG-042 §3 raw output). This is the fail-open closing **for this shape only**. Fixture: `BUG-042-qa-red-fixtures/pre-fix/cr-s1-empty-sandbox.md` — note this fixture deletes the label, which is why QA-Red read it as a clean close.
- **S1b — label-bearing `## 3. Execution Sandbox`** (`**Modify:**` present, zero paths beneath) → `cr.ready-to-apply` still **PASSES 8/8**. **EXPECTED. Known residue (R10), not a regression, not a BUG-042 defect.** No fixture exists yet for this shape; QA-Verify authors one by re-adding the bare `**Modify:**` line to the S1 fixture. Do not kick back on this PASS.
- **S2 — empty `## 2. Blast Radius & Invalidation`** → `blast-radius-populated` **FAILS** and `sandbox-paths-declared` **passes** (it no longer reads Blast Radius). Unchanged — the CR §2 body is checkbox bullets, not a label, so both shapes coincide here.
- **S3 — empty `## 1. The Context Override`** → the gate **passes**. Post-fix, §1 is gated by nothing — accepted, see R2.
- **S4 — bare `## 4. Technical Grounding`** (heading present, **both** `**Affected Files:**` and `**Data Changes:**` labels deleted) → `affected-files-declared` **FAILS**: `section 8 has 0 declared-item`. Pre-fix it passed 12/12. Fixture: `BUG-042-qa-red-fixtures/post-fix-probe/epic-s4c-diagnostic-no-labels.md`.
- **S4b — label-bearing `## 4. Technical Grounding`** (labels present, zero bullets beneath) → `epic.ready-for-decomposition` still **PASSES 12/12**. **EXPECTED. Known residue (R10), not a regression.** Verified by QA-Red against the corrected-registry probe with three fixtures: `epic-s4-empty-affected-files.md`, `epic-s4b-empty-both.md`, `epic-s4d-label-only.md` — all pass. Do not kick back on these PASSes; do not "harden" `countDeclaredItems` or the epic template to make them fail.
- **S5 — `git diff cleargate-cli/src/lib/readiness-predicates.ts` is empty.** Hard constraint. R10 does **not** relax it — the label-counting fix is BUG-050's, not BUG-042's.

- **S7 — `npm --prefix cleargate-cli test` green**, `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run typecheck` clean. No behavioural code changed, so this is a regression check only.

**Net acceptance statement for QA-Verify.** BUG-042 is accepted when every criterion resolves to the heading its id names — the 9/12 → 12/12 metric — and S1/S2/S4 flip as above. It is **not** conditioned on S1b/S4b failing. "The criterion now reads the right section" is the deliverable; "the criterion is now hard to satisfy vacuously" is BUG-050's.

### Gotchas surfaced from code inspection

- **`cleargate-cli/templates/cleargate-planning/.cleargate/knowledge/readiness-gates.md` is a third copy.** It is currently byte-identical to the other two (verified). It is **gitignored** in the `cleargate-cli` repo (`cleargate-cli/.gitignore:5` → `templates/cleargate-planning/`) and regenerated by `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run prebuild` (`scripts/copy-planning-payload.mjs`). **Do not hand-edit it and do not commit it.** Regenerating is a Gate-4/release concern per CLAUDE.md, not a BUG-042 deliverable — but if the Developer's local tree has a stale payload copy, any test that reads it will read pre-fix content. The pinning test must not read the payload as its primary source (see 054-05).
- **`npm --prefix cleargate-cli test` does not run `prebuild`.** `package.json` `scripts.test` invokes `run-default-tests.mjs` directly. The payload copy therefore may be **absent entirely** in a fresh clone. Any test that hard-requires it fails on a clean checkout.
- **The live `/.claude/` instance is irrelevant here.** `.cleargate/knowledge/` and `.cleargate/templates/` are **tracked** in the outer repo (`git ls-files` confirms 9 templates + `readiness-gates.md` in both trees). Only `/.claude/**` is gitignored. Both of BUG-042's file surfaces are worktree-materializable — this story is safe to execute in `.worktrees/BUG-042/`.
- **Real-corpus impact of the epic correction is not uniform** — see Cross-story risks R1. In particular `EPIC-031` has only 7 `##` sections and would report `section 8 not found (body has 7 sections)`. This is expected under the recorded "lazy re-check on reopen" decision. **Do not add a fallback, do not soften the criterion, do not bulk-rewrite the archive.** Both were decided in BUG-042's Open Questions.
- **`countDeclaredItems` (`readiness-predicates.ts:712-763`) counts a bare subsection *label* as a declared item — which is what makes the corrected criterion vacuous, not what makes it safe.**
  > **AMENDED 2026-08-27.** The previous wording of this bullet read "… so they count ≥1 comfortably; the corrected criterion is not vacuously strict." That framing is inverted. The label-counting is not reassurance that the criterion is satisfiable — it is the reason the criterion is satisfiable **by boilerplate alone**. Corrected below; see **R10**.

  The definition-list-term branch matches `/^(\*{1,2}|_{1,2})?[A-Z][^|*\n]*(\*{1,2}|_{1,2})?:/` against each trimmed line. A bold label standing alone — `**Modify:**`, `**Affected Files:**`, `**Data Changes:**` — matches it and counts **1**, with nothing declared beneath. Replayed over the shipped template bodies: `epic.md` §4 label-only → **2**, `CR.md` §3 label-only → **1**; delete the labels too and both → **0**. Both corrected criteria threshold at `≥1`, so **both pass on an empty-but-labeled section.** The italic guidance lines (`*(Restrict the agent's scope…)*`, `*(AI Planning Engine:…)*`) do **not** match — they open with `*(`, and `[A-Z]` cannot consume `(`.
  The corpus survey still holds and is still relevant: across all 25 pending-sync CRs none drops below 1 declared item at the corrected indices (min observed = 2, `CR-089` / `CR-109`) — i.e. **no in-flight item breaks on the correction.** What that survey does not show, and was previously misread as showing, is that the criterion has teeth. It does not, for the label-bearing shape. That gap is **BUG-050**, quarantined out of this sprint; do not close it here.

## STORY-054-05 — Pin every gated section index to the heading it names

**Cross-story coupling.** Hard successor of BUG-042 (consumes its three corrections). Becomes an **adjudicator** for STORY-054-02 (spike gate blocks → new fixture rows), STORY-054-06 (`## Task Breakdown` in three templates → three index shifts), CR-111 (test-layer table in the same three templates), STORY-054-01 (`spike.md`, a seventh template), and EPIC-052 WS1 (`## Grounding` in five templates, next sprint). Every one of those five must update the fixture **in the same commit** as its template/registry edit. That obligation is the story's real deliverable.

> **AMENDED 2026-08-27 — R10 impact check: STORY-054-05 is UNAFFECTED. Scope is NOT widened.** The label-counting defect (R10 / BUG-050) lives in `countDeclaredItems`; 054-05 asserts **index → heading resolution** and never evaluates a threshold. Its four Gherkin scenarios (story §2.1) are "resolves to the heading its criterion id names", "a reverted correction is caught", "a new heading is caught", "the test does not drift from the evaluator" — all positional. Grepping the story for `threshold`, `≥`, `>=`, `declared-item`, `countDeclared` returns **zero hits**. S1–S6 in §Test shape below compare heading lines, not item counts, and none of them changes. **Corollary the Developer must not act on:** 054-05 will not detect BUG-050 either — a criterion correctly aimed at a section it can satisfy from boilerplate still resolves to the right heading and still passes S1. That is the correct boundary. Do **not** add an item-count assertion, a threshold-adequacy check, or a seventh scenario to this story.

### Schema changes

None. Test-only. `readiness-gates.md` is **read**, not written, by this story — 054-05 occupies position 2 in the merge chain because it *reads* the file, not because it edits it.

### Red-phase contract (normative — resolves the "test-only story has no Red" problem)

BUG-042 merges first, so a test that only pins live indices is **green on authoring** and never demonstrates it can fail. That is precisely the failure mode the dispatch brief warns about. The Red phase is therefore relocated, not skipped:

- **Red comes from the mutation scenarios (S2, S3, S5 below).** They drive the checker against *synthetic mutated copies* in a tmpdir and assert it reports a specific named failure. They are red until the checker exists and stay red-capable forever — they do not decay the way "three transient failures against a pre-fix tree" would.
- **Ratio:** of the 6 scenarios, 3 are positive (live tree) and 3 are mutation. **A submission whose only assertions read the live tree is a QA kick-back**, regardless of green.
- Red-test file naming for the QA-Red artifact: `*.red.node.test.ts` (matches `test/**/*.node.test.ts` in `run-default-tests.mjs:24`, so it is picked up by the default runner). Precedent and header shape: `cleargate-cli/test/lib/readiness-predicates-heading-anchor.red.node.test.ts:1-21`, which documents its own `Expected baseline fail count: 3`. Mirror that header convention — state the expected red count explicitly.

### Test shape (normative)

**Primary file:** `cleargate-cli/test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts`
**Fixture:** `cleargate-cli/test/fixtures/gate-section-index/expected-headings.ts` (a typed literal, not JSON — the fixture is a statement of intent and should carry its rationale in comments).

`test/docs/` is the right directory: it already holds the doc-lint tier (`dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts`, `pin-placeholder-coverage.node.test.ts`, `autonomy-contract.red.node.test.ts`). Note `test/fixtures/**` is excluded from the default runner (`run-default-tests.mjs:27`), so a `.ts` module there is imported, never executed as a test — correct for a fixture.

**Source of truth for reads: the CANONICAL tree.**

```
CLI_ROOT  = path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..')            // cleargate-cli/
META_ROOT = path.resolve(CLI_ROOT, '..')                   // repo root
CANON     = path.join(META_ROOT, 'cleargate-planning', '.cleargate')
LIVE      = path.join(META_ROOT, '.cleargate')
```

Exact precedent: `cleargate-cli/test/docs/dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts:39-41` (`CLI_ROOT`/`META_ROOT` derivation) and `cleargate-cli/test/agents/reporter-content.node.test.ts:15-28` (reads `cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/reporter.md` from `REPO_ROOT`). **Reuse that derivation verbatim; do not invent a third one.**

Why canonical and not live, and not the payload:
- **canonical** (`cleargate-planning/.cleargate/**`) is the CLAUDE.md-declared source of truth and is **tracked** (`git ls-files` confirms).
- **live** (`.cleargate/**`) is also tracked here, but in a target install it is the *installed* copy; pinning it alone would pin a per-install artifact.
- **payload** (`cleargate-cli/templates/cleargate-planning/**`) is gitignored and only exists after `prebuild`, which `npm test` does not run. Reading it as primary makes the test fail on a clean clone.

**Guard, following `dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts:66-70`:** `{ skip: !fs.existsSync(CANON) }` at the describe level, so the file degrades to skipped inside an extracted npm tarball rather than throwing. It must **not** be skipped inside the meta-repo — add one always-on assertion that `CANON` exists when `META_ROOT/cleargate-planning` exists, so the skip can never silently swallow the whole suite.

**Six scenarios.** The Gherkin in STORY-054-05 §2.1 has four; S5 and S6 below are additions this plan requires — S5 because the sprint's own §2.3 warns EPIC-052/054-06 will re-shift, S6 because two criteria are un-pinnable today and silence must not read as coverage.

1. **S1 — Every gated index resolves to its named heading (positive, live canonical tree).** Enumerate every `section(N)` criterion across every gate block; for each, resolve index → heading line in the matching template; assert equality against the fixture. Must cover **12** criteria and assert the count is 12 (see S6).
2. **S2 — A reverted correction is caught (mutation).** Copy `readiness-gates.md` to tmpdir with `cr.sandbox-paths-declared` rewritten back to `section(3)`; run the checker against the tmpdir registry + real templates; assert it reports failure, and assert the message contains all three of: `sandbox-paths-declared`, the expected heading `## 3. Execution Sandbox`, and the resolved heading `## 2. Blast Radius & Invalidation`. Repeat for `cr.blast-radius-populated` and `epic.affected-files-declared` — **three reversion cases, one per BUG-042 correction.**
3. **S3 — A new heading is caught (mutation).** Copy `CR.md` to tmpdir with `## 9. Task Breakdown` inserted immediately after `## 2. Blast Radius & Invalidation`; assert the checker fails and names **every** criterion whose resolved heading changed (here: `sandbox-paths-declared`), not just the first. This is the exact mutation STORY-054-06 will perform for real.
4. **S4 — The test does not drift from the evaluator (source pin).** Read `cleargate-cli/src/lib/readiness-predicates.ts` as text and assert it still contains the three load-bearing lines the checker replicates — `body.split(/^(?=## )/m)` (`:640`), `!rawParts[0]!.startsWith('## ')` (`:646`), and `hasPreamble ? parsed.index : parsed.index - 1` (`:648`). If any is edited, this fails with a message telling the reader to re-sync the checker. **This is how Requirement 2 is satisfied without touching the evaluator** — see §Rejected approaches.
5. **S5 — Adding a template heading requires a fixture edit (guard for 054-06 / CR-111 / EPIC-052).** Assert the checker's enumeration is **exhaustive and closed**: every `section(N)` criterion parsed out of the registry must have a fixture entry, and every fixture entry must correspond to a live criterion. A criterion with no fixture row fails loudly (`no expected heading declared for <type>.<id> — add one to expected-headings.ts`); an orphan fixture row fails too. Without this, 054-02's two new spike criteria would be silently unpinned.
6. **S6 — The un-pinnable set is explicit, not silent.** `proposal` / ready-for-decomposition declares two `section(N)` criteria (`architecture-populated` `section(2)`, `touched-files-populated` `section(3)`) and **there is no `proposal.md` template on disk** in either tree — `proposal` is a registered `WorkItemType` (`cleargate-cli/src/lib/work-item-type.ts:8,17,31,76`) with a gate and no template. The checker must carry an explicit `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE` set naming exactly those two, assert the set has size 2, and fail if a criterion falls outside both the fixture and that set. The moment `proposal.md` is added, the test demands a fixture row. **14 criteria total = 12 pinned + 2 explicitly un-pinnable.** Silence is not a pass.

**Failure-message contract (Requirement 3), one line per finding:**

```
<work_item_type>.<criterion-id>: section(<N>) in <template> resolves to "<actual heading line>", expected "<fixture heading line>"
```

Assert on the message text in S2/S3, not merely on `assert.throws`. A pinning test whose failure output does not name the criterion sends the next reader back to re-derive the split by hand — which is how this defect was born.

### Reuse (no duplication)

| What | Where | How to use it |
|---|---|---|
| `parsePredicate` | `cleargate-cli/src/lib/readiness-predicates.ts:43` (**exported**) | **Import it.** Use it to classify each `check:` string and read `.index` off `kind === 'section'`. Do **not** hand-write a `/section\((\d+)\)/` regex — a divergent predicate parser in the test guarding a parser defect is the BUG-041 shape all over again. |
| `headingTitleOf` | `cleargate-cli/src/lib/readiness-predicates.ts:459` (**exported**) | Available if the fixture is expressed as titles rather than full heading lines. **Prefer full heading lines** in the fixture (`## 3. Execution Sandbox`) — the printed ordinal is part of what makes a drift human-legible, and `headingTitleOf` strips it. |
| gate-block fence parsing | `cleargate-cli/src/commands/gate.ts:72-98` `loadGateBlocks` — **not exported** | Replicate the regex `/^```yaml\n([\s\S]*?)^```/gm` and the `Array.isArray(parsed) ? parsed[0] : parsed` unwrap **exactly**, and cite `gate.ts:85` in a comment. The unwrap is FLASHCARD-backed (`readiness-gates.md` fenced blocks are YAML *lists*); dropping it yields `undefined` criteria and a vacuous green. Do **not** export `loadGateBlocks` — that is a `src/` change on a story whose §3.3 says "test-only". |
| `CLI_ROOT`/`META_ROOT` derivation + `existsSync` skip guard | `cleargate-cli/test/docs/dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts:39-41,66-70` | Copy the pattern. |
| meta-repo file read from a cli test | `cleargate-cli/test/agents/reporter-content.node.test.ts:15-28` | Precedent that this direction of read is sanctioned. |
| `js-yaml` with `CORE_SCHEMA` | `cleargate-cli/src/wiki/parse-frontmatter.ts:14,40` | Use plain `yaml.load` for the gate blocks (as `gate.ts` does); `CORE_SCHEMA` matters for frontmatter timestamps, not for these blocks. |

**Adjacent-implementation audit (`git log sprint/S-39 --name-only`).** Two commits on the branch ahead of `main`: `ec5e6d56` (docs/planning only) and `4b171393` (`fix(BUG-049)`). BUG-049 touched `.cleargate/scripts/collision_surface.sh` — it exports no importable module, but see Cross-story risk R5. **No JS/TS module has merged on `sprint/S-39` that either M0 item could reuse.** `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/sprint-context.md` §Adjacent Implementations is still `(populated as stories merge)` and is accurate.

### Rejected approaches (do not re-litigate)

- **Export `evalSection`, or add `resolveSectionHeading()` to `readiness-predicates.ts`.** Cleanest anti-drift design, and **forbidden**: the Execution Guidelines and STORY-054-05 Requirement 5 both say the file is not modified, and its DoD asserts `git diff` on it is empty. S4's source-text pin is the sanctioned substitute.
- **Change `evalSection` to resolve by printed ordinal.** BUG-042 Open Question 1, decided 2026-08-25: renumber the criteria. It would break rows 3–4 and 7–12 of the resolution table, which are correct *because* they are positional.
- **Copy the templates into `test/fixtures/` and pin the copies.** Pins a copy; real-template drift becomes invisible; defeats the entire purpose.
- **Derive the expected headings from the templates.** STORY-054-05 §1.4, decided: hand-written fixture. A derived expectation re-derives the wrong answer.
- **Read the npm payload as primary.** Gitignored; absent on a clean clone; `npm test` does not run `prebuild`.

### File surface — STORY-054-05 (exhaustive)

| Repo | Path | Change |
|---|---|---|
| `cleargate-cli` (own git repo) | `cleargate-cli/test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` | **new** |
| `cleargate-cli` | `cleargate-cli/test/fixtures/gate-section-index/expected-headings.ts` | **new** |
| — | `cleargate-cli/src/**` | **forbidden — zero diff** |
| — | `.cleargate/knowledge/readiness-gates.md` (either tree) | **read-only** for this story |
| — | `.cleargate/templates/*.md` (either tree) | **read-only** for this story |

**Two-tree note.** 054-05 is the one M0 item that is *not* a two-tree edit — its deliverable lives entirely in `cleargate-cli/`, which has no `cleargate-planning/` mirror. It **enforces** the two-tree rule for everyone else (see S1-parity below) rather than obeying it itself. If the Developer finds themselves editing a `cleargate-planning/` path for this story, they have gone out of scope.

**Add a live↔canonical parity assertion to S1.** For each of the six templates and for `readiness-gates.md`, assert `.cleargate/<p>` and `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/<p>` are byte-identical (skip when `cleargate-planning/` is absent). Both are currently identical (verified). This is a two-line addition that machine-checks the sprint's most-cited cross-cutting rule — "a story that edits one tree is incomplete regardless of green tests" — for **all 18 items**, not just M0. Cheapest high-leverage assertion in the sprint.

> **TPV RULING 2026-08-27 (Architect §C.3.5, QA-Red commit `7778722`) — verdict PASS. Five rulings below; three are binding Developer instructions. No scenario is deleted, weakened, or renamed; nothing above this line changes.**

**T1 — Checker stays inline in the test file. Do not extract to `test/helpers/`.** The extraction is *legal* (`test/helpers/wrap-script.ts` is precedent, and `test/helpers/**` is not `src/**`) and is still refused, for three reasons. (a) The M0 §File surface table is exhaustive at two new files; a third is a surface deviation that buys nothing today. (b) **S4 is a source pin binding `resolveHeadingLine`/`templateBodyOf` to three exact lines of `readiness-predicates.ts`.** Moving that logic to `test/helpers/` while S4 stays in the test file puts the pin and the pinned code in different files — the drift shape this story exists to close. (c) There is no second consumer: 054-02 / 054-06 / CR-111 / EPIC-052 edit **data**, not the checker. **Extraction trigger (future, not now):** the first time a second test file needs `checkPinning`, extract to `cleargate-cli/test/helpers/gate-section-index-checker.ts` **and move S4 with it**.

**T2 — Correction to R3/R4: a heading-shifting story edits TWO places, not one.** R3 and R4 say the downstream obligation is "update the fixture in the same commit". That is incomplete. `TEMPLATE_FOR` and `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE` live in the test file (lines 98–111), not the fixture. Concretely: **STORY-054-01 / -02 must add `spike: 'spike.md'` to `TEMPLATE_FOR` in `gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts`** as well as adding the spike rows to `expected-headings.ts`. Without the `TEMPLATE_FOR` row, `canonicalTemplateReader` returns `null`, the checker emits `no template found for work_item_type "spike"`, and S1b fails with a message that reads like a missing template rather than a missing map row. Useful property, verified: `S1c` derives its parity list from `Object.values(TEMPLATE_FOR)`, so adding the row automatically extends the two-tree byte-parity check to `spike.md`. **M1/M2 plans must restate T2 verbatim.**

**T3 — S3 is insufficient as submitted and is strengthened, not kicked back.** The plan's S3 requires the checker name **every** shifted criterion, "not just the first". QA-Red asserts that structurally via `findings.length === 1` (`:513-517`) — which a first-hit-only checker satisfies equally, so the multiplicity claim is **unproven**. QA-Red's report is right that the plan's insertion point cannot shift two criteria; the resolution is a **second** insertion point, not a changed one. S3 also asserts only on `f.key`, while the plan makes message-text assertion normative for S2 **and S3**. Both are closed by the Developer instruction set below (D3, D4). Empirically verified against the canonical tree, so the expected values are not estimates.

**T4 — S6's narrowness is ACCEPTED; do not widen it.** The general "a future gated type with no template" case is already covered — by `checkPinning`'s `body == null` branch (`:252-259`), which emits `<key>: no template found for work_item_type "<type>" — either add one to TEMPLATE_FOR or add "<key>" to KNOWN_UNPINNABLE`, combined with S1b's `findings.length === 0`. That fires for **any** future type carrying a `section(N)` criterion, with exact remediation text. S6 is a size-assertion on the *known* set and is correctly scoped to it. Enumerating `WorkItemType` registrations would test a different axis (types with no `section(N)` criteria at all), which this story does not gate. No change.

**T5 — Fixture rows are FULL heading lines, including parentheticals. One row is a trap.** `resolveHeadingLine` returns `part.split('\n',1)[0].trim()` (`:224`) — the entire heading line. Verified by execution against the canonical tree: **`epic.affected-files-declared` resolves to `## 4. Technical Grounding (The "Shadow Spec")`**, not `## 4. Technical Grounding`. The M0 §Test shape prose and R11 both abbreviate that heading; the fixture must not. It is the only one of the twelve carrying a parenthetical, and it carries embedded double quotes — use single-quoted TS strings. All twelve pinnable criteria resolve to a heading their id names against the post-BUG-042 tree (verified), so S1b is expected green on first authoring.

## Cross-story risks

**R1 — The epic correction is right for the template and wrong for a third of the real corpus.** Verified by replaying the corrected indices over all 14 pending-sync epics: `section(8)` resolves to `## 4. Technical Grounding` in only **6** (EPIC-052/053/054/055/056/057 — the recently-authored ones). In the rest it resolves to `## 5. Acceptance Criteria` (EPIC-030/044/046/047/048/050), to `## ClearGate Ambiguity Gate` (EPIC-012), and in **EPIC-031 the section does not exist at all** → `section 8 not found (body has 7 sections)`, a hard fail on reopen. Root cause: `## Existing Surfaces` / `## Prior work` / `## Why not simpler?` were added to `epic.md` over time, so older epics have fewer headings. This is inherent to positional indexing and is **accepted** under BUG-042's recorded "lazy re-check on reopen" decision. Consequences for this sprint: **EPIC-054 passes at `section(8)` (19 declared items) — verified**, so nothing in SPRINT-39 breaks. Do not soften the criterion; do not bulk-fix the archive; **do** record the residue in the sprint report. (The same replay over all 25 pending-sync CRs shows `blast-radius-populated`→`section(3)` resolves correctly in **25/25** and never drops below 1 item; `sandbox-paths-declared`→`section(6)` resolves correctly in **23/25**, missing only CR-083 and CR-085, which predate `## Prior work` and land on `## 4. Verification Protocol` — both still count ≥4, so no in-flight CR fails.)

**R2 — Correcting `blast-radius-populated` un-gates `## 1. The Context Override`.** It was accidentally gated by the off-by-one. Post-fix, CR §1 is checked by nothing. Accepted: §1 is narrative and every CR in the corpus populates it (min 6 declared items observed). Flagging so nobody reports it later as a new fail-open. **Symmetrically, `## Existing Surfaces` loses its accidental epic gate — but is still covered by `reuse-audit-recorded` and `existing-surfaces-verified`, so nothing is lost there.**

**R3 — STORY-054-06 and CR-111 will both break the pinning test on purpose, and each must ship a fixture edit in the same commit.** 054-06 inserts `## Task Breakdown` into `story.md`, `CR.md`, `Bug.md`; CR-111 inserts a test-layers table (and, if it lands as a `## ` heading, shifts again). A Developer working only on 054-06 will see an unrelated-looking failure in `test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` and may be tempted to "fix" it by editing the fixture to whatever the tree now produces — **which silently re-breaks the gate.** The correct response is: decide the heading's position, recompute which criteria shift, correct those `section(N)` values in `readiness-gates.md` **and** update the fixture to the same intent. The Architect must restate this in the M2 plan; the vocabulary paragraph BUG-042 adds is the in-file reminder.

**R4 — STORY-054-02 adds spike gate blocks with new `section(N)` criteria and will trip S5.** By design: S5 refuses any criterion without a fixture row. 054-02's Developer must add rows for the spike criteria against `spike.md` (created by 054-01, M1-wave1). **Ordering consequence: 054-01 must merge before 054-02**, which the sprint plan already has (wave3 → wave4). If 054-01 slips, 054-02's spike criteria have no template to resolve against and must be added to `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE` with a dated note, not left silent.

**R5 — `## 3. Execution Sandbox` now has two independent locators with two different grammars.** `collision_surface.sh:87` (merged on `sprint/S-39` as `4b171393`, BUG-049) locates it by heading **text**: `/^## [0-9]+\.[[:space:]]*Execution Sandbox/`. The gate locates it by **position**: `section(6)`. They agree today only by coincidence. If STORY-054-06 or CR-111 inserts a heading, the positional locator drifts and the text locator does not — the exact divergence class BUG-041 was filed to eliminate. Not in M0's scope to unify. **Surfaced for the sprint report** as a candidate follow-on CR; also relevant to BUG-046's rewrite of the same script.

**R6 — Code fences can hijack the split.** FLASHCARD 2026-07-19 `#test-harness #readiness-gates`: `body.split(/^(?=## )/m)` ignores fenced blocks, so a fenced `## Example` in a work item's prose creates a phantom section and shifts everything below it. **Verified: no shipped template contains a fenced `## ` line today** (scanned all 9 in `.cleargate/templates/`). But authored items can, and STORY-054-06's Task Breakdown section is likely to carry a fenced example. Two obligations: (a) 054-06 must not put a `## `-leading line inside a fence in the template; (b) the pinning test operates on templates, so it will not catch an authored-item hijack — **this remains an open exposure and is not closed by M0.**

**R7 — Execution route: STORY-054-05 cannot run in a worktree.** `cleargate-cli/` is its own git repo and is gitignored in the outer repo (0 tracked files), so it does **not** materialize in `.worktrees/STORY-054-05/`. Per the established practice, 054-05 executes on a branch in the `cleargate-cli` checkout itself (currently on `main`, clean apart from an untracked `cleargate-0.23.1.tgz`), while its *reads* target the meta-repo tree at `../`. BUG-042, by contrast, touches only tracked outer-repo paths and is fully worktree-safe. **Do not cut a worktree for 054-05 expecting `cleargate-cli/` to be inside it.** This is precisely the fail-open BUG-046 (M4) exists to detect — M0 hits it before that fix lands, so it is handled here by plan rather than by enforcement.

**R8 — The pinning test reads across a repo boundary, so its own green depends on the meta-repo being present.** In `cleargate-cli`'s standalone CI (or an extracted tarball) `../cleargate-planning/` does not exist and the suite skips. That is the correct degradation (precedent: `dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts`), but it means **the gate is only enforced when the test runs from the side-by-side checkout.** Do not let the skip become the normal case: add the "if `cleargate-planning/` exists then `CANON` must exist" assertion described above, and note in the test header that a skipped run is not a passed run.

**R9 — Sprint-context §Test Stack and §Cross-Cutting Rules are stubs.** Every downstream agent reads them as overriding their built-in defaults, and right now they say `(rule 1)`. M0's operative values are supplied at the top of this plan. **Orchestrator action:** backfill `sprint-context.md` §Test Stack from the table above before dispatching M1, or every subsequent Developer re-derives it.


> **AMENDED 2026-08-27 — R10 added below. R1–R9 are unchanged and still hold.**

**R10 — A bare subsection label satisfies `≥1 declared-item`, so BOTH criteria BUG-042 corrects stay fail-open for the realistic authoring shape. Out of BUG-042's scope; filed as BUG-050.**

*Mechanism.* `countDeclaredItems` (`cleargate-cli/src/lib/readiness-predicates.ts:712-763`) has a definition-list-term branch matching `/^(\*{1,2}|_{1,2})?[A-Z][^|*\n]*(\*{1,2}|_{1,2})?:/` against each trimmed line. A bold label line standing alone with nothing beneath it matches and counts **1**.

*Reach — both sides, not just the epic.* Both corrected criteria threshold at `≥1` (`readiness-gates.md:99`, `:174`), and both target sections whose **shipped template body is label-shaped**:

| Template | Section | Labels shipped | Count when emptied but labeled | Count when label deleted too |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `epic.md` | `## 4. Technical Grounding` | `**Affected Files:**`, `**Data Changes:**` | **2** → passes `≥1` | 0 → fails |
| `CR.md` | `## 3. Execution Sandbox` | `**Modify:**` | **1** → passes `≥1` | 0 → fails |

QA-Red verified the epic side against its corrected-registry probe (`epic-s4d-label-only.md` → ✅ 12/12). **The Orchestrator verified the CR side, which QA-Red's evidence missed** — its S1 fixture deleted the `**Modify:**` label along with the paths, so it exercised the bare shape and read as a clean close. Bare `## 3. Execution Sandbox` → `❌ sandbox-paths-declared: section 6 has 0 declared-item (≥1 required)`; label-bearing → `✅ cr.ready-to-apply passed (8 criteria)`. Independently re-derived for this amendment by replaying the predicate over both shipped template bodies: 2 / 1 label-bearing, 0 / 0 bare.

*What this does not invalidate.* BUG-042's index correction is **still correct and still necessary**: it moves both criteria off the wrong section and onto the one their id names, and the **9/12 → 12/12** metric holds unchanged. The only claim retracted is that the fix *closes* the fail-open — it closes it for the label-deleted shape and leaves it open for the label-bearing one, which is the shape authors actually produce.

*Why it is not fixed here.* A threshold bump does not work: `epic.md` ships **two** labels, so `≥2` still passes on an empty section. The real fix is in `countDeclaredItems`, which — per sprint-context §Cross-Cutting Rules rule 3 — is inside `readiness-predicates.ts`, frozen for the whole sprint, and whose zero-diff is asserted by the DoD of **both** M0 items (BUG-042 S5, STORY-054-05 §File surface). Fixing it here would break two DoDs and a cross-cutting rule to close a defect BUG-042 was not filed against.

*Ownership.* Filed as **BUG-050** — confirmed free: the highest ID present across `.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/`, `.cleargate/delivery/archive/` and `.cleargate/wiki/bugs/` is `BUG-049`, and a repo-wide grep for `BUG-050` returns zero hits. Quarantined out of SPRINT-39 by Orchestrator decision.

*Obligations.* (a) QA-Verify follows the shape-explicit S1/S1b/S4/S4b pairs in BUG-042's §Test scenarios — a PASS on S1b/S4b is the expected residue, **not** a kick-back. (b) The Reporter records this in the sprint report as a known residue of BUG-042 with a pointer to BUG-050, alongside the R1 (EPIC-031) and R5 (two-locator) residues already queued there. **R11 — The vocabulary paragraph BUG-042 lands carries a worked example and a forward file reference. STORY-054-06 can falsify the first; a rename can strand the second. Neither is pinned by anything.**

*What landed.* `readiness-gates.md:36` (both trees) now asserts two concrete facts: (a) "`## 3. Execution Sandbox` in `CR.md` is `section(6)`, not `section(3)`", and (b) the pinning test lives at `cleargate-cli/test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts`. Both are **true today and unenforced** — STORY-054-05's checker enumerates `section(N)` *criteria* out of the YAML gate blocks, while the Predicate Vocabulary is prose the checker never parses.

*(a) is falsifiable by STORY-054-06.* Verified CR.md heading positions today: `1 ## 0.5 Open Questions · 2 ## 1. The Context Override · 3 ## 2. Blast Radius & Invalidation · 4 ## Existing Surfaces · 5 ## Prior work · 6 ## 3. Execution Sandbox · 7 ## 4. Verification Protocol · 8 ## Context Source · 9 ## ClearGate Ambiguity Gate`. STORY-054-06 Requirement 4 pins placement **only for `story.md`** ("directly after §3 The Implementation Guide"); CR.md and Bug.md placement is unspecified. Two outcomes:

- `## Task Breakdown` inserted **after** `## 3. Execution Sandbox` → Sandbox stays position 6. `sandbox-paths-declared` unchanged; the vocabulary example stays true.
- `## Task Breakdown` inserted **anywhere above it** → Sandbox becomes position 7. `sandbox-paths-declared` must move to `section(7)` **and the worked example at `:36` must be rewritten in the same commit.**

The pinning test catches the criterion. It does **not** catch the prose. **M2 obligation: STORY-054-06 fixes CR.md and Bug.md placement explicitly, and if Sandbox moves, edits `readiness-gates.md:36` alongside `:174` in both trees.** The same obligation binds CR-111 if its test-layer table lands as a `## ` heading. For reference, `story.md` today is `1 §1 The Spec · 2 §2 The Truth · 3 §3 The Implementation Guide · 4 §4 Quality Gates · 5 Existing Surfaces · 6 Prior work · 7 Why not simpler? · 8 Ambiguity Gate`, so the specified insertion moves `story.dod-declared` from `section(4)` to `section(5)` and leaves `implementation-files-declared` at `section(3)` — matching STORY-054-06 §3.2.

*(b) is a one-wave forward reference. ACCEPTED as-is — with a condition on STORY-054-05.* `cleargate-cli/test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` does not exist (verified: `test/docs/` holds five files, none of them this one; `test/fixtures/gate-section-index/` does not exist). STORY-054-05 creates it in wave2 — same milestone, immediately next wave, behind the M0→M1 barrier — so the dangling window is exactly one wave and closes before any other item reads the paragraph. **BUG-042 does not need to change.** The condition: STORY-054-05 §4.2 DoD says only "New `*.node.test.ts` under `cleargate-cli/test/`" and pins **no filename**, so a Developer may legally name it something else and strand the reference permanently. **The path is therefore contractual as of now.** Two normative additions to STORY-054-05, both cheap and neither widening its scope:

1. **The primary file MUST be exactly `cleargate-cli/test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts`**, and the fixture exactly `cleargate-cli/test/fixtures/gate-section-index/expected-headings.ts`. Not "a test under `test/`". A different name is a QA kick-back.
2. **Add a seventh scenario — S7, the doc reference resolves.** Read `readiness-gates.md` from the canonical tree, extract every backticked path matching `cleargate-cli/test/**/*.node.test.ts` out of the Predicate Vocabulary section, and assert each exists on disk. Fails loudly on a rename in either direction. This reads a file the story already reads, asserts no threshold, and touches no `src/` — it is inside the story's declared surface. Three lines.

**R12 — `backfill_hierarchy.mjs` can re-own any item authored during the remaining 12 waves. The exposure is batched rather than per-write, and CR-108 would make it the default for every scaffolded story.**

*Mechanism (verified, `.cleargate/scripts/backfill_hierarchy.mjs`).* `SPRINT_REGEX = /\bSPRINT-(\d+)\b/` (`:92`) is run over `first50Lines(body)` (`:88`, `:118-124`) as the last-resort fill for a missing `sprint_cleargate_id`. Any ordinary prose mention — a repro step naming a `sprint-runs/` path, a `**Surface:**` citation, an `<instructions>` note — silently re-owns the item. It mis-attributed 19 items today; corrected on `sprint/S-39` at `ebe9d087`. Full write-up: **BUG-048 §3.5** (filed, **not** in SPRINT-39's wave list — unscheduled).

*Correction to the standing premise — the trigger is NOT the PostToolUse hook.* `.claude/settings.json:46-55` wires `PostToolUse: Edit|Write` to `.claude/hooks/stamp-and-gate.sh`, which filters to `*.cleargate/delivery/*` (`:22`) and runs `stamp-tokens` (`:30`) → `gate check` (`:36`) → ingest. It **never invokes `backfill_hierarchy.mjs`** — a repo-wide grep for the script name returns only the script's own header lines. The backfill is a manually-run one-shot (`node .cleargate/scripts/backfill_hierarchy.mjs [--dry-run]`, `:19`) scanning `pending-sync/` and `archive/` flat and non-recursively (`:61-62`, `:223`). **So the hazard does not fire on every Write — it fires whenever the backfill is next run, and then sweeps the entire corpus at once.** Lower frequency, undiminished blast radius. `.cleargate/templates/` and `.cleargate/sprint-runs/` are outside the scan.

*Why the 19 corrections hold.* The write is guarded: `sprintToWrite = (existingSprint === null || existingSprint === undefined) ? sniffedSprint : null` (`:280`). A non-null value is never overwritten. Confirmed by reading, not assumed.

*The live trap — an explicit `null` is NOT protection.* That same guard treats `sprint_cleargate_id: null` as absent. An item scaffolded with `null` frontmatter boilerplate is fully exposed.

*Per-item assessment for the remaining waves:*

| Item | Wave | Exposed? | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| STORY-054-05 | wave2 | No | Creates only `cleargate-cli/**` files; nothing under `delivery/`. |
| STORY-054-01 | wave3 | **Guard note** | Creates `.cleargate/templates/spike.md`. The template itself is unscanned, but every charter scaffolded from it lands in `pending-sync/`. |
| STORY-054-02, -03, -04, -07; BUG-043/044/045/046; CR-105/106/107/110 | — | No | Edit code, agents, protocol or scripts. None writes a file into `.cleargate/delivery/**`. |
| STORY-054-06 | wave6 | **Guard note** | Edits `<instructions>` prose in `story.md`, `CR.md`, `Bug.md`. |
| CR-111 | wave13 | **Guard note** | Same three templates. |
| CR-108 | wave12 | **Systemic — strongest exposure** | Ships `cleargate new <type>`, writing `null`-scaffolded items into `pending-sync/`. |

*Why the template edits matter — already true, not hypothetical.* `.cleargate/templates/story.md` **already** contains `SPRINT-09` at body line 28 (the `complexity_label: L3` guidance, "observed in SPRINT-09 on STORY-013-02/03/04") and again at body line 45 — both inside the 50-line window and both inside the `<instructions>` block. `Sprint Plan Template.md` carries `SPRINT-16` at body line 44. The templates are therefore **already poisoned**; this is inert today only because the conversational agent strips `<instructions>` when authoring by hand.

*Why CR-108 converts that from inert to systemic.* `cleargate hotfix new` — the exact mechanism CR-108 generalizes to all nine types — writes the template **verbatim** with only `{ID}`/`{SLUG}`/`{ISO}` substitution (`cleargate-cli/src/commands/hotfix.ts:178-192`) and performs **no `<instructions>` stripping**. Generalized to `story`, a `cleargate new story "<slug>"` would emit `SPRINT-09` at body line 28 of a brand-new item whose `sprint_cleargate_id` is `null`; the next backfill run stamps it `SPRINT-09`. That is a phantom attribution on **every newly scaffolded story**, shipped as default behaviour.

*Guard notes to carry into the M1/M2/M4 plans. Do not fix the script — BUG-048 owns it and is unscheduled.*

- **STORY-054-01 (M1):** `spike.md` MUST NOT contain a literal `SPRINT-<digits>` token anywhere in its first 50 body lines. Write `SPRINT-NN` or `SPRINT-<n>` in guidance prose instead. This is free before the template ships and expensive afterwards.
- **STORY-054-06 (M2) and CR-111 (M4):** the same rule for any `<instructions>` prose added to `story.md`, `CR.md`, `Bug.md`. Neither item may introduce a **new** `SPRINT-<digits>` literal. Pre-existing occurrences are BUG-048's to clean — **do not expand scope to remove them.**
- **CR-108 (M4):** its DoD must state that a scaffolded item is created with a **resolved or omitted** `sprint_cleargate_id` — never a literal `null` sitting alongside un-stripped `<instructions>` prose. Either strip `<instructions>` on render, or omit the key. **Orchestrator flag:** this is arguably part of BUG-048's fix landing inside CR-108. If BUG-048 stays unscheduled, CR-108 ships the regression rather than inheriting it.

**R13 — QA-Verify's S1b fixture is untracked and dies with the worktree.** `.worktrees/BUG-042/.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/BUG-042-qa-red-fixtures/post-fix-probe/cr-s1b-label-only-sandbox.md` is the **only** artifact demonstrating the CR-side label-bearing residue (`**Modify:**` present, zero paths → `✅ cr.ready-to-apply passed (8 criteria)`). `git status` in the worktree shows it `??`, and the tracked fixture set on `sprint/S-39` has eight files and does not include it. It is BUG-050's primary CR-side evidence, and BUG-050 is quarantined to a later sprint. **Orchestrator: copy it into the main checkout's `BUG-042-qa-red-fixtures/post-fix-probe/` before teardown.** Not a merge blocker — the fix does not depend on it.

> **POST-FLIGHT AMENDMENT 2026-08-27 (Architect §C.6, STORY-054-05 at `c79f615`) — verdict PASS, cleared for merge. R14/R15 added; T2 corrected. R1–R13 are unchanged and still hold. Nothing above this line changes STORY-054-05's scope, commit, file surface, or merge position.** Full reasoning: `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/STORY-054-05-arch-postflight.md`.

**T2 — CORRECTED. Adding a gated template is a FOUR-site edit, not two.** T2 as written names `TEMPLATE_FOR` (verified present at `gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:111-118`, and its predicted `no template found for work_item_type "spike"` text is exactly what `checkPinning:265-272` emits) plus the fixture rows. It stops one site short: **S1a's hardcoded totals** at `:432` (`assert.strictEqual(criteria.length, 14)`) and `:434` (`pinnable.length, 12`), and **S6's hardcoded `12`** at `:644` (plus `:634`'s `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE.size, 2` if the un-pinnable set changes), must be bumped in the same commit. The totals are deliberate tripwires — S6's "silence is not a pass" — but a Developer who edits only `TEMPLATE_FOR` + fixture lands a red S1a/S6 and may wrongly conclude the fixture is wrong. **M1/M2 plans must restate T2 with all four sites.** Sites 3–4 can only be bumped to the correct number once the registry blocks exist, so **all four belong in STORY-054-02's commit** — with site 1 optionally pre-landed by STORY-054-01 (see R14).

**R14 — STORY-054-01 should add `spike: 'spike.md'` to `TEMPLATE_FOR` even though it ships no gate blocks.** 054-01 §1.3 puts gate blocks out of scope, so between 054-01 and 054-02 the registry never mentions `spike`, the enumeration stays at 14, and the test stays 14/14 green — correct. But `S1c` (`:443-460`) derives its live↔canonical byte-parity list from `Object.values(TEMPLATE_FOR)` (`:446`), so **`spike.md`'s Cross-Cutting Rule 1 two-tree parity is not machine-checked at all** until the map row exists. One line, cannot fail (S1c `continue`s on missing paths, `:451`), buys parity coverage a full wave early. STORY-054-02 MUST add it if 054-01 did not.

**R15 — The fixture is INDEX-FREE, so Cross-Cutting Rule 4's "AND update the fixture" is over-broad — and that over-instruction is itself a hazard.** `EXPECTED_HEADINGS` (`expected-headings.ts:35-56`) maps `<type>.<criterion-id>` → the full heading LINE; no `section(N)` integer appears in it, and `checkPinning:283-292` compares only heading text. So a story that inserts a `## ` heading **and correctly recomputes** the shifted index needs **zero** fixture edits — the criterion resolves to the same heading text as before. Concretely: STORY-054-06's specified `story.md` placement moves `story.dod-declared` `section(4)`→`section(5)` and requires **no fixture change**. A Developer who opens the fixture "because Rule 4 said to" and finds nothing to change is one keystroke from the tampering Rule 4 forbids. Rule 4's real content: **decide and fix the index first; touch the fixture only on a heading-text rename or a new pinnable criterion.** Coupled hazard, recorded because M2 and M4 both hit it: **nine of the twelve fixture rows are single-witness** — only `cr.sandbox-paths-declared`, `cr.blast-radius-populated` and `epic.affected-files-declared` have a fixture-independent second witness (S2a/S2b/S2c + S3a/S3b hardcode their heading text). For the other nine — including `story.dod-declared`, `story.implementation-files-declared` and `bug.repro-steps-deterministic`, **all three touched by STORY-054-06** — editing the row to match a drifted tree goes GREEN. **QA-Verify on any heading-inserting story must read the `readiness-gates.md` diff, not just the test result.** Accepted, not fixed: a second witness per row means duplicating the fixture, which is a pin of a pin.

**R11 — DISCHARGED.** Verified against the shipped test: no case reads the worked example. S7 (`:649-663`) reads the same paragraph via `extractPredicateVocabularySection` (`:360-369`) but its extractor regex (`:373`) pulls **only** backticked `cleargate-cli/test/**/*.node.test.ts` paths — the `section(6)` sentence is never parsed. R11(a) stands verbatim. **Disposition (M2 obligation, recorded in `sprint-context.md` §Mid-Sprint Amendments):** STORY-054-06 takes the **zero-shift placement** — `## Task Breakdown` immediately AFTER `## 3. Execution Sandbox` in `CR.md` (new position 7) and immediately AFTER `## 2. Reproduction Protocol` in `Bug.md` (new position 3) — which keeps `section(6)`/`section(2)` correct, keeps the worked example true, and leaves S2a/S3a/S3b's hardcoded strings untouched, making the prose obligation evaporate rather than be maintained. Fallback DoD (if placement above Sandbox is chosen) and the rejected S8 prose-pinning option are recorded in the sprint-context amendments and in `STORY-054-05-arch-postflight.md` §3. **Note for any prose edit at `readiness-gates.md:36`: preserve the backticked test path or S7 goes red (`:656-659`).**

**R12 — CORRECTED on reach; guard notes stand.** `hotfix.ts:179-182` confirmed verbatim (`{ID}`/`{SLUG}`/`{ISO}` only; `grep -n instructions` on that file returns one doc comment at `:131`). `story.md`'s two `SPRINT-09` literals confirmed at raw lines 28 and 45, both trees — **but both are inside the `<instructions>` block (lines 1–62), not in the body** (frontmatter is 64–105), and `awk 'NR>105' story.md | head -50 | grep 'SPRINT-[0-9]'` returns nothing. `backfill_hierarchy.mjs` `parseFm` (`:70`) bails on `!raw.startsWith('---')`, and all seven authoring templates open with `<instructions>` at line 1 — so a **verbatim**-rendered item is **skipped entirely** by the backfill, and a **stripped**-render item's body carries no `SPRINT-<digits>` in its first 50 lines today. **R12's "phantom attribution on every newly scaffolded story" does not hold on either path**; the exposure is latent-zero, not already-poisoned. The 19 real mis-attributions came from hand-authored items that already start with `---` — untouched, still real, close-gate obligation unchanged. **New coupling for CR-108:** stripping `<instructions>` on render is precisely what makes a scaffolded item backfill-visible for the first time, so CR-108 cannot ship the strip without settling `sprint_cleargate_id` in the same commit. All three guard notes (054-01, 054-06/CR-111, CR-108) are recorded in `sprint-context.md` §Mid-Sprint Amendments, phrased against the **post-frontmatter body** so nobody wastes a dispatch purging harmless `<instructions>` prose.

## How M1 advances the Sprint Goal

Sprint Goal clause 1: *"a pre-sprint SPIKE charter for bounded discovery."* M1 is that clause, delivered end to end. One sentence per story:

- **STORY-054-01** ships the document shape a spike can actually be written in — the one template in the corpus whose contract is *"the answer is unknown at draft time"* — without which "author a spike" has no artifact.
- **STORY-054-04** makes a concluded spike's findings addressable in the awareness layer, closing the orphaned-citation failure that is EPIC-054's stated reason for existing: an un-ingested spike survives only as long as someone hand-copies it.
- **STORY-054-02** turns the shape into a *type* — gate-checkable, transition-bearing, push-recognised — which is the difference between a markdown file and a first-class work item.
- **STORY-054-03** makes the type **reachable**: triage runs before any template is opened, so a spike that is not in the always-on `CLAUDE.md` classification list is unreachable regardless of how good the other three stories are.

M1 does not touch the Task Breakdown surface (M2) or the gate-index defect (M0, shipped). It **consumes** M0: STORY-054-02 appends to the registry M0 corrected and extends the enumerating test M0 built.

## Order

**Confirmed by the orchestrator; not re-planned here.**

```
wave 3   STORY-054-01  ‖  STORY-054-04
wave 4   STORY-054-02
wave 5   STORY-054-03
```

Cross-wave dependencies that make this ordering load-bearing (verified, not inherited):

- **054-01 → 054-02.** 054-02 adds `spike: 'spike.md'` to `TEMPLATE_FOR`; `canonicalTemplateReader` (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:309-315`) reads `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/templates/<file>` from the **main checkout**. Without `spike.md` merged to `sprint/S-39` first, S1b emits four `no template found for work_item_type "spike"` findings. Hard dependency.
- **054-01 → 054-02** again, on content: 054-02's four `section(N)` values are positions in the file 054-01 authors. Section indices cannot be computed before the template exists.
- **054-02 → 054-03.** 054-03's `CLAUDE.md` triage-list entry and its SKILL.md "spikes run pre-sprint" clause both point at a type that must already exist; and 054-03 edits `.cleargate/templates/story.md`, which 054-02 does not touch — so the ordering here is doctrinal coherence, not file conflict.
- **054-04 ⟂ everything else.** Its file surface intersects no other M1 story (see §Wave-3 disjointness re-check). It is ordered into wave 3 for wall-clock, not dependency.

# Per-story blueprint

## STORY-054-01 — Spike charter template

**Goal linkage.** Ships the document shape a spike can be written in; without it there is no artifact for 054-02 to type, 054-04 to ingest, or 054-03 to route to.

**Cross-story coupling.** Producer for **054-02** (which computes `section(N)` positions against the file this story writes, and adds the `TEMPLATE_FOR` row), for **054-04** (whose Scenario 1 ingests an instance of this template), and for **054-03** (whose `story.md:27` reroute names the artifact this story creates). Consumes nothing.

### Execution route

| | |
|---|---|
| Repos touched | **outer only** |
| Commits | **1** |
| Root | `.worktrees/STORY-054-01/`, branch `story/STORY-054-01` off `sprint/S-39` |
| cli branch | **none** |

Both surfaces are **tracked** outer-repo paths (`git ls-files` confirms `.cleargate/templates/` and `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/templates/` are fully tracked), so they materialize inside a linked worktree. This story is worktree-safe and is the only M1 story that is.

**R14 is explicitly NOT taken here.** M0's post-flight recommends 054-01 pre-land `spike: 'spike.md'` in `TEMPLATE_FOR` to buy S1c two-tree parity a wave early. **Refused, for two verified reasons.** (a) That is a `cleargate-cli` commit, and 054-04 holds the cleargate-cli checkout for the whole of wave 3 — one checkout, one branch; taking it serialises wave 3 for a benefit that is not even realised. (b) The benefit is not realised from a worktree: `META_ROOT = path.resolve(CLI_ROOT, '..')` (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:97-99`) always resolves to the **main** checkout, so with `spike.md` living in `.worktrees/STORY-054-01/` the S1c loop hits `!fs.existsSync(livePath)` and `continue`s (`:451`) — zero coverage, zero failure, zero signal. **The `TEMPLATE_FOR` row is 054-02's, per M0 R14's own fallback clause ("STORY-054-02 MUST add it if 054-01 did not").** 054-01's two-tree parity is verified by its own §2.2 `diff`, which is sufficient for a story that ships two files.

**Failure mode: none available.** One repo, one commit, two new files. There is no half-landed state.

### File surface (exhaustive; reconciled against §3.1 — **§3.1 is correct as written**)

| Tree | Path | Change |
|---|---|---|
| live | `.cleargate/templates/spike.md` | **new** |
| canonical | `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/templates/spike.md` | **new**, byte-identical |
| — | `cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json` | **do not stage.** Whitelisted (`.cleargate/scripts/surface-whitelist.txt:12`); regenerated by `build-manifest.ts` (pattern `.cleargate/templates/*` → tier `template`, `merge-3way`, automatic — no hand edit). DevOps refreshes post-merge; precedent `a582c890`. |
| — | `cleargate-cli/**` | **forbidden for this story** |
| — | `cleargate-cli/templates/cleargate-planning/**` | generated + gitignored (Cross-Cutting Rule 2) |

### Schema — the exact heading layout (NORMATIVE; binds 054-02)

`section(N)` is **the Nth `## ` heading in document order** — verified: `evalSection` (`readiness-predicates.ts:640-648`) splits on `/^(?=## )/m`, and with a preamble (`# {ID}: {SLUG}` H1) `arrayIndex = N`, so `rawParts[N]` is the Nth heading. The template body **must** carry exactly these six `## ` headings, in this order, with these exact strings:

| Position | Heading line (exact, including punctuation) |
|---|---|
| 1 | `## 1. The Question` |
| 2 | `## 2. Timebox & Kill Criteria` |
| 3 | `## 3. Decision Unblocked` |
| 4 | `## 4. Decision Log` |
| 5 | `## 5. Outcome & Spawned Items` |
| 6 | `## ClearGate Ambiguity Gate (🟢 / 🟡 / 🔴)` |

**No other `## ` heading.** Specifically: **no `## 0.5 Open Questions`** (a spike *is* the open question; adding it shifts every index and is self-parody), **no `## Existing Surfaces`**, **no `## Why not simpler?`**, **no `## Context Source`**. §1.2 Requirement 1 is an exhaustive ordered list; Requirement 4 adds the Ambiguity Gate. Every additional heading costs a position that 054-02 must then recompute. `## Prior work` is an **open decision** — see §Open decisions #1; the default is **omit**.

**T5 applies (M0 post-flight):** `resolveHeadingLine` returns the **full heading line**, parentheticals included. Heading 6 carries a parenthetical and embedded emoji — but it is not a gated position, so no fixture row. Headings 1, 2, 4, 5 **are** gated by 054-02 and must carry **no parenthetical**, because the fixture rows in §STORY-054-02 below pin them verbatim.

**Deviation from this table is a QA kick-back on 054-01, not a re-index by 054-02.** If a heading must change, it changes here, before 054-02 is dispatched.

### Template invariants (NORMATIVE — each is a QA kick-back criterion)

These make 054-02's gate criteria non-vacuous and keep the template out of three known traps.

1. **Zero `- ` bullet lines inside §1, §2, §4, §5 — including inside code fences.** `evalSection`'s `listed-item` branch matches `/^\s*- /gm` against the raw section text and **does not strip fences** (verified `readiness-predicates.ts:668`). Guidance in those four sections is prose, bold sub-labels, or a table — never a bullet. This is the reason 054-02's presence criteria have teeth: the shipped template scores **0**, so a criterion passing means an author actually wrote something. (Contrast BUG-050: a shipped `**Modify:**` label scores 1 for `declared-item` and makes the criterion vacuous. That trap is avoided here by construction, not by predicate change.)
2. **§4 Decision Log ships as a markdown table: header row + `|---|` separator + ZERO data rows.** `countDeclaredItems` counts table data rows only *after* a separator, and a `|`-leading line cannot match the definition-list-term regex (`/^(\*{1,2}|_{1,2})?[A-Z][^|*\n]*(\*{1,2}|_{1,2})?:/` excludes `|`). So the shipped §4 scores `declared-item` = **0**. This is also the cleanest expression of Requirement 3's "append-only, one entry per discovery round".
3. **The literal string `TBD` appears nowhere in the file.** 054-02 adds `no-tbds` (`body does not contain marker 'TBD'`) to both spike gate blocks. A template shipping `TBD` fails its own gate on first render.
4. **No literal `SPRINT-<digits>` token anywhere in the post-frontmatter body.** Write `SPRINT-NN` or `SPRINT-<n>`. Recorded as a `sprint-context.md` Mid-Sprint Amendment guard note (M0 R12). Free now, expensive after the template ships. Occurrences inside `<instructions>` are harmless under both render paths (M0 R12 correction) — **do not spend effort purging those**; just do not create body-level ones.
5. **No line consisting of exactly `---` inside the `<instructions>` block.** `templateBodyOf` (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:219-224`) locates the frontmatter with `/^---[ \t]*\r?\n[\s\S]*?\r?\n---[ \t]*\r?\n/m` — the **first** `---`-delimited pair in the raw file. A horizontal rule inside `<instructions>` would be mistaken for the frontmatter opener and every downstream index would be computed against garbage. A `---` **after** the frontmatter is fine (`hotfix.md:100` has one).
6. **No `## `-leading line inside a code fence anywhere in the file.** FLASHCARD 2026-07-19 `#test-harness #readiness-gates`: `body.split(/^(?=## )/m)` ignores fences, so a fenced `## Example` creates a phantom section and shifts everything below it. Verified: none of the nine shipped templates does this today. `spike.md` must not be the first.
7. **`<instructions>` closes with `Do NOT output these instructions.`** — house pattern, `hotfix.md:27`.

### Frontmatter (NORMATIVE)

Required keys, per §1.2 Requirement 2: `spike_id`, `timebox`, `kill_criteria`, `spawned_items: []`, `concluded_at`, plus the sync-attribution block copied **verbatim** from `initiative.md` — i.e. `created_at`, `updated_at`, `created_at_version`, `updated_at_version`, `server_pushed_at_version`, `draft_tokens{input,output,cache_read,cache_creation,model,sessions}`, `cached_gate_result{pass,failing_criteria,last_gate_check}`, `pushed_by`, `pushed_at`, `last_pulled_by`, `last_pulled_at`, `last_remote_update`, `source`, `last_synced_status`, `last_synced_body_sha` (`initiative.md` frontmatter, verified). Add `status`, `approved`, `ambiguity`, `context_source`, `parent_cleargate_id`, `area`.

**Omit `sprint_cleargate_id` entirely.** A spike is pre-sprint by doctrine (054-03 Requirement 3) so a sprint owner is semantically wrong; and the M0 R12 guard note is explicit that a scaffolded item's `sprint_cleargate_id` must be **resolved-or-omitted, never a literal `null`** — `backfill_hierarchy.mjs:280`'s write guard treats `null` as absent, so shipping `null` gives the false impression of protection. Omission plus invariant 4 is the correct pair. **Omit `carry_over` too** (same reason).

### Reuse (no duplication)

| What | Where | How |
|---|---|---|
| Sync-attribution frontmatter block | `.cleargate/templates/initiative.md` frontmatter | Copy **verbatim** so `stamp-tokens` and the conflict detector behave identically. |
| Body proportions + `<instructions>` shape + POST-WRITE BRIEF block | `.cleargate/templates/hotfix.md:1-28` | Copy the shape; a charter is hotfix-length, not epic-length. Adapt the BRIEF's Ambiguity line to report **question-sharpness**, per §1.4's accepted decision. |
| `spawned_items: []` + archive-on-conclusion lifecycle | `.cleargate/templates/initiative.md` | Same lifecycle verbatim (Requirement 5). |
| Ambiguity Gate block shape | `.cleargate/templates/hotfix.md:102-111` | Same shape; the **criteria text is inverted** per Requirement 4 — 🟢 means the question is sharp, the timebox is set and the kill criteria are falsifiable. State the inversion **twice**: once in `<instructions>` and once in the rendered gate block body, because instructions are stripped from instances (FLASHCARD 2026-08-25 `#agents #scaffold #danger`). |

### Test scenarios (from §2.1 Gherkin — all four must be covered)

1. **Scenario 1 — authoring before the answer is known.** Structural: the six headings exist in the order pinned above; no section requires acceptance Gherkin or a files-to-touch list. Verified by reading the file.
2. **Scenario 2 — the inversion survives instruction-stripping.** Strip lines 1..`</instructions>` and assert the remaining body still explains that 🟢 means a sharp question, not a known answer. **This is the scenario that fails if the inversion is stated only in `<instructions>`.**
3. **Scenario 3 — conclusion hands off.** §5 names `spawned_items` and instructs the author to move the file to `.cleargate/delivery/archive/`.
4. **Scenario 4 — canonical/live parity.** `diff .cleargate/templates/spike.md cleargate-planning/.cleargate/templates/spike.md` → empty.

**Test shape.** §4.1 asks for 0 unit tests and 1 structural assertion. Take that literally: **no new `.node.test.ts` file for this story.** The structural assertion is §2.2's `diff` plus the heading-order read, executed as a QA-Verify manual step and recorded in the QA report. Rationale: the only machine home for a template-structure assertion in this repo is `gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts`, and touching that file is a `cleargate-cli` commit — refused above. Adding a fresh cli test file for a template that gains its gate one wave later duplicates what 054-02's S1a/S1b/S1c will assert for free.

### QA kick-back criteria (specific)

- Any `## ` heading present, absent, reordered, or textually different from the six-row table → **kick back**.
- Any `- ` line inside §1, §2, §4 or §5 (fenced or not) → **kick back** (invariant 1; it pre-vacates 054-02's gate).
- §4 not a zero-data-row table → **kick back** (invariant 2).
- Literal `TBD` anywhere → **kick back** (invariant 3).
- Literal `SPRINT-<digits>` in the post-frontmatter body → **kick back** (invariant 4). `<instructions>` occurrences are **not** a kick-back.
- A bare `---` line inside `<instructions>` → **kick back** (invariant 5).
- A fenced `## ` line anywhere → **kick back** (invariant 6).
- `sprint_cleargate_id: null` present in frontmatter → **kick back**.
- The ambiguity inversion stated only inside `<instructions>` → **kick back** (Scenario 2).
- `diff` between trees non-empty → **kick back** (Cross-Cutting Rule 1).
- Any staged file under `cleargate-cli/` → **kick back** (out of surface).

### Gotchas surfaced from code inspection

- `.cleargate/templates/**` is **not** matched by the PostToolUse stamp/gate hook — `stamp-and-gate.sh:22` filters on `*.cleargate/delivery/*`. Writing `spike.md` triggers no stamp, no `gate check`, no ingest. Expect silence; it is correct.
- `cleargate hotfix new` writes its template **verbatim**, substituting only `{ID}`/`{SLUG}`/`{ISO}` (`cleargate-cli/src/commands/hotfix.ts:179-182` — re-verified for this plan; `grep -n instructions src/commands/hotfix.ts` returns one doc comment at `:131`, zero handling). CR-108 generalises that allocator. **Use `{ID}`, `{SLUG}`, `{ISO}` and no other placeholder token** in `spike.md`, so the generalised allocator can render it unchanged.
- `MANIFEST.json` tiering is automatic: `matchRule('.cleargate/templates/spike.md', '.cleargate/templates/*')` → true → tier `template`, `overwrite_policy: merge-3way`, matching all nine existing templates (verified against the current manifest). Nothing to hand-write.
- `test/docs/pin-placeholder-coverage.node.test.ts` walks `cleargate-planning/` for `__CLEARGATE_VERSION__`. `spike.md` must not contain that token (it has no reason to).

Surface-disjoint from all three other M1 stories.

### Execution route — CROSS-REPO

| | |
|---|---|
| Repos touched | **outer + cleargate-cli** |
| Commits | **2** (one per repo) |
| Outer root | `.worktrees/STORY-054-04/`, branch `story/STORY-054-04` off `sprint/S-39` |
| cli root | `/Users/ssuladze/Documents/Dev/ClearGate/cleargate-cli`, branch `story/STORY-054-04` off cli `main` (single checkout, held for all of wave 3) |
| Pre-merge suite run | `CLEARGATE_META_ROOT=<abs-path-to-worktree> npm --prefix cleargate-cli test` |

**A worktree is usable here ONLY because the new parity test is authored to honour `CLEARGATE_META_ROOT`. That is mandatory, not optional.** Every `cleargate-cli` test that reads meta-repo files derives its root as `path.resolve(CLI_ROOT, '..')` (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:97-99`; same in `dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts:39-41`, `pin-placeholder-coverage.node.test.ts:30`). `CLI_ROOT` is the physical `cleargate-cli/` directory, which lives inside the **main** checkout — so by default **a cli test can never see a file that exists only inside `.worktrees/*`**, and P6 would read main's unedited `config.yml` and go red for a reason invisible from inside the worktree.

The repo already has the sanctioned escape, in four landed test files (`test/scaffold/enforcement-doc-coherence.node.test.ts:45-47`, `test/scaffold/ratchet-retired.node.test.ts`, `test/commands/no-exec-mode-vocab.node.test.ts:41-43`, `test/scripts/close-sprint-assume-ack-guard.node.test.ts:53-55`) and an explicit prior ruling (FLASHCARD 2026-07-18 `#test-harness #cross-repo`: *"CLEARGATE_META_ROOT override is the correct pre-merge path, not a defect"*). **Copy the idiom verbatim:**

```ts
const REPO_ROOT = process.env.CLEARGATE_META_ROOT
  ? path.resolve(process.env.CLEARGATE_META_ROOT)
  : path.resolve(__dirname, '..', '..', '..');
```

Pre-merge, run the suite with `CLEARGATE_META_ROOT` pointed at the worktree. Post-merge — main checkout on `sprint/S-39` carrying commit A — the same test passes with **no env var**, which is the state DevOps must verify. **Omitting the override and running from the main checkout is also acceptable; omitting the override and running from a worktree is not.** The ten source-site extractors are unaffected either way: they read `cleargate-cli/src/**` and `cleargate-cli/templates/**`, which live in the cli checkout, not the outer tree.

### Commit plan — exact

**Commit A — outer repo, `.worktrees/STORY-054-04/`, branch `story/STORY-054-04`. LANDS FIRST.**

Contents, exhaustively:
- `.cleargate/config.yml` — add `    - spikes` to `wiki.ingest_buckets` (`:12-18`)
- `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.yml` — same one-line addition (`:12-18`)

Nothing else. Message: `feat(EPIC-054): STORY-054-04 add spikes to wiki.ingest_buckets (both trees)`.

**Commit B — `cleargate-cli` repo, branch `story/STORY-054-04`. LANDS SECOND.**

Contents, exhaustively: sites 1–11 plus the new test file (see §File surface below). Message must record the **thirteen** independent lists per §1.2 Requirement 5 and cite `BUG-051` as the deferred-unification home.

**Why A before B — the inert-intermediate argument (verified, not assumed).**

- **A alone is inert.** `isBucketIngestable(bucket, ingestBuckets)` (`src/lib/wiki-config.ts:58-61`) is a membership filter consulted *after* a bucket has already been derived. With `spikes` in the allowlist but absent from `PREFIX_MAP`, no code path can ever produce the bucket `spikes`, so the extra allowlist entry is unreachable. No test reads `ingest_buckets` for `spikes` until commit B's test exists. `npm --prefix cleargate-cli test` stays **green** after A alone.
- **B alone is NOT inert.** The parity test's P6 asserts `spikes ∈ ingest_buckets` in both trees. With B landed and A missing, the cli suite is **red on `cleargate-cli` `main` for every other developer**.

There is exactly one ordering with a green intermediate. Take it.

**Failure handling.**

| Situation | Action |
|---|---|
| A lands, B fails or is abandoned | Tree is green and inert. Either land B, or `git revert <A>` on `story/STORY-054-04`. **Never `git reset --hard`, never force-push.** Report BLOCKED with the revert SHA. |
| B lands, A missing (should be impossible given the order) | cli suite is red. Land A immediately; if A cannot land, `git revert <B>` in the cli repo. |
| Merge | **Binding on DevOps: verify both commits exist before merging either.** Merge A → `sprint/S-39`, then B → cli `main`, then re-run `npm --prefix cleargate-cli test` and `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run typecheck`. A merged-A-only sprint branch is acceptable and green; a merged-B-only cli `main` is not. |

**No hooks fire on the cli commit.** Verified: `cleargate-cli/.git/hooks/` contains only `.sample` files — **zero installed hooks.** The pre-commit surface gate, `check:no-vitest`, `check:no-inline-id-regex` and the red-test immutability gate all live on the **outer** repo's `pre-commit` symlink and run only for outer commits. **The Developer must therefore run `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run typecheck && npm --prefix cleargate-cli test` by hand before commit B.** Nothing will do it for them. (See R17.)
