A spike is neither a sprint nor an epic. This is the same value `initiatives`, `crs`, `bugs` and `topics` carry.

### Test shape — the parity test (NORMATIVE)

**File:** `cleargate-cli/test/wiki/bucket-registry-parity.node.test.ts` (default-runner glob `test/**/*.node.test.ts`, not `.integration.`, not under `test/fixtures/`).

**Scope decision: UNIVERSAL parity, with an explicit, size-asserted exception table.** Not spike-only. Reasoning, and what breaks under each alternative:

- **Spike-only** (`assert spikes ∈ every list`) is **vacuous in the way that matters**. It goes green today while `initiatives` is missing from four of the eleven lists (verified: `load-wiki.BUCKET_DIRS`, `product-state.buckets`, `product-state.total_*`, the mustache template) and `stories` is missing from the mustache template. It certifies "the bucket-list class is pinned" when the class is demonstrably broken, and it would go green against a twelfth list added tomorrow that nobody wired. This is the same defect shape TPV found in 054-05's S3b, where `findings.length === 1` was satisfied equally by a first-hit-only checker: **an assertion whose passing set includes the broken world proves nothing.**
- **Naive universal** (`assert every bucket ∈ every list`, no exceptions) is **red on merge**. Computed exhaustively over 11 sites × 7 current delivery buckets: **exactly 5 pre-existing gaps**, listed below. Closing them means adding `initiatives` and `stories` to the product-state family — which changes compiled wiki output, is filed as `BUG-051` (`approved: false`, not in this sprint), and is outside 054-04's declared surface. The Developer's only escapes would be to fix BUG-051 inside 054-04 (scope explosion) or to weaken the test to spike-only (loses the class). Neither is acceptable.
- **Universal + explicit size-asserted exception table** is green on merge, non-vacuous, and converts BUG-051's drift from *silent* to *counted*. It is the `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE` pattern STORY-054-05 established and FLASHCARD 2026-08-26 `#gate #test-harness` blesses: *"the un-pinnable set must be explicit and size-asserted, or silence reads as coverage."* **Take it.**

**Reference set — derived, not hand-written.** `DELIVERY_BUCKETS` is extracted from `src/wiki/derive-bucket.ts` `PREFIX_MAP` (site 1), which is the authority on "what is a bucket compiled out of `.cleargate/delivery/**`". One authority, ten followers. Consequence: `spikes` enters the reference set the moment the HARD BLOCKER is fixed, and the matrix then demands it in the other ten sites automatically. `topics` and `unknown` are **excluded by construction** — `topics` is written by `wiki query --persist`, never derived from a delivery filename, and `unknown` is a `BUCKET_SYNTHESIS_MAP` sentinel; neither appears in `PREFIX_MAP`.

**Extraction method: source TEXT, not imports.** This is the same fork `BUG-051 §5` names and `STORY-054-05` faced for `evalSection`. **Do not export the private constants.** Three reasons: (a) `BUCKET_DIRS` (`load-wiki.ts:13`), `BUCKET_ORDER` (`wiki-build.ts:54`), `PREFIX_MAP` (`derive-bucket.ts:9`) and `product-state.ts`'s function-local `buckets` are module-private, and exporting them is the first half of the unification §1.3 explicitly defers — a half-done unification is worse than none; (b) sites 9, 10 and 11 are not importable lists at all (`total_*` is a set of object literal keys; the mustache row is a markdown file); a text extractor is the only mechanism that covers all eleven uniformly; (c) precedent inside this sprint — `checkPinning` replicates `loadGateBlocks` by text and S4 pins `readiness-predicates.ts` by source text rather than importing it.

**The ten follower sites, each with its own named extractor:**

| # | Label | Path | Extract |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `page-schema.WikiPageType` | `src/wiki/page-schema.ts` | the `'…' \| '…'` union members on the `export type WikiPageType` line (compare **singular** forms) |
| 2 | `page-schema.BUCKET_LABELS` | `src/wiki/page-schema.ts` | object keys of the `BUCKET_LABELS` literal |
| 3 | `page-schema.ACTIVE_BUCKET_ORDER` | `src/wiki/page-schema.ts` | array members |
| 4 | `page-schema.ARCHIVE_BUCKET_ORDER` | `src/wiki/page-schema.ts` | array members |
| 5 | `wiki-build.BUCKET_ORDER` | `src/commands/wiki-build.ts` | array members |
| 6 | `wiki-ingest.BUCKET_SYNTHESIS_MAP` | `src/commands/wiki-ingest.ts` | object keys |
| 7 | `load-wiki.BUCKET_DIRS` | `src/wiki/load-wiki.ts` | array members |
| 8 | `product-state.buckets` | `src/wiki/synthesis/product-state.ts` | array members of the `const buckets = [...]` literal |
| 9 | `product-state.total_*` | `src/wiki/synthesis/product-state.ts` | the `<bucket>` in each `total_<bucket>:` key |
| 10 | `synthesis-template.mustache` | `templates/synthesis/product-state.md` | the `<bucket>` in each `{{total_<bucket>}}` token |

**Seven assertions.**

- **P1 — the reference set is live and includes `spikes`.** `DELIVERY_BUCKETS.length >= 8` and `DELIVERY_BUCKETS.includes('spikes')`. This is the HARD BLOCKER (site 1) expressed as an assertion: without the `PREFIX_MAP` row, `deriveBucket` throws and Scenario 1 cannot pass.
- **P2 — the matrix.** For every `(bucket, site)` in `DELIVERY_BUCKETS × SITES` not present in `KNOWN_BUCKET_GAPS`: the bucket appears in the site. **Collect every finding; never stop at the first** (the `checkPinning` contract). Message, one line per finding, exact shape:
  `bucket '<b>' missing from <site-label> (<path>) — add it, or record a KNOWN_BUCKET_GAPS row with a reason.`
- **P3 — the exception table is exact.** `assert.strictEqual(KNOWN_BUCKET_GAPS.size, 5)`, and every row carries a one-line reason citing `BUG-051`. The five rows, verified by exhaustive computation over the current tree:
  | bucket | site |
  |---|---|
  | `initiatives` | `load-wiki.BUCKET_DIRS` |
  | `initiatives` | `product-state.buckets` |
  | `initiatives` | `product-state.total_*` |
  | `initiatives` | `synthesis-template.mustache` |
  | `stories` | `synthesis-template.mustache` |
- **P4 — `spikes` has ZERO rows in `KNOWN_BUCKET_GAPS`.** This is 054-04's deliverable expressed as an assertion: the story cannot ship by declaring its own gaps known. Mirrors S6's closed escape hatch in 054-05.
- **P5 — extractor liveness.** Each of the ten extractors returned a **non-empty** list. A regex that stops matching after a rename or reformat must fail loudly rather than return `[]` and read as "no gaps found". **Empty is never a pass.**
- **P6 — the config allowlist, both trees.** `spikes ∈ wiki.ingest_buckets` in `<REPO_ROOT>/.cleargate/config.yml` **and** `<REPO_ROOT>/cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.yml`, where `REPO_ROOT` honours `CLEARGATE_META_ROOT` per the idiom above, each guarded by `fs.existsSync` (the R8 degradation pattern from `dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts:66-70`). **This is a membership assertion, not a byte-parity assertion** — the two files legitimately differ (R22).
- **P7 — acceptance (§2.1 Scenarios 1 and 2), tmpdir.** Build a throwaway project (`mkdtemp` precedent: `test/wiki/ingest.node.test.ts`) with its own `.cleargate/config.yml` carrying `spikes`, write `pending-sync/SPIKE-001_Probe.md`, run the ingest handler with `cwd` pointed at it, then assert (a) `.cleargate/wiki/spikes/SPIKE-001.md` exists, (b) `index.md` lists it, (c) after a `wiki build`, `product-state.md` carries a `| Spikes |` row with a count. `loadWikiConfig(cwd)` is cwd-parameterised (`wiki-config.ts`, `wiki-ingest.ts:155`), so P7 needs no repo-level state.

**§2.1 Scenario 3 ("a bucket list is missed → the test names the list that does not contain spikes") is P2's failure message, verified negatively.** Add one negative case: feed `checkBucketParity` a synthetic site list with `spikes` removed and assert the emitted message names that site by label. Without this, P2's naming claim is unproven — the same gap TPV closed in 054-05's S3.

### QA kick-back criteria (specific)

- Any of the eleven code sites unedited → **kick back** (P2 will already be red).
- `KNOWN_BUCKET_GAPS` size ≠ 5, or any row naming `spikes`, or any row lacking a reason → **kick back**.
- The parity test asserts `spikes` membership only (no matrix over `DELIVERY_BUCKETS`) → **kick back**: that is the vacuous shape this plan rejects by name.
- `DELIVERY_BUCKETS` hand-written as a literal rather than extracted from `PREFIX_MAP` → **kick back**: it becomes the fourteenth list.
- Any private constant given an `export` keyword → **kick back** (starts BUG-051's unification inside a story that defers it).
- P2 stops at the first finding (`findings[0]`, `.find(...)`, an early `assert` inside the loop) → **kick back**.
- Missing P5 → **kick back**: an extractor that silently returns `[]` turns the whole test vacuous.
- `REPO_ROOT` in the new test hardcoded to `path.resolve(__dirname,'..','..','..')` with no `CLEARGATE_META_ROOT` branch → **kick back** (R18): the story is then unverifiable from its own worktree.
- `.cleargate/config.yml` and `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.yml` reconciled beyond the one-line list addition → **kick back** (destroys live gate config).
- Commit B landed without commit A → **kick back and hold the merge**.
- Any edit to `contradict.ts`, `wiki-comments-render.ts`, `sync/work-items.ts`, `push.ts` → **kick back** (BUG-051's).
- Story files under `.cleargate/delivery/**` staged in either commit → **kick back**.

### Gotchas surfaced from code inspection

- `deriveBucket` matches with `id.startsWith(prefix)` where `id` is the filename stem **before the first `_`** (`derive-bucket.ts:58-70`). `SPIKE-001_Probe.md` → id `SPIKE-001` → matches. **Row placement inside `PREFIX_MAP` is order-insensitive here** because no other prefix is a prefix of `SPIKE-`. (This is *not* true of `work-item-type.ts`'s map — see R24, which binds 054-02, not this story.)
- `product-state.ts:48-53` is a **hand-written** `total_*` block that is *not* derived from the `buckets` array at `:36`. Editing `:36` alone gives you `active_spikes`/`shipped_spikes` and no `total_spikes`.
- `templates/synthesis/product-state.md:9-13` is a **hardcoded mustache row per bucket**. Without row 11, sites 9 and 10 render nothing at all and Scenario 2 fails with no error message.
- `BUCKET_SYNTHESIS_MAP` is the only one of the six code lists that is already `export`ed (`wiki-ingest.ts:565`). Do not read that as licence to export the other five.
- `lint-checks.ts:303` `ENFORCING_TYPES = new Set(['epic','story','cr','bug'])` is a gate-**severity** set, not a bucket list. `spike` must **not** be added — 054-02 ships the spike gate as `advisory`.
- `.cleargate/wiki/spikes/` need not be pre-created; the ingest path `mkdirSync`s with `recursive: true`.

## STORY-054-02 — Spike as a first-class type

**Goal linkage.** Turns the document shape into a *type* — gate-checkable, transition-bearing, push-recognised — which is the difference between a markdown file and a work item ClearGate can carry.

**Cross-story coupling.** Hard successor of **054-01** (needs `spike.md` in both trees of the main checkout to compute and pin indices) and of **M0/BUG-042 + STORY-054-05** (appends to the registry BUG-042 corrected and extends the enumerating test 054-05 built). Producer for **054-03** (the doctrine points at this type). Surface-disjoint from **054-04**.

| `cleargate-cli/src/commands/push.ts` (`:506-518` `getItemType`) | **Required by §1.1's user story; not by any §2.1 scenario** | Orchestrator scope call — see §Open decisions #2 |

  assert.strictEqual(keys.length, 8);

Adding `spike` makes it 9. This is a **deliberate tripwire of exactly the same class as S1a's `14`/`12`** — and it is not named anywhere in the story, the M0 plan, or `sprint-context.md`. A Developer who edits only `work-item-type.ts` lands a red test in a file they were not told they own and may conclude they registered the type wrong. **Bump the assertion to 9 and update the test title and the header comment at `:18` in the same commit.**

| # | Site | Edit |

Sites 3 and 4 can only be bumped to the correct number once the registry blocks exist, so **all four belong in this story's cli commit**. `TEMPLATE_FOR` was **not** pre-landed by 054-01 (see that story's §Execution route) — site 1 is unconditionally this story's.

**Finding 3 — `cleargate push <spike-charter>` hard-errors today.** `push.ts:506-518` `getItemType` carries a private `typeMap` of six frontmatter keys (`story_id`, `epic_id`, `proposal_id`, `sprint_id`, `cr_id`, `bug_id`) and returns `null` otherwise; `push.ts:253-257` then prints `Error: cannot determine item type from frontmatter in "<path>".` and `exit(1)`. A spike charter never reaches the server, so §1.2 Requirement 5's stated purpose ("so a spike push does not raise an L2 `TYPE_UNKNOWN`") is unreachable and §1.1's "gate-checked **and pushed**" is unmet. The fix is one line: `spike_id: 'spike',`. **Recommended TAKE — routed to the orchestrator because no §2.1 scenario covers push.** See §Open decisions #2.

### Execution route — CROSS-REPO

| | |
|---|---|
| Repos touched | **outer + cleargate-cli** |
| Commits | **2** (one per repo) |
| Outer root | **the MAIN checkout**, branch `story/STORY-054-02` off `sprint/S-39`. **NOT a worktree.** |
| cli root | `cleargate-cli`, branch `story/STORY-054-02` off cli `main` |

**Why not a worktree — and why the escape 054-04 uses is unavailable here.** S1a/S1b/S6 read `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/knowledge/readiness-gates.md` and S1c reads `.cleargate/templates/spike.md` + `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/templates/spike.md`, all via `META_ROOT = path.resolve(CLI_ROOT, '..')` (`:97-99`) — **the main checkout, unconditionally.** With the registry edits sitting in a worktree, S1a still enumerates 14 and all four new fixture rows fail as orphans (S5). 054-04 escapes this by authoring a *new* test that honours `CLEARGATE_META_ROOT`; **054-02 cannot, because `gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` has no such branch and adding one would be a fifth edit to a merged, QA-passed story's file plus a semantic loosening of a source-pinned guard.** Wave 4 runs alone, so the main checkout is free. Use it. (R18.)

**Precondition:** the main checkout must be on a branch containing 054-01's merge (`story/STORY-054-02` off `sprint/S-39`, post-054-01-merge). Verify `ls .cleargate/templates/spike.md cleargate-planning/.cleargate/templates/spike.md` before starting.

### Commit plan — exact

**Commit A — outer repo, MAIN checkout. LANDS FIRST.**
- `.cleargate/knowledge/readiness-gates.md` — append the two spike gate blocks
- `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/knowledge/readiness-gates.md` — **byte-identical** (Cross-Cutting Rule 1 applies here; verify with `diff`)
- `.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-protocol.md` — KNOWN_TYPES row + heading count
- `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-protocol.md` — byte-identical (verify with `diff`; the two are identical today)

**Commit B — `cleargate-cli` repo. LANDS SECOND, back-to-back in the same Developer turn.**
- `src/lib/work-item-type.ts`
- `test/lib/work-item-type.node.test.ts` (8 → 9, title, header comment)
- `test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` (T2 sites 1, 3, 4)
- `test/fixtures/gate-section-index/expected-headings.ts` (T2 site 2 — four rows)
- `test/lib/work-item-type-spike.node.test.ts` (**new** — this story's own four scenarios)
- *(conditional on §Open decisions #2)* `src/commands/push.ts`

**THERE IS NO INERT INTERMEDIATE. This is the one M1 story with an unavoidable red window.** Verified in both directions:

- **A alone** — registry now declares 18 `section(N)` criteria; the test still expects 14. **S1a red** (`criteria.length` 18 ≠ 14), **S1b red** ×4 (`spike.<id>: no template found for work_item_type "spike" — either add one to TEMPLATE_FOR or add "spike.<id>" to KNOWN_UNPINNABLE`, `checkPinning:265-272`), **S5 red** (no fixture rows), **S6 red** (`18 - 2 ≠ 12`).
- **B alone** — test expects 18; registry still declares 14. **S1a red**, **S5 red** (four orphan fixture rows).

**Handling.** Order A → B anyway (A carries the knowledge-doc two-tree obligation that is easiest to verify in isolation). Do both commits **inside one Developer turn**; do not end the turn between them. Run the suite **once, after B**. A red suite between A and B is expected and is not a defect — **do not "fix" it by editing the fixture** (that is exactly the tampering `expected-headings.ts:20-32` forbids and R15 warns about). The outer pre-commit hook does not run tests (verified: it runs only the red-test immutability check, `check:no-vitest`, `check:no-inline-id-regex` and the file-surface gate), so committing A with a red cli suite is not blocked.

**Merge gate (binding on DevOps):** verify both commits exist; merge A → `sprint/S-39`, then B → cli `main`; the suite is green only after both. **Do not merge A alone into `sprint/S-39` and call the wave done** — a sprint branch whose registry declares four criteria that no pinned test knows about is exactly the pre-BUG-042 state this sprint exists to end.

### Schema changes (verbatim)

**(a) Two gate blocks, appended to `readiness-gates.md` (both trees), after the `hotfix` block at the file tail (`:235-249`).** Modelled on the `initiative` advisory block (`:226-235`). Appending does **not** shift any existing `section(N)` — those are positions inside *templates*, not inside this file.

```yaml
- work_item_type: spike
  transition: ready-to-investigate
  severity: advisory
  criteria:
    - id: question-stated
      check: "section(1) has ≥1 listed-item"
    - id: timebox-and-kill-criteria-set
      check: "section(2) has ≥2 listed-item"
    - id: no-tbds
      check: "body does not contain marker 'TBD'"
    - id: ambiguity-gate-resolved
      check: "ambiguity-gate-resolved"
```

```yaml
- work_item_type: spike
  transition: ready-to-conclude
  severity: advisory
  criteria:
    - id: decision-log-populated
      check: "section(4) has ≥1 declared-item"
    - id: outcome-declared
      check: "section(5) has ≥1 listed-item"
    - id: no-tbds
      check: "body does not contain marker 'TBD'"
```

**Every predicate-kind choice here is deliberate and is the reason the gate is not vacuous.** §1.2 Requirement 4 says "the existing `section(N) has >=1 declared-item` and `body does not contain marker` predicates". Three of the four section criteria use **`listed-item`, not `declared-item`** — a superseding decision, made for cause:

- `declared-item` counts a bare bold subsection **label** as one item (`countDeclaredItems`, `readiness-predicates.ts:712-763`, definition-list-term branch). That is precisely the fail-open **BUG-050** documents and this sprint has quarantined. A spike §1 shipping `**Question:**` with nothing beneath would score 1 and pass.
- `listed-item` counts only `/^\s*- /gm`. Combined with STORY-054-01's **invariant 1** (zero bullets in §1, §2, §4, §5), the shipped template scores **0** in every gated section, so a pass means an author wrote something. This is FLASHCARD 2026-08-06 `#gate #danger` applied in its inverse: match the predicate to what the template emits — here, deliberately emit **none** of the counted shape.
- `decision-log-populated` is the exception and uses **`declared-item`**, because §4 ships as a table (invariant 2) and `listed-item` does not count table rows. A header + `|---|` separator + zero data rows scores `declared-item` **0**; a `|`-leading line cannot match the definition-list regex (which excludes `|`). Non-vacuous.
- `ambiguity-gate-resolved` is a closed-set predicate with no parameters that fails a document claiming 🟢 while unchecked `- [ ]` boxes remain. It is the machine backstop for STORY-054-01 Requirement 4's inverted gate and adds **no** fixture row.
- `severity: advisory` throughout, per Requirement 4 — a spike must never hard-block.

**(b) `cleargate-protocol.md` (both trees), two edits.**
- `:680` heading: `### KNOWN_TYPES — advisory registry (8 entries)` → `(9 entries)`.
- Table at `:682-693`: add one row. Placement — after `| \`sprint_report\` |`, so the ordering stays "narrative order then artifacts". Suggested text: `| \`spike\` | Bounded pre-sprint discovery charter |`.
- Verify with `diff` that both trees stay byte-identical (they are identical today).

**(c) `work-item-type.ts` — three registrations.**
- `:8` `WorkItemType` union += `| 'spike'`
- `:14-23` `FM_KEY_MAP` += `{ key: 'spike_id', type: 'spike' }`
- `:28-37` `PREFIX_MAP` += `{ prefix: 'SPIKE-', type: 'spike' }` — **append LAST, after `HOTFIX-`.** See R24: `detectWorkItemType` matches with `basename.includes(prefix)`, not `startsWith`, so a file named `STORY-054-0N_Spike-Something.md` contains the substring `SPIKE-` once uppercased. Placing `SPIKE-` after `STORY-` guarantees the story prefix wins. This is why §1.2 Requirement 2's "preserving longest-first ordering" is the wrong frame for **this** map — longest-first is `work-item-id.ts`'s `TYPE_PREFIXES` doctrine (a regex alternation); this map is a first-`includes`-wins scan and needs **most-specific-container-last**, which for `SPIKE-` means last.
- `:75-84` `WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS` += `spike: ['ready-to-investigate', 'ready-to-conclude']`. Typed `Record<WorkItemType, string[]>`, so omitting it fails `tsc` — the intended forcing function, and the only one of the six registration sites the compiler actually enforces (BUG-051 §"Why this was not caught").
- `:41-53` `work-item-id.ts` `TYPE_PREFIXES` **already contains `SPIKE`** (verified at `:48`). **Zero diff. Requirement 6 is a DoD assertion, not an edit.**

**(d) Fixture rows — exactly four, full heading lines, single-quoted TS strings (T5).**

```ts
'spike.question-stated':                 '## 1. The Question',
'spike.timebox-and-kill-criteria-set':   '## 2. Timebox & Kill Criteria',
'spike.decision-log-populated':          '## 4. Decision Log',
'spike.outcome-declared':                '## 5. Outcome & Spawned Items',
```

These pin the heading table in STORY-054-01's blueprint. **If the shipped `spike.md` deviates, the fix is 054-01's heading, not this fixture** (`expected-headings.ts:20-32`, invariant 3).

| `initiative` gate block shape | `.cleargate/knowledge/readiness-gates.md:226-235` | Copy the block skeleton; change type, transition, criteria. |
| `ambiguity-gate-resolved` predicate | `readiness-gates.md` Predicate Vocabulary #9 | Cite by name; it is closed-set and parameterless. **Do not implement anything** — `readiness-predicates.ts` is frozen (Cross-Cutting Rule 3). |
| `detectWorkItemTypeFromFm` / `detectWorkItemType` | `src/lib/work-item-type.ts:43`, `:59` (exported) | Import in the new test; do not re-implement detection. |
| Existing spike-adjacent test shape | `test/lib/work-item-type-hotfix.red.node.test.ts:32-100` | STORY-043-04 registered `hotfix` through the identical four sites. **This is the closest precedent in the repo — copy its structure.** |

### Test scenarios (from §2.1 Gherkin — all four, plus one this plan adds)

1. **`detectWorkItemTypeFromFm({ spike_id: 'SPIKE-001' })` → `'spike'`.**
2. **`detectWorkItemType('SPIKE-001')` → `'spike'`.** Add the ordering guard as a second assertion: `detectWorkItemType('STORY-054-03_Spike-Doctrine.md')` → `'story'`, **not** `'spike'`. Without it, R24 is unproven and a future map reorder silently mis-types stories.
3. **The advisory gate passes without an answer.** `cleargate gate check` against a tmpdir charter with §1, §2 populated and §5 empty, transition `ready-to-investigate` → pass. **Use a tmpdir fixture, never a live item** (FLASHCARD 2026-08-27 `#gate #worktree #test-harness #danger`: `gate check <abs-path>` roots the project on the *fixture's* tree, so a fixture in the main checkout reads main's registry — which, since this story runs in the main checkout, is what you want; state it explicitly in the test so nobody moves the fixture later).
4. **Error — a charter missing its kill criteria.** §2 with one bullet instead of two → the gate reports `timebox-and-kill-criteria-set` **by id**, with `section 2 has 1 listed-item (≥2 required)`.
5. **(added) `WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS` has 9 entries and `spike` maps to `['ready-to-investigate','ready-to-conclude']`.** This is the existing `:182` test bumped, not a new one — do not write a second count assertion.

Plus the inherited assertions that must be **green after commit B**: S1a (18/16), S1b (0 findings), S1c (now covering `spike.md` two-tree parity via the new `TEMPLATE_FOR` row), S5 (closed both ways), S6 (16, `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE.size` still 2).

### QA kick-back criteria (specific)

- `git diff cleargate-cli/src/lib/work-item-id.ts` non-empty → **kick back** (Requirement 6, §4.2 DoD).
- `git diff cleargate-cli/src/lib/readiness-predicates.ts` non-empty → **kick back** (Cross-Cutting Rule 3).
- Any of the four T2 sites unedited → **kick back**. A red S1a with an unedited `:432` is the T2-shaped mistake; a red S1a with `:432` edited to a number that does not equal `12 + 4 + 2` is worse.
- `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE` gained a member → **kick back**. No spike criterion is un-pinnable; `spike.md` exists. Taking the `:268` escape hatch trips `:634` anyway.
- A fixture row edited to match a drifted `spike.md` rather than 054-01's heading being fixed → **kick back** (`expected-headings.ts:20-32`).
- `readiness-gates.md` two trees not byte-identical, or `cleargate-protocol.md` two trees not byte-identical → **kick back**.
- `test/lib/work-item-type.node.test.ts:184` still asserting 8 → **kick back**.
- `SPIKE-` inserted before `STORY-` in `work-item-type.ts` `PREFIX_MAP` → **kick back** (R24).
- Commit B merged without commit A, or vice versa → **kick back, hold the merge**.
- `severity: enforcing` on either spike block → **kick back** (Requirement 4).
- Any predicate kind other than the four specified above, without an Architect amendment → **kick back**.

### Gotchas surfaced from code inspection

- **`WorkItemType` is declared twice, in two modules, with different members.** `src/lib/work-item-type.ts:8` (eight lowercase work-item types) and `src/lib/work-item-id.ts:56` (`typeof TYPE_PREFIXES[number]` — twelve UPPERCASE prefixes including `PLATFORM`, `AUDIT`, `PROP`). `gate.ts:33` imports the former by name. Adding `'spike'` to one is not adding it to the other, and the two are not interchangeable. (See R25.)
- **`check:no-inline-id-regex` runs on every outer commit** (`pre-commit-surface-gate.sh:52-64`, scanning `cleargate-cli/src` and `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/scripts`). It flags a type word adjacent to a regex escape (`TYPE_WORD` at `check-no-inline-id-regex.mjs:42`, which already lists `SPIKE`). The string literal `'SPIKE-'` carries no escape → no hit. Verified safe.
- **The registry's fenced yaml blocks are LISTS.** `loadGateBlocksFromText` (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:144-167`) replicates `gate.ts:85`'s `Array.isArray(parsed) ? parsed[0] : parsed`. Each appended block must be its **own fenced block opening with `- work_item_type:`**, exactly like every existing one. Two blocks in one fence, or a block without the leading `- `, yields `undefined` criteria and a **vacuous green at 0** — the S1a count would then be wrong in the silent direction.
- **`spike.no-tbds` appears in both blocks.** That is fine and already normal (`no-tbds` occurs eight times across the registry) — it is not a `section(N)` criterion, so `enumerateSectionCriteria` never keys on it and no duplicate-key collision arises. `mutateCriterionIndex`'s non-block-scoped anchor (M0 post-flight §6.4) is likewise unaffected: none of the four new **section** ids collides with an existing one (verified).
- **`gate.ts:124` reads `WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS[type]`** to resolve which transitions a type offers. Omitting the `spike` entry is a `tsc` error, not a runtime surprise. Good.
- **`stamp-tokens.ts:196` `idKeys` lacks `spike_id`** (it already lacks `hotfix_id`). A spike charter would not get `draft_tokens` stamped. Degradation, no §2.1 scenario depends on it, and it is one of the fourteen BUG-051 registries. **Out of surface. Record the count in the commit message** (mirroring 054-04 Requirement 5), do not fix.
- **`.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-protocol.md` is not a gated template**, so adding a table row shifts no `section(N)` anywhere. Cross-Cutting Rule 4 does **not** fire for edit (b).

Producer for nothing in M1. **Touches `.cleargate/templates/story.md`, which STORY-054-06 (M2) also edits** — 03 lands first, so 06 rebases onto the corrected rubric line. Surface-disjoint from 054-01, 054-02 and 054-04.

The `Related Files` cell lists `.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md` — the **live** copy, which is **untracked** (`git ls-files .claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md` returns nothing; `.gitignore:13` covers `.claude/`, and CR-099 removed the last tracked file under it). All three are byte-identical today (md5 `8dd6ef8cec45ef30e1ac6b55e60dfa94` on live and canonical; the payload copy is the same size and was regenerated 2026-08-27), so the delta is clean and the Gate-4 re-sync is a straight copy.

The phrase `as its own story` must not survive (§2.2 verification step 2). **This line is raw line 27, inside the `<instructions>` block (lines 1–62; frontmatter is 64–105).** Consequences the Developer must know and not act on:

```
**Triage first, draft second.** Every user request gets classified (Epic / Story / CR / Bug / Pull / Push) *before* any drafting.
```

Add `Spike` to the parenthetical. §1.5's mitigation is explicit that this is **one word added to an existing list, not a new paragraph** — `CLAUDE.md` is loaded every session and carries a standing token cost. A `###` subsection, **not** a new top-level `## ` — §2 currently has no subsections and every `## ` in that file is numbered, so a new top-level heading would force a renumber of §§3–11. Content, all four facts from Requirement 3: spikes run **before sprint kickoff**; a spike takes **no `state.json` slot**; the sprint loop **gives it no worktree**; prototype code lives on a throwaway `spike/SPIKE-NNN` branch that is **discarded and never merged**. Add one cross-reference bullet to `## 10. What This Skill Does NOT Cover` (`:755-767`), matching that section's existing `- **Topic** → pointer.` shape.

**Requirement 4 — the protocol subsection, both trees.** Append `## Guidance Surface Reach` (**unnumbered**, per §3.2 and the EPIC-043 trailing-section convention) at the **end of the file**, after `### 23.4` (`:918`). Protocol max numbered section is **23**; the next free number is §24 and this story deliberately does not consume it. Three rows, matching Requirement 4 verbatim:

| Surface | Reaches | At what moment |
|---|---|---|
| Template `<instructions>` block | the **drafting** agent only — stripped from every authored instance | draft time |
| An agent's own `.md` (`.claude/agents/*.md`) | that **executing** agent | dispatch time |
| Always-on `CLAUDE.md` | every agent in every session | **before** a template is chosen — the only surface reachable at triage |

Ground it in the verified fact rather than asserting it: authored instances carry zero `<instructions>` blocks (FLASHCARD 2026-08-25 `#agents #scaffold #danger`, EPIC-054), which is why a rule an executing agent needs cannot live only in a template.

1. **Triage can reach the spike type** → `grep -n "Spike" CLAUDE.md` shows it in the classification parenthetical, **and** the same in `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md`.

4. **A rule placed only in a template instructions block** → the protocol's `## Guidance Surface Reach` subsection identifies that such a rule reaches only the drafting agent.

**Test shape.** §4.1 asks for **0 unit tests** and **3 grep assertions**. Take that literally: **no new `.node.test.ts`.** The grep assertions are executed as QA-Verify manual steps and their output pasted into the QA report. Rationale: a machine assertion would have to live in `cleargate-cli/test/`, which makes a documentation-only story cross-repo for no gain — and the two existing tests that would be its natural home are both integration-tier and excluded from the default runner anyway.

**Add a fourth manual check** beyond §4.1's three: `diff` **all four** canonical/live pairs (`story.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `cleargate-protocol.md`; the SKILL.md pair is deliberately *not* diffed because live is untracked and re-syncs at Gate 4). §4.2's DoD says "All four canonical mirrors updated and parity-diffed" — the parity claim is only meaningful for the three tracked pairs.

## Wave-3 disjointness re-check (re-tested against the AMENDED 054-04 surface)

**Verdict: YES on file surfaces and execution roots. NO on the pre-commit surface gate, which is repo-global. Parallelism survives with one stated serialization.**

**(a) File surfaces — disjoint. Re-derived, not inherited.** 054-04's amended surface added seven paths beyond the original four; every one was re-tested against 054-01's two:

| 054-04 site | Path | Collides with 054-01? |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `cleargate-cli/src/wiki/derive-bucket.ts` | no |
| 2–5 | `cleargate-cli/src/wiki/page-schema.ts` | no |
| 6 | `cleargate-cli/src/commands/wiki-build.ts` | no |
| 7 | `cleargate-cli/src/commands/wiki-ingest.ts` | no |
| 8 | `cleargate-cli/src/wiki/load-wiki.ts` | no |
| 9–10 | `cleargate-cli/src/wiki/synthesis/product-state.ts` | no |
| 11 | `cleargate-cli/templates/synthesis/product-state.md` | no |
| 12 | `.cleargate/config.yml` | no |
| 13 | `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.yml` | no |
| new test | `cleargate-cli/test/wiki/bucket-registry-parity.node.test.ts` | no |

054-01's surface is `.cleargate/templates/spike.md` + `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/templates/spike.md`. **Neither appears anywhere in 054-04's thirteen sites, its advisory list, or its test file.** Intersection is empty. Confirmed disjoint against the amended surface, not the original.

**(b) Execution roots — disjoint, but only because R14 was refused.** 054-01 → `.worktrees/STORY-054-01/` (outer only). 054-04 → `.worktrees/STORY-054-04/` (outer) + the `cleargate-cli` checkout (cli). Neither story occupies the main outer checkout, which stays on `sprint/S-39` for the orchestrator. `cleargate-cli` is a single working checkout with no worktrees (`git worktree list` there returns one row), so **two concurrent cli branches are impossible** — 054-04 holds it alone for all of wave 3. Had 054-01 taken M0 R14's optional `TEMPLATE_FOR` row, wave 3 would have serialized on the cli checkout for a benefit that a worktree cannot even realise (S1c `continue`s on a path that exists only in the worktree). **Refusing R14 is what makes wave 3 parallel.**

**(c) The pre-commit surface gate is repo-global and will cross-judge. This is the real wave-3 hazard.**

`file_surface_diff.sh` resolves **one** active story for the entire outer repo and checks **every** staged file against that story's §3.1. Its resolver (`:123-133`) filters on `st.get('state','') in ('In Progress','Ready','In Review')` — and **none of those three strings is a member of `VALID_STATES`** (`.cleargate/scripts/constants.mjs:35-44`: `Ready to Bounce`, `Bouncing`, `QA Passed`, `Architect Passed`, `Sprint Review`, `Done`, `Escalated`, `Parking Lot`). The primary filter is **dead code**; the gate always falls through to the `max(updated_at)` fallback at `:135-144`. That is the mechanism behind the already-recorded wave-2 Mid-Sprint Amendment ("while any story is `Bouncing`, an orchestrator commit … is judged against the in-flight story's surface"). Filed as **R16**.

**Mitigation for wave 3 — no bypass required.** Both wave-3 stories make outer commits (054-01: one; 054-04: commit A). **Serialize those two outer commits**, and immediately before each, run `bash .cleargate/scripts/run_script.sh update_state.mjs <story-id> Bouncing` so the committing story holds `max(updated_at)` and its §3.1 is the one enforced. Everything else — authoring, the entire cli-side commit B, test runs — proceeds in parallel. `SKIP_SURFACE_GATE=1` is **not** authorized for wave 3; if the gate still blocks after the state touch, that is a finding, not a nuisance, and the Developer returns BLOCKED.

Note that 054-04's **commit B is unaffected**: `cleargate-cli/.git/hooks/` has zero installed hooks, so no surface gate runs there at all (R17).

---

## Cross-story risks

IDs continue from M0, which ended at **R15**.

**R16 — The pre-commit surface gate's active-story resolver is dead code, so it always judges by `max(updated_at)`. Any wave with two concurrent stories cross-judges.** `file_surface_diff.sh:126` filters `state in ('In Progress','Ready','In Review')`; `constants.mjs:35-44` defines the v3 vocabulary as `Ready to Bounce | Bouncing | QA Passed | Architect Passed | Sprint Review | Done | Escalated | Parking Lot`. **Zero overlap** — the primary branch can never match, and the fallback (`max(updated_at)`) runs on every invocation. Consequences: (a) the gate is enforced against whichever story was most recently state-touched, not the one committing; (b) the wave-2 `SKIP_SURFACE_GATE=1` bypass already logged in `sprint-context.md` had this as its root cause, which the amendment did not identify; (c) any future parallel wave hits it. **Not fixed here** — `file_surface_diff.sh` is in no M1 story's surface and fixing a gate mid-sprint while it guards four stories is the wrong order. **Mitigation is procedural and stated in §Wave-3 disjointness (c).** **Orchestrator: file it.** It is a one-line fix (`VALID_STATES`-aware filter, ideally importing from `constants.mjs`) and it is the same divergence class as BUG-041 and BUG-051 — a vocabulary duplicated into a second file and left to drift. Route to the sprint report alongside R1, R5 and R10.

**R17 — `cleargate-cli` has ZERO installed git hooks. Every cli-side commit in this sprint is completely ungated.** `ls cleargate-cli/.git/hooks/` returns only `.sample` files; the outer repo's `.git/hooks/pre-commit` symlink to `.claude/hooks/pre-commit.sh` exists only there. So for **commit B of 054-02 and 054-04**: no file-surface check, no red-test immutability check, no `check:no-vitest`, no `check:no-inline-id-regex`, **and no typecheck or test run** (the outer hook does not run those either — `config.yml` `gates.precommit` is consumed by `pre_gate_runner.sh`, not by git). **The Developer must run `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run typecheck && npm --prefix cleargate-cli test` by hand before every cli commit, and QA-Verify must confirm the run happened rather than assume the hook did it.** CLAUDE.md's "Pre-commit: typecheck clean + test green — non-negotiable" is a discipline rule here, not an enforced one. This also means STORY-054-05's cli commits were ungated, which is worth knowing when reading its green.

**R18 — By default a `cleargate-cli` test cannot see a file that exists only in an outer worktree. `CLEARGATE_META_ROOT` is the sanctioned escape, and the pinning test does NOT have it.** `META_ROOT = path.resolve(CLI_ROOT, '..')` (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:97-99`; same shape in `dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts:39-41`, `pin-placeholder-coverage.node.test.ts:30`). `CLI_ROOT` is the physical `cleargate-cli/` directory inside the **main** checkout. Two failure shapes, both quiet: an `existsSync`-guarded read `continue`s (S1c `:451`) and reads as coverage; an unguarded read returns the main checkout's pre-edit content and reads as a legitimate red. Four landed test files already carry the override (`enforcement-doc-coherence.node.test.ts:45-47`, `ratchet-retired.node.test.ts`, `no-exec-mode-vocab.node.test.ts:41-43`, `close-sprint-assume-ack-guard.node.test.ts:53-55`) under an explicit prior ruling (FLASHCARD 2026-07-18 `#test-harness #cross-repo`). **Split verdict for M1:**

- **054-04 authors a NEW test → it MUST carry the override**, and therefore may run in `.worktrees/STORY-054-04/`.
- **054-02 must edit `gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts`, which has NO override**, and adding one is a fifth edit to a merged, QA-passed story's file plus a semantic change to a source-pinned guard. **054-02 therefore executes in the MAIN outer checkout on `story/STORY-054-02`.** Wave 4 runs alone, so the main checkout is free.
- M0's R14 optimization is unrealisable from a worktree for the same reason, which is the second half of why 054-01 refuses it.

Related: FLASHCARD 2026-08-27 `#gate #worktree #test-harness #danger` and 2026-07-27 `#dogfood #worktree`.

**R19 — STORY-054-02 has no inert intermediate; its red window is unavoidable and must not be "fixed".** Verified in both directions (registry-first → S1a/S1b/S5/S6 red; test-first → S1a/S5 red). The mitigation is temporal, not structural: both commits in one Developer turn, one suite run after the second. **The danger is a Developer who runs the suite between commits, sees four `no template found for work_item_type "spike"` findings, and "resolves" them by adding the four ids to `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE`** — which the `:268` message itself suggests. That escape hatch trips `:634`'s `size === 2`, so it fails loudly, but it costs a bounce. Say it in the dispatch. Contrast 054-04, which **does** have an inert intermediate (config-first) and must use it.

**R20 — `test/lib/work-item-type.node.test.ts:182-186` hardcodes `WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS` at 8 entries, and nothing tells 054-02's Developer.** Same tripwire class as S1a's `14`/`12`, same failure mode: a red test in a file the story never mentions, easily misread as "I registered the type wrong". **Bump to 9; update the test title and the header comment at `:18`.** Precedent for the whole edit set: STORY-043-04 registered `hotfix` through the identical sites — `test/lib/work-item-type-hotfix.red.node.test.ts` is the template to copy.

**R21 — `cleargate push <spike-charter>` hard-errors today, and STORY-054-02's §3.1 does not include the fix.** `push.ts:506-518` `getItemType` has a six-key private map that lacks `spike_id`; `push.ts:253-257` turns the resulting `null` into `Error: cannot determine item type from frontmatter` + `exit(1)`. §1.1's user story ("gate-checked **and pushed**") and §1.2 Requirement 5's premise (a spike push reaching the server) are both unmet. One-line fix. **No §2.1 scenario covers it**, so it is a scope call — §Open decisions #2, recommendation: **take it**. If it is not taken, STORY-054-02 ships a work-item type that cannot leave the machine, and the failure surfaces to a human, not a test.

**R22 — `.cleargate/config.yml` and `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.yml` are NOT byte-identical, and STORY-054-04's §3.1 says they must be.** Verified: the live file carries `index_token_ceiling`, `bucket_pagination_ceiling`, a `gates:` block and a `worktree:` block that canonical does not, plus different comment prose. Cross-Cutting Rule 1 is scoped to `.cleargate/knowledge/**` and `.cleargate/templates/**` — `config.yml` is neither, and the divergence is correct (canonical is the shipped scaffold seed; live is this repo's configuration). **A Developer who reads row 13 literally and "restores parity" deletes this repo's gate and worktree configuration.** The obligation is the one-line list addition in both files, same commit, nothing else. Two adjacent sites deliberately **not** taken: `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.example.yml:11-16` (commented-out reference list) and `.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-protocol.md:650` (§21.2's "by default the wiki ingests every bucket (…)" sentence, which goes stale) — see R27.

**R23 — STORY-054-01's template invariants are what make STORY-054-02's gate non-vacuous, and they are cheap now and impossible later.** Zero `- ` bullets in §1/§2/§4/§5 (fences included — `evalSection` does not strip them), §4 as a zero-data-row table, no literal `TBD`, no body-level `SPRINT-<digits>`, no bare `---` inside `<instructions>`, no fenced `## `. Drop invariant 1 and `question-stated`/`outcome-declared` pass on the unedited template; drop invariant 2 and `decision-log-populated` does too. **This is BUG-050 avoided by authoring rather than by predicate change** — which is the only route available, since `readiness-predicates.ts` is frozen. Every invariant is a QA kick-back criterion in 054-01's blueprint.

**R24 — `work-item-type.ts`'s `PREFIX_MAP` matches with `includes`, not `startsWith`, so `SPIKE-` must be appended LAST.** `detectWorkItemType:60-68` uppercases the basename and returns on the first `basename.includes(prefix)`. A file named `STORY-054-0N_Spike-Something.md` uppercases to `…_SPIKE-SOMETHING.MD` and **contains the substring `SPIKE-`**. With `SPIKE-` before `STORY-`, that story is typed `spike`. Today's M1 story filenames all use `Spike_` (underscore) so the hazard is latent, not live — but it is one filename away. Requirement 2's phrase "preserving longest-first ordering" is `work-item-id.ts` `TYPE_PREFIXES` doctrine (a regex alternation, where longest-first prevents `PROPOSAL` settling on `PROP`); this map needs **most-specific-container-last**. Append after `HOTFIX-` and assert it with a `detectWorkItemType('STORY-054-03_Spike-Doctrine.md') === 'story'` test case.

**R25 — Two different `WorkItemType` unions with the same name in two modules.** `src/lib/work-item-type.ts:8` (eight lowercase types; consumed by `gate.ts:33`, `lint-checks.ts:17`, `stamp-tokens.ts:20`) and `src/lib/work-item-id.ts:56` (`typeof TYPE_PREFIXES[number]` — twelve UPPERCASE prefixes, already including `SPIKE`, plus `PLATFORM`/`AUDIT`/`PROP` which are not work-item types at all). STORY-054-02 edits the first and must leave the second at zero diff (Requirement 6). An import from the wrong module compiles and means something else. Not a defect to fix here; a naming hazard to state.

**R26 — `cleargate-cli/templates/synthesis/product-state.md` is TRACKED, and is not the gitignored payload.** `cleargate-cli/.gitignore:5` ignores `templates/cleargate-planning/` only; `git ls-files templates/synthesis/` returns four files. STORY-054-04 site 11 is a required, committable edit. Cross-Cutting Rule 2 does not apply to it. Getting this wrong in either direction — refusing to edit it, or hand-editing the payload copy instead — breaks Scenario 2 silently, because a bucket present in the code and absent from the mustache table renders nothing at all.

**R27 — `cleargate-protocol.md:650` names the default bucket set in prose and goes stale the moment `spikes` exists.** §21.2 reads *"By default the wiki ingests every bucket (`epics`, `stories`, `sprints`, `proposals`, `initiatives`, `crs`, `bugs`)"*. Nothing pins it — this is R11's shape one file over: prose that states a fact no test parses. **Not assigned to 054-04** (it is not required by any §2.1 scenario, and 054-04 has no other reason to open the protocol). **Recommended home: STORY-054-02's commit A**, which already opens `cleargate-protocol.md` in both trees for the KNOWN_TYPES row — a one-token addition to a file already staged. Routed as §Open decisions #3.

**R28 — The bucket-parity test's extractors can go silently blind, and an empty extraction must never read as a pass.** Ten of the eleven sites are read by regex over source text; a rename, a reformat, or a Prettier line-break can turn any extractor into a function returning `[]`, at which point the matrix has nothing to compare and P2 passes vacuously. **P5 (every extractor returned non-empty) is not optional garnish — it is what keeps the test honest.** Same lesson as TPV's T3 on 054-05's S3b: a structural assertion satisfied by the broken world proves nothing. QA-Verify must confirm P5 exists and that its failure message names the extractor.

**R29 — `cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json` is regenerated, whitelisted, and belongs to DevOps.** `build-manifest.ts` walks `cleargate-planning/` by pattern; `.cleargate/templates/spike.md` matches `.cleargate/templates/*` → tier `template`, `overwrite_policy: merge-3way`, automatically. The file is whitelisted at `.cleargate/scripts/surface-whitelist.txt:12`, so staging it will not trip the surface gate — but **no story stages it**. DevOps refreshes it post-merge; precedent `a582c890` ("manifest refresh"). Note this is a *different* artifact from the gitignored `cleargate-cli/templates/cleargate-planning/**` payload: MANIFEST.json is tracked in the outer repo and must not be left stale past 054-01's merge, or `cleargate upgrade` will not install `spike.md` into existing target repos.

**R30 — Registering `spike` in `work-item-type.ts` while leaving eleven other id-key registries untouched ADDS a row to BUG-051's drift table in the same sprint that documents it.** Verified sites lacking `spike_id` after 054-02: `src/lib/sync/work-items.ts:149`+`:178` (falls through to a silent `return 'story'` — the one genuinely silent path), `src/commands/push.ts:507`+`:459`+`:481` (hard error, R21), `src/commands/pull.ts:228`+`:270`, `src/commands/sync.ts:561`+`:714`, `src/commands/stamp-tokens.ts:196`, `src/lib/wiki-comments-render.ts:34`+`:46`, `src/lib/wiki/contradict.ts:312`, `.claude/hooks/stamp-and-gate.sh`'s work-item-id grep alternation. `src/lib/parent-rollup.ts:64-72` is safe — it falls back to the filename stem, which yields `SPIKE-001` correctly. **None is in any M1 story's surface and none is taken.** Obligation: **STORY-054-02's commit message must record the count**, exactly as STORY-054-04 Requirement 5 requires for buckets, so BUG-051 inherits measured evidence rather than a second archaeology pass. Only the compiler-forced site (`WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS`) is self-enforcing; that is one of twelve, which is BUG-051's whole thesis.

**R31 — The live `/.claude/` re-sync after STORY-054-03 is unenforced by any test that actually runs.** `sprint-execution-mirror.integration.node.test.ts` and `canonical-live-parity.red.integration.node.test.ts` are both `@cleargate-tier: integration`, excluded by `run-default-tests.mjs:26`; the latter additionally skips gitignored live paths by design. So a canonical SKILL.md that has diverged from the live one produces **no signal at all** during the sprint. This is the mechanism by which CR-026 shipped its own fix while still running the buggy hook (CLAUDE.md, *Dogfood split*). **Gate-4 obligation, stated explicitly:** after 054-03 merges, re-sync `/.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md` from canonical (`cleargate init` or hand-port) and regenerate the npm payload (`npm --prefix cleargate-cli run prebuild`). All three copies are byte-identical today, so the delta is a clean copy.

## Open decisions for orchestrator

1. **Does `spike.md` carry a `## Prior work` section?** CLAUDE.md's duplicate-check rule tells the drafting agent to record wiki-query evidence in "the document's own `## Prior work` section" for anything written under `pending-sync/`, and a spike charter lands there. But STORY-054-01 §1.2 Requirement 1 is an exhaustive ordered list that omits it, and `prior-work-recorded` passes when the section is absent (Predicate Vocabulary #8, migration grace). **Default in this plan: OMIT** — a spike's premise is that no prior answer exists, its §1 and §3 carry the citation load, and every extra `## ` costs a position. **If the orchestrator overrides:** `## Prior work` becomes position 6 and `## ClearGate Ambiguity Gate` becomes 7; STORY-054-02's four fixture rows and four `section(N)` values are unaffected (all are ≤5), so the override is cheap — but it must be decided **before 054-01 is dispatched**, not after.

2. **Does STORY-054-02 take the one-line `push.ts` fix (R21)?** Arguments to take: §1.1's user story says "gate-checked **and pushed**"; §1.2 Requirement 5's entire purpose (avoiding an L2 `TYPE_UNKNOWN`) presumes the push reaches the server, and today it exits 1 before that; it is one map entry; and shipping a work-item type that cannot leave the machine is a visible-to-humans regression. Arguments to defer: no §2.1 Gherkin scenario covers push, so by the same standard 054-04's amended §3.1 applied ("every site is required by the approved Gherkin"), it is out; and `push.ts` is named in BUG-051 §4's Modify list. **Recommendation: TAKE**, add `cleargate-cli/src/commands/push.ts` to §3.1's Related Files, and add a fifth Gherkin scenario (`Given a spike charter / When cleargate push resolves its type / Then it resolves to spike`). **Flagged, not decided.**

3. **Who fixes `cleargate-protocol.md:650`'s stale default-bucket sentence (R27)?** Options: (a) STORY-054-02 commit A, which already opens that file in both trees — one token, zero extra surface; (b) leave stale and route to BUG-051 / the sprint report. **Recommendation: (a).** **Flagged, not decided.**

4. **File the surface-gate resolver defect (R16) now, or carry it to the sprint report?** It is a live cause of `SKIP_SURFACE_GATE=1` bypasses (one already logged this sprint) and it degrades every parallel wave. It is also a one-line fix in a file no story owns, and touching a gate mid-sprint while it guards four stories is the wrong order. **Recommendation: file now, schedule later** — the same disposition R5 got. **Flagged, not decided.**

5. **Confirm the sprint-context §Mid-Sprint Amendment framework gap is still open.** The wave-2 amendment raised "should the surface gate exempt `.cleargate/delivery/**` and `.cleargate/sprint-runs/**` when the committing branch is the sprint branch" to the human and records no answer. Wave 3 will make orchestrator commits between the two serialized story commits and will hit it again. **Not mine to decide.**

Append-only. Nothing above this line is rewritten. Full detail:
`.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/STORY-054-01-arch-postflight.md`.

## A1. §"Schema changes (verbatim)" is CONFIRMED, not amended

The four gated criteria were re-executed against the committed `spike.md` (`85473ffb`) in both trees with
the real exported `evaluate()`. All four score **0** and therefore fail on the unedited template:
`section(1) >=1 listed-item` → 0 · `section(2) >=2 listed-item` → 0 · `section(4) >=1 declared-item` → 0 ·
`section(5) >=1 listed-item` → 0. `no-tbds` and `ambiguity-gate-resolved` also behave as specified. The
four fixture rows in §(d) each pin a **character-exact** match to what `resolveHeadingLine` returns. The
`## Prior work` decision landed at position 6 and **shifted nothing** — all four gated positions are ≤5.
**No change to the verbatim YAML, the four `section(N)` values, or the four fixture rows.**

## A2. AMENDMENT to §STORY-054-01 "Template invariants" #1 — the invariant is correct but has no witness, and this is now measured

Invariant 1 ("zero `- ` bullets in §1/§2/§4/§5 … this is the reason 054-02's presence criteria have
teeth") is right, and the Developer complied. What the blueprint did not say is that the invariant is a
**pure authoring convention with no machine witness**, and that this repo has already broken the identical
convention twice, silently:

| Criterion | Predicate | Score on its own shipped template | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|
| `story.dod-declared` | `section(4) >=1 listed-item` | **3** | `story.md` §4 ships three `- [ ]` |
| `initiative.success-criteria-populated` | `section(5) >=1 listed-item` | **3** | `initiative.md` §5 ships three `- {…}` |

Neither is a BUG-050 case. Registry-wide: **9 of 12 pinnable `section(N)` criteria are already vacuous on
their own template**; only `initiative.user-flow-populated`, `hotfix.anomaly-populated` and
`hotfix.verification-steps-nonempty` are not. Full table in the post-flight §1.2.

**Consequence for the plan's §4-asymmetry claim.** §4's `declared-item`-over-a-table guarantee is
*presence of a shape that cannot score* and survives arbitrary editing. §1/§2/§5's is *absence of a shape
that would score* and does not survive one edit. The asymmetry is genuine and **cannot be closed by
rewriting `spike.md`** — no prose shape is immune to a later bullet. Equalization is a test's job.

Mutation-verified collapse modes (real `evaluate()`, committed body): bulleting §2's two bold-label
paragraphs → `timebox-and-kill-criteria-set` green on an empty charter; bulleting §1's blockquote →
`question-stated` green; **deleting any one `## ` heading above position 5** slides bullet-rich
`## Prior work` (1 `listed-item`) or the Ambiguity Gate (5 `listed-item`) into a gated slot →
`outcome-declared` **and** `decision-log-populated` green. Cross-Cutting Rule 4 covers heading
*insertion* only; **the deletion direction is uncovered**.

## A3. NEW OBLIGATION on §STORY-054-02 — Pin A (non-vacuity assertion)

Add to 054-02's new `*.node.test.ts` (~12 lines, no new file, no new surface, inside Requirement 4's own
claim): for each of the four spike criteria, assert the **shipped** `.cleargate/templates/spike.md` FAILS.
Catches every collapse mode in A2. Needs no fixture, no allowlist, and no edit to any merged file.

**Pin B is explicitly NOT taken** — the registry-wide form with a size-asserted `KNOWN_VACUOUS` set edits
`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` (merged, QA-passed) and cannot be green until the nine-row
allowlist is seeded. Routed to the sprint report as a follow-on CR; the seed is measured and recorded in
sprint-context §Adjacent Implementations.

## A4. NEW FINDING routed as Open Decision — the spike gate is non-satisfiable by following the template

A complete, correct charter written in the prose shape `spike.md` teaches scores **0** on
`question-stated`, `timebox-and-kill-criteria-set` **and** `outcome-declared`; the same answers as `- `
bullets flip all three green. Advisory, so nothing blocks — but every authored spike ships three failing
criteria. Cheapest fix, non-conflicting with invariant 1: amend §1/§2/§5 guidance to instruct `- ` bullet
answers (the instruction sentence itself contains no line-initial `- `, so the template still scores 0);
phrase as prose or inside the existing `>` blockquote, **never as a worked-example bullet**.
**Not a 054-01 kickback** — §1.3 scopes gate satisfiability out and no §2.1 scenario covers it.
**Orchestrator decision:** whether `.cleargate/templates/spike.md` + mirror join **STORY-054-02 §3.1**.

## A5. CORRECTION to the T2 restatement — four assertion sites PLUS three stale test titles

Re-verified against merged `db13a03`. Sites 1-4 stand as written (`TEMPLATE_FOR` `:111-118`; fixture rows;
S1a `:432`=`14` / `:434`=`12`; S6 `:644`=`12`, with `:634` `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE.size, 2` staying **2**).
Site 5 is the **test titles** at `:430`, `:443`, `:633` — after 054-02 they must read 18 / 16 / seven
templates. They break no build, which is exactly why they rot.

`cleargate-cli/test/docs/gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts`, and

## Post-flight addendum — STORY-054-04 (Architect, 2026-08-27). Append-only.

Full pass: `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/STORY-054-04-arch-postflight.md`. Verdict **PASS**.
Three corrections to this plan's own risk register, each verified by execution:

**R18 refinement — the failure mode is inverted for this test.** The plan warns that a wrong root
"reads as a legitimate red". For `bucket-registry-parity.red.node.test.ts` it does not: P6a/P6b carry
`skip: !fs.existsSync(...)` (`:389`, `:402`), so a wrong `REPO_ROOT` presents as **skipped** — silent
absence of coverage. **Acceptance for any targeted run is three numbers: `pass 11 · fail 0 · skipped 0`.**
`pass 9 · fail 0 · skipped 2` is a failed verification, not a partial one.

**R22 confirmed by the merged bytes, not by inspection.** `git merge-tree --write-tree sprint/S-39
story/STORY-054-04` → `0c2bce0b`; the merged live `config.yml` is the current file **plus exactly one
line**, retaining `index_token_ceiling`, `bucket_pagination_ceiling`, `gates:` and `worktree:`;
canonical stays the seed shape. The parity test run against that materialized tree: **11/0/0**.

**New, not in the register — the merge-order argument is cross-BRANCH, not just cross-repo.**
The plan's inert-intermediate argument is correct, but its conclusion holds only because the main
outer checkout is on `sprint/S-39`. P6a/P6b read that checkout's working tree, so after B lands, cli
`main` depends on an **unmerged outer branch** until sprint close. Any outer checkout switched to
`main` runs the cli suite red on P6a/P6b; a standalone cli clone skips instead. Unavoidable, minimal
under A→B, closed at close. **The Failure-handling table's "A lands, B fails" row should read: leave
A in place** — A-alone is inert (verified: every `ingest_buckets` reader is a post-derivation
membership filter, and no validator rejects an unknown entry), so reverting costs a commit and buys
nothing. Revert only under policy, and then `git revert -m 1 <merge commit>`.

**Binding on every future bucket, from this merge forward.** `derive-bucket.ts` `PREFIX_MAP` is now
the parity test's authority: appending a row is an **eleven-site commitment**, and P3/P4 make
papering over it a visible edit. P4 is currently spike-specific and must be generalised by the next
bucket-adding story.

  lands green through `gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` — measured below at 14/14.

## NORMATIVE RULINGS (bind both stories; supersede the story text where they conflict)

The M1 post-flight issued five pins (P1-P5). All five were re-verified here by execution. Verdicts:

| Pin | M1 claim | M2 verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **P1** | `story.md` insertion at position 4 moves exactly one registry value: `story.dod-declared` `section(4)`→`section(5)` at `readiness-gates.md:149`, both trees | **CONFIRMED.** Measured: full default suite 2516 tests, **zero new failures** with the bump applied; **three** failures without it (§Blast radius). |
| **P2** | `CR.md` placement must be ≥7 (strictly after `## 3. Execution Sandbox`) ⇒ zero CR registry edits | **CONFIRMED.** Placement at 7 measured: S3a/S3b green, `blast-radius-populated` (3) and `sandbox-paths-declared` (6) both still resolve to their fixture headings. |
| **P3** | `Bug.md` placement must be ≥3 ⇒ zero Bug registry edits | **CONFIRMED.** Placement at 5 measured: `bug.repro-steps-deterministic` `section(2)` still resolves to `## 2. Reproduction Protocol`; shell test T4-B green. |
| **P4** | The real heading must NOT be `## 9. Task Breakdown` | **CONFIRMED and taken.** See N3. |
| **P5** | `task-breakdown-complete` must be a NAMED predicate, not `section(N)` | **CONFIRMED, with evidence stronger than the pin claimed.** See N1. |

### N1 — `task-breakdown-complete` is a NAMED predicate. Ruling: agreed, with code-level proof.

The pin was argued from `section(N)`'s inability to express "passes on absence". That argument is
correct and there is a second, harder one: `evalSection` is the *only* branch that can express a
count, and **Cross-Cutting Rule 3 freezes it for the whole sprint**
(`readiness-predicates.ts:632-657`, read-only, not exported). A `section(N)` form of R5 would
require either editing frozen code or accepting a hard `section N not found` on every legacy item.

Measured, on the real evaluator:

- `evalSection` returns `pass: false, detail: "section N not found (body has M sections)"` when the
  index overruns (`readiness-predicates.ts:648-655`). There is no absence-tolerant mode.
- The three existing named predicates — `existing-surfaces-verified` (`:894`),
  `prior-work-recorded` (`:995`), `ambiguity-gate-resolved` (`:1052`) — all locate their section by
  `body.split(/^(?=## )/m)` + `headingTitleOf` and all three return `pass: true` on absence. That is
  the shape R5 describes, verbatim, three times over.
- As a named predicate, `enumerateSectionCriteria`
  (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:183-206`) never keys on it — it calls `parsePredicate`
  inside a `try` and `continue`s on anything that is not `kind === 'section'` (`:191-195`).
  **Measured: S1a's `18`/`16` (`:433`/`:435`) and S6's `16` (`:645`) did not move, and
  `expected-headings.ts` needed zero edits — the pinning test ran 14 pass / 0 fail / 0 skipped
  under the complete change.**

**Adding the predicate is a four-site edit in one file, plus one doc site.** All in the same commit:

1. `readiness-predicates.ts:12-23` — add `| { kind: 'task-breakdown-complete' }` to the
   `ParsedPredicate` union.
2. `readiness-predicates.ts:~130` — add the closed-set branch in `parsePredicate`, immediately after
   the `ambiguity-gate-resolved` branch (`:129-132`).
3. `readiness-predicates.ts:~181` — add `case 'task-breakdown-complete': return evalTaskBreakdownComplete(doc);`
   to the `evaluate` switch (after `:181`).
4. End of file — the `evalTaskBreakdownComplete` function (verbatim in §Schema below).
5. `readiness-gates.md` Predicate Vocabulary (`:8` and the entry list) — "exactly **9** predicate
   shapes" → **10**, plus entry **10**. Both trees.

`evalSection` is **not touched**. `git diff` on `readiness-predicates.ts:632-657` must be empty —
this is a QA kick-back criterion, and S4 (`:594-608`) pins the three lines the checker replicates.

### N2 — Present-case semantics: teeth, not vacuity. (This plan decides it; the story does not.)

R5 fixes the absent case. The story never says what the criterion asserts when the section *is*
present. Two candidates were measured against the real evaluator:

- **(a) count any row, ship placeholder rows in the template** → the criterion passes on the
  unedited scaffold. That is a new instance of **BUG-054** ("most gate criteria pass on blank
  template") authored *during the sprint that filed BUG-054*. Rejected.
- **(b) ship the section with guidance prose and ZERO `- ` lines; require ≥1 `- [ ]`/`- [x]` row
  when present** → measured on a scratch story rendered from the mutated template:

  ```
  Gate: story.ready-for-execution (enforcing)
  ❌ existing-surfaces-verified: cited paths do not exist on disk: ./name.ext, path/to/file.ext
  ❌ prior-work-recorded: ## Prior work has no [[ID]] wikilink or "none found" sentinel
  ❌ task-breakdown-complete: ## Task Breakdown is present but carries no `- [ ]` task rows
  ```

**(b) is taken.** It is the `prior-work-recorded` shape exactly (present-but-unfilled fails, absent
passes), it joins two criteria that already behave this way rather than inventing a third
behaviour, and it is **satisfiable by following the template's own guidance** — write rows, or
delete the section at L1 per R3. That last clause is the direct answer to FLASHCARD 2026-08-27
`#gate #dx #danger` ("making a gate non-vacuous by counting a shape the template never emits makes
it non-SATISFIABLE by the same stroke"): here both escape routes are printed in the section's own
guidance block.

Two consequences the Developer must not "fix":

- The template's guidance block **must not contain a line-initial `- `**. It is written as a
  blockquote (`> `). A bullet there re-vacates the criterion. This is the measured second half of
  FLASHCARD 2026-08-27 `#gate #readiness-gates #danger` — `listed-item` is only safe paired with a
  zero-bullet section, and this repo has already broken that twice.
- The predicate counts `- [ ]` / `- [x]`, **not** `listed-item` and **not** `declared-item`.
  `declared-item` is forbidden here: `countDeclaredItems` (`:712`) scores bare bold labels and
  ordinary prose sentences (BUG-050 / FLASHCARD 2026-08-27 `#gate #readiness-gates #danger`).

### N3 — Heading title: `## Task Breakdown`, unnumbered. Nothing else.

`## 9. Task Breakdown` is the synthetic mutation heading S3a/S3b inject into an in-memory copy of
`CR.md` (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:566`) and S3b quotes it **verbatim** in its
expected message (`:588`). A real heading carrying that literal string makes S3b's assertion
ambiguous — it can no longer tell the synthetic insertion from the shipped one.

`## Task Breakdown` was chosen over a numbered form for a second, independent reason: the three
templates number their sections differently (`story.md` 1-4, `CR.md` 0.5-4, `Bug.md` 1-5). Any
number picked would be right in at most one file. The unnumbered form matches the four sections
that already do this — `## Existing Surfaces`, `## Prior work`, `## Why not simpler?`,
`## Context Source` — and `headingTitleOf` (`:459`) strips numeric prefixes anyway, so an author
who writes `## 3.5 Task Breakdown` in an instance still matches the predicate.

**Measured:** S3a and S3b both green with the real heading at CR position 7 and the title
unnumbered.

| Template | Insert immediately before | New position | Registry consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `.cleargate/templates/story.md` | `## 4. Quality Gates` (line **180**) | **4** | `story.dod-declared` `section(4)`→`section(5)` (`readiness-gates.md:149`) — the ONLY value that moves |
| `.cleargate/templates/CR.md` | `## 4. Verification Protocol` (line **113**) | **7** | none |
| `.cleargate/templates/Bug.md` | `## 5. Verification Protocol (The Failing Test)` (line **101**) | **5** | none |

One rule to remember it by: **immediately after the Execution Sandbox** in `CR.md` and `Bug.md`;
**immediately after the Implementation Guide** in `story.md` (which is that template's sandbox
equivalent). R4's "directly after §3 The Implementation Guide" is satisfied literally for

### N5 — CORPUS DISPOSITION: **ACCEPT.** Decided here, with the measurement that decides it.

The M1 post-flight measured the `section(4)`→`section(5)` bump as flipping **74 of 231** authored
stories PASS→FAIL, 0 FAIL→PASS, against an `enforcing` gate. **Re-measured here and confirmed
exactly**, using the real exported `evaluate()`:

```
authored STORY-*.md in pending-sync + archive:                  231
  already carrying a "## … Task Breakdown" heading:               0
  section(4) >=1 listed-item PASS (today):                    195/231
  section(5) >=1 listed-item PASS (post-054-06):              121/231
  cross-tab: pass→pass 121 · PASS→FAIL 74 · FAIL→PASS 0 · fail→fail 36
```

Two measurements the post-flight did not have, and which change the disposition from a judgement
call into an obvious one:

**(i) The item-level gate verdict does not move at all.** Evaluating the *complete*
`story.ready-for-execution` criteria list (all eleven today, all twelve post-change) over the same
231 files:

```
ITEM-LEVEL verdict — today PASS 65/231 · post-054-06 PASS 65/231
item-level flips: PASS→FAIL 0 · FAIL→PASS 0 · fail→fail 166
```

**Zero items change verdict.** The same 65 pass before and after.

**(ii) The mechanism is structural, and it is why (i) holds.** All 74 flipped items fail *other*
criteria today:

```
the flip set: 74; of these, already failing >=1 OTHER criterion today: 74
  74 reuse-audit-recorded          (no '## Existing Surfaces' section)
  74 simplest-form-justified       (no '## Why not simpler?' section)
   3 implementation-files-declared
   2 gherkin-present
   1 discovery-checked
```

Every one of the 74 is a **pre-EPIC-051 item that never had the two trailing sections** — which is
precisely *why* it has only four or five `## ` headings and *why* `section(5)` misses. The set of
items the bump reddens and the set already red are the same set, by construction.

Third fact: **72 of the 74 are in `archive/`. Only 2 are in `pending-sync/`** —
`STORY-031-01_Cli_Test_Runner_Split.md` and `STORY-031-02_QA_Scoped_Tests_Default.md`, both
EPIC-031, already the known accepted residue (M0 R1: EPIC-031 hard-fails `section 8 not found`;
Mid-Sprint Amendment `2026-08-27 · M0/R1`). Fourth: nothing bulk-re-gates the corpus —
`wiki lint` reads `cached_gate_result` from frontmatter (`lint-checks.ts:341`, `:361`, `:415`) and
never re-evaluates, and `validate_bounce_readiness.mjs` checks state + tree cleanliness only, not
gates. The failure materialises only when an item is individually re-gated.

**Ruling: ACCEPT. Do not migrate. Do not soften `dod-declared`. Do not revert the index.**
Migration would mean inserting a heading into 231 archived work items to preserve a positional
index — rewriting shipped history to flatter a gate. Rationale, in one line for the Developer:
*the bump changes zero item-level gate verdicts, and the 74 criterion-level flips land entirely on
items that already fail two other criteria for the same structural reason.*

Obligations that come with accepting:
- The Reporter records this residue in `SPRINT-39_REPORT.md` alongside EPIC-031's `section 8 not
  found`, with the item-level number (**0**), not just the criterion-level number (74).
- A Developer who runs `cleargate gate check` on an archived story mid-implementation and sees
  `dod-declared: section 5 not found` is seeing the accepted residue. It is **not** a defect and
  **not** a reason to touch the index. This sentence goes in the Developer dispatch verbatim.
- The general defect — positional gates are correct-at-authoring-time, not correct-forever
  (FLASHCARD 2026-08-27 `#gate #readiness-gates #danger`, BUG-042) — is **not** closed by this
  story and must not be attacked inside it. Candidate follow-on in §Open decisions.

### N6 — `dist/cli.js` MUST be rebuilt in the same turn as 054-06. Non-optional.

`.claude/hooks/stamp-and-gate.sh:12-13` resolves the CLI as **`${REPO_ROOT}/cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js`
when present**, ahead of any PATH or npx branch. That file exists and is dated **Aug 24** — built
before this sprint. It contains `prior-work-recorded`; it cannot contain `task-breakdown-complete`.

`gate.ts:238-242` wraps `evaluate()` in a `try` and converts a parse throw into
`{ pass: false, detail: "predicate error: …" }`. So the moment the *live* `readiness-gates.md`
carries the new criterion and the *stale* dist is still on disk, **every PostToolUse edit to any
`.cleargate/delivery/**/*.md` emits `⚠️ gate failed: <ID> — predicate error: unsupported predicate
shape: task-breakdown-complete`** — for the rest of the sprint, on every work item, including M3
and M4's. Not a crash, not caught by any test, and highly visible.

**Required step, same turn as the 054-06 commits:** `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run build`
(`rm -rf dist && tsup`). `dist/` is gitignored in the cli repo (`.gitignore:2`, verified with
`git check-ignore -v`), so this produces **zero commits**. Verify afterwards:
`grep -c task-breakdown-complete cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js` returns non-zero.

This is a rebuild, not a payload regeneration — Cross-Cutting Rule 2 is about
`cleargate-cli/templates/cleargate-planning/**` and is untouched by it.

### N7 — Commit ORDER for 054-06 is enforced by the outer pre-commit hook. Take advantage of it.

Measured. Two partial states exist and they are **not** symmetric:

- **cli code first, registry second → INERT.** The new parser branch is unreachable while no
  criterion names it. Full default suite: green.
- **registry first, cli code second → RED, in two default-tier tests.** Measured, exact messages:
  - `test/commands/gate-unit.node.test.ts` — *"all criterion check strings in readiness-gates.md
    parse via parsePredicate without throwing"* → `unsupported predicate shape: task-breakdown-complete`
  - `test/lib/readiness-predicates.node.test.ts` — *"every criterion.check parses via parsePredicate
    without throwing"* → same message

  Both read the **live** tree (`gate-unit.node.test.ts:735`:
  `path.join(GATE_TEST_REPO_ROOT, '.cleargate/knowledge/readiness-gates.md')`), which is the
  cross-branch coupling FLASHCARD 2026-08-27 `#test-harness #cross-repo #danger` describes.

Because `.cleargate/config.yml` `gates.precommit` runs `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run typecheck &&
npm --prefix cleargate-cli test`, an outer commit landing the registry **before** the cli code
**fails its own pre-commit hook**. The order is self-enforcing. Do not reach for `--no-verify`
(Cross-Cutting Rule 5); reorder instead.

This is the R19 shape resolved in the **opposite** direction from STORY-054-02: 054-02 had no inert
intermediate and needed both commits in one turn. **054-06 has one** — see its Commit plan.

### Execution route

**MAIN CHECKOUT on `sprint/S-39`. No worktree.** Three independent reasons, each sufficient:

1. `cleargate-cli/` has **0 tracked files in the outer repo** and therefore does not exist inside
   `.worktrees/*` (BUG-046 / FLASHCARD 2026-08-26 `#worktree #collision-surface #danger`). This
   story must edit cli source. A worktree cannot hold it.
2. The two cli tests that catch the registry/code ordering read the **live** meta tree via
   `GATE_TEST_REPO_ROOT`, which resolves to the main checkout — from a worktree they would judge
   the wrong tree.
3. `dist/cli.js` (N6) is built into the main checkout regardless of where the source edit happened.

**`cleargate-cli` IS involved, and cli commits are completely ungated.** `cleargate-cli/.git/hooks/`
contains only `*.sample` and no `core.hooksPath` is set — **zero** installed hooks (Cross-Cutting
Rule 6, BUG-053, FLASHCARD 2026-08-27 `#gate #cross-repo #danger`). The surface gate,
`check:no-vitest`, `check:no-inline-id-regex` and the typecheck/test pre-commit are all bypassed
silently, with nobody passing `--no-verify`. Therefore:

- The Developer runs `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run typecheck` and the full suite **by hand** and
  reports **both numbers** in the report.
- **QA-Verify must confirm the run happened** — it may not assume it. Evidence required: the two
  numbers in the Developer report AND `dist/cli.js` newer than the source edit (proof N6 ran).
- Never `cd cleargate-cli && npm test`. `npm --prefix`, always (FLASHCARD 2026-06-03
  `#gate #npm #workspace`).
- Targeted runs: `npm --prefix cleargate-cli exec -- tsx --test cleargate-cli/test/<path>`.
  `npm --prefix cleargate-cli test -- <file>` silently runs the whole 2493-test suite
  (`run-default-tests.mjs:23` hardcodes its glob and never reads `process.argv`).
- The full suite carries **1 pre-existing failure** — `test/commands/sync.node.test.ts`,
  `cannot reach https://cleargate-mcp.soula.ge (fetch failed)`. No outbound network in this sandbox.
  It fails identically on `main`. Not yours.

### Commit plan — exact. An inert intermediate EXISTS (unlike 054-02).

Everything is edited in the working tree first; then two commits, in this order:

**Commit A — `cleargate-cli` repo.** `feat(epic-054): STORY-054-06 task-breakdown-complete predicate`
- `src/lib/readiness-predicates.ts` (4 edits + docstring line 3)
- `test/lib/readiness-predicates-task-breakdown.red.node.test.ts` (promoted from red, `git mv` if
  QA-Red authored it under that name already)
- `test/lib/readiness-predicates-prior-work-ambiguity.node.test.ts` (prose de-staling)

Inert on its own: no criterion names the predicate yet, the new parser branch is unreachable, the
suite is green.

**Commit B — outer repo, `sprint/S-39`.** `feat(epic-054): STORY-054-06 Task Breakdown section in story/CR/Bug`
- 6 template files (3 live + 3 canonical, byte-identical pairs)
- 2 `readiness-gates.md` files (live + canonical, byte-identical)

Gated by the outer pre-commit hook, which runs the cli suite — and passes because Commit A already
landed. **If B is attempted first, its own hook fails** (N7). That is the design, not an obstacle.

**Between the working-tree edits and Commit A:** run typecheck + full suite once, with *both* halves
present in the working tree. That is the only state in which the suite means anything, and it is
the run whose numbers go in the report.

**Immediately after Commit B:** `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run build` (N6). Zero commits.

**State touch before Commit B** (the outer commit is surface-gated, and the gate resolves the
"active story" by `max(updated_at)` because its state filter is a dead branch — BUG-052):

```
CLEARGATE_STATE_FILE="$PWD/.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/state.json" \

```
Run from the **main checkout**. `run_script.sh` takes `<command> [args…]` so the interpreter is
