### Schema changes (verbatim — copy, do not paraphrase)

**(1) The section body, identical in all three templates.** Insert exactly this block, followed by
one blank line, immediately before the anchor line named in N4:

```markdown
## Task Breakdown

> **Required at L3 and above. Optional at L2. Omit the whole section at L1.**
> An absent section passes the gate; a section that is present but carries no task rows does not.
> Write one row per executable step, in execution order:
> `- [ ] <action>` with an optional trailing `-> <requirement-id>`. The requirement reference is
> reserved for grounding ids and is not interpreted today.
```

Invariants on that block — each is a QA kick-back criterion:
- **Zero line-initial `- `.** Every guidance line begins with `> `. A bullet here re-vacates the
  criterion (N2).
- **Zero `- [ ]` placeholder rows.** The teeth depend on the shipped section being row-free.
- **No `TBD` / `TODO` / `FIXME`** — `no-tbds` is a live criterion on all three types.
- **No literal `SPRINT-<digits>`** (Mid-Sprint Amendment `2026-08-27 · STORY-054-05/post-flight R12`
  guard note for M2). Pre-existing hits at `story.md` raw 28 and 45 are BUG-048's — do not touch.
- **Identical bytes in the live and canonical copies** (Cross-Cutting Rule 1).

**(2) One `<instructions>` line per template (R3).** These are inside `<instructions>`, which
`templateBodyOf` strips — they cannot shift any index (proved by 054-03's `story.md:27` edit,
M1 §C1).

- `story.md` — insert after line **6** (`§3 Implementation Guide: …`), before line 7:
  ```
  Task Breakdown: one `- [ ] <action>` row per executable step, in execution order. REQUIRED at L3 and above, optional at L2, omit the section entirely at L1. An absent section passes the gate; a present-but-empty one fails.
  ```
- `CR.md` — insert after line **7** (`§3 Execution Sandbox: …`), before line 8: same sentence.
- `Bug.md` — insert after line **7** (`§4 Execution Sandbox: …`), before line 8: same sentence.

Do **not** edit `CR.md:2` ("Output sections in order 1-4") or `Bug.md:2` ("order 1-5"). The new
section is unnumbered and those lines still describe the numbered ones.

**(3) `readiness-gates.md` — both trees, byte-identical.**

Registry edit 1, at `:148-149`:
```yaml
    - id: dod-declared
      check: "section(5) has ≥1 listed-item"     # was section(4)
```

Registry edit 2 — append to the `story`, `cr` and `bug` blocks (after `ambiguity-gate-resolved` in
each):
```yaml
    - id: task-breakdown-complete
      check: "task-breakdown-complete"
```
Block boundaries: `story.ready-for-execution` ends at `:165`, `cr.ready-to-apply` at `:190`,
`bug.ready-for-fix` begins at `:192`. Three blocks, three identical two-line additions.

Registry edit 3 — Predicate Vocabulary. `:8` "There are exactly **9 predicate shapes**." → **10**.
Then, immediately before the `---` that precedes `## Severity Model`:
```markdown
**10. `task-breakdown-complete`**
Closed-set predicate (no parameters). Locates the `## Task Breakdown` section by heading title
(numeric prefixes tolerated). Passes when the section is absent — every item authored before this
criterion existed lacks it, and an L1 item omits it deliberately. When present, passes only if it
carries at least one task row (`- [ ]` or `- [x]`); a present-but-row-free section fails, naming
the gap. The optional trailing `-> <requirement-id>` on a row is accepted and not otherwise
interpreted. Example: `task-breakdown-complete` against a Story whose `## Task Breakdown` reads
`- [ ] add the predicate branch -> R5` passes; the unedited template scaffold fails.
```

**(4) `readiness-predicates.ts` — the evaluator, verbatim.** Append at end of file:

```ts
// ─── Task-breakdown-complete evaluator ────────────────────────────────────────

/**
 * STORY-054-06: Verify that a `## Task Breakdown` section, when present, carries at
 * least one task row. Migration grace: section entirely absent → pass (every item
 * authored before this criterion existed lacks it, and R3 says an L1 item omits it
 * deliberately). Present but row-free → fail — this is the teeth: the scaffolded
 * template body deliberately ships zero rows, so an unfilled freshly-authored item
 * fails until the author writes the sequence or deletes the section.
 *
 * Counts `- [ ]` / `- [x]` rows only — NOT `listed-item` and NOT `declared-item`.
 * countDeclaredItems (L712) scores bare bold labels and ordinary prose (BUG-050),
 * and `listed-item` would be satisfied by the guidance block if it ever grew a
 * bullet (BUG-054). A checkbox row is the one shape the guidance cannot accidentally
 * emit.
 *
 * Section locator clones evalPriorWorkRecorded's heading-anchored pattern
 * (`body.split(/^(?=## )/m)` + `headingTitleOf` exact match). headingTitleOf strips
 * numeric prefixes, so `## 3.5 Task Breakdown` in an authored instance still matches.
 */
function evalTaskBreakdownComplete(doc: ParsedDoc): { pass: boolean; detail: string } {
  const rawParts = doc.body.split(/^(?=## )/m);
  let sectionContent: string | undefined;
  for (const part of rawParts) {
    const firstLine = part.split('\n')[0] ?? '';
    if (headingTitleOf(firstLine) === 'Task Breakdown') {
      sectionContent = part;
      break;
    }
  }

  if (!sectionContent) {
    return {
      pass: true,
      detail: `not-applicable: ## Task Breakdown section absent`,
    };
  }

  const rows = (sectionContent.match(/^\s*- \[[ xX]\]/gm) || []).length;
  if (rows === 0) {
    return {
      pass: false,
      detail: '## Task Breakdown is present but carries no `- [ ]` task rows',
    };
  }

  return {
    pass: true,
    detail: `## Task Breakdown has ${rows} task row${rows === 1 ? '' : 's'}`,
  };
}
```

Plus the three one-line edits (union member, parser branch, switch case) named in N1, and
`readiness-predicates.ts:3` — "Supports exactly 6 closed-set predicate shapes" → **10**. That
docstring is already stale by four; fix it while the file is open. It does not touch `:640/:646/:648`,
so S4 stays green (measured).

### Test shape — a red baseline IS authorable. Dispatch QA-Red.

Unlike STORY-054-03 (0 unit tests / 3 grep assertions → QA-Red correctly skipped) and STORY-054-01
(structural assertions only → skipped), **054-06 has a real code contract with a real red window.**
Written against the registry-free state, `evaluate('task-breakdown-complete', …)` throws
`unsupported predicate shape: task-breakdown-complete` from `parsePredicate` (`:135`), so every
scenario fails on the first line — a genuine, non-vacuous red.

**File:** `cleargate-cli/test/lib/readiness-predicates-task-breakdown.red.node.test.ts`
(promoted by `git mv` to `…-task-breakdown.node.test.ts` at green, or kept — the sprint's other

| # | Test | Why |

It is the only assertion that fails if someone later adds a `- [ ]` example row to the shipped guidance. Read canonical, not live — canonical is the tracked source of truth (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:60-70`). |

§4.1's regression row ("STORY-054-05's pinning test green") is satisfied by running that file, not
by writing a new one.

**Red-baseline acceptance:** every scenario fails, and it must fail with
`unsupported predicate shape`, not with a fixture-guard error. FLASHCARD 2026-08-27
`#test-harness #tpv #danger` — a red test that fails a guard first leaves its helpers unexecuted, so
the baseline certifies scenario count and nothing else. Here the throw is at the assertion line, so
every helper runs. TPV should confirm that specific fact rather than the generic one.

**TPV:** dispatch only if the `pre_gate_runner.sh` wiring scan flags. If dispatched, the wiring
checks that matter are: the import path `../../src/lib/readiness-predicates.js` resolves, the
`ParsedDoc` shape is `{ fm, body, absPath }` (`:25-29`), and the canonical-template reader in

fixture' over-instructs: it sends a Developer into the fixture with nothing to change, one keystroke
from the tampering the rule forbids."* Cross-Cutting Rule 4's "AND update the fixture" clause does
**not** apply to this story. **Measured: S1b green with the fixture untouched.**

6. The shipped `## Task Breakdown` block contains a line-initial `- ` in any of the six template

7. `task-breakdown-complete` does not return `pass: false` when evaluated against the canonical
   `story.md` body. That is the non-vacuity pin; a green here means the criterion certifies nothing.

9. Developer report omits either the typecheck number or the suite numbers. Cli commits are
   ungated; the numbers are the only evidence that anything ran. **QA must confirm the run
   happened — a green claim without both numbers is a kick-back, not a pass.**

11. `readiness-predicates-prior-work-ambiguity.node.test.ts` is untouched (the four stale sites).

    migrate.

14. A new `SPRINT-<digits>` literal appears in any prose added to the three templates.

**QA-Verify must read the `readiness-gates.md` diff, not only the test result.** Nine of the twelve
pinnable fixture rows are single-witness, `story.dod-declared` among them — editing a fixture row to
match a drifted tree goes green (sprint-context, 054-05 post-flight).

### Gotchas surfaced from code inspection

- `gate.ts:238-242` catches predicate throws and converts them to a failing criterion. A registry
  criterion with no parser branch is therefore a **silent hard-fail on every item of that type**,
  not a crash. This is the mechanism behind N6 and N7.
- `enumerateSectionCriteria` (`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:188-195`) swallows
  `parsePredicate` throws with `continue`. So the *pinning* test would stay green under a
  registry-without-code state — it is `gate-unit` and `readiness-predicates` that catch it. Two
  tests reading the same document with opposite error policies.
- `templateBodyOf` (`:210-227`) locates the frontmatter by its `---` delimiters, not by byte offset,
  so it strips `<instructions>` **and** frontmatter in one step. That is why R3's `<instructions>`
  lines are index-free.
- `headingTitleOf` (`:459-470`) strips numeric prefixes, so `## 3.5 Task Breakdown` in an authored
  instance matches the predicate — but the match is **exact equality** on the remainder, so
  `## Task Breakdown (optional)` does **not**. Same limitation `prior-work-recorded` has.
- `story.md:8` teaches the same `{TYPE}-{ID}-{Name}.md` filename shape BUG-057 filed against
  `CLAUDE.md:162`. It is in a file this story opens. **Do not fix it here** — it is BUG-057's, and
  taking it would widen an already-12-file surface.
- `.cleargate/templates/story.md` is already stale in the npm payload (canonical ≠ payload today,
  from 054-03). No test sees it; Gate-4 obligation #2 owns it.

## STORY-054-07 — Architect writes the tasks, Developer ticks them, QA verifies

**Goal linkage:** completes goal clause 2. 054-06 makes the section *exist*; 054-07 makes it
*written and checked by the loop rather than by author diligence* (§1.1). Without it the sprint
ships a section that no agent is told to fill — the exact failure mode M1 hit at `CLAUDE.md:161`
and that STORY-054-03's `## Guidance Surface Reach` doctrine names.

**Lane:** `standard` (unchanged). Rubric check 1 (3 files + 3 mirrors) and check 4 (four Gherkin
scenarios) both fail fast-eligibility.

### §3.1 STATUS: **INCOMPLETE, and inverted.** Two problems, one of them measured-red.

**Problem 1 — the Primary/Mirror labelling is backwards, and the story says so itself.** §3.1 names
`.claude/agents/developer.md` (the **live, fully untracked** tree — CR-099) as "Primary File" and
`cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/*.md` as "Mirrors". §3.2 states the correct relationship in
prose: *"the canonical mirror is the tracked source and the live copy is re-synced separately — a
story that edits only the live tree leaves nothing in the commit."* The prose is right; the table
is misleading. Verified: `git ls-files .claude/` returns zero; all three live/canonical pairs are
byte-identical today (`diff -q` silent on all three).

**Problem 2 — one required path is entirely absent, and omitting it turns the cli suite RED.**
Measured, and this is the finding that most needs to reach the Developer:

> `cleargate-cli/test/docs/readme-qa-doc-truth-043-06.red.node.test.ts` — a **default-tier** test —
> asserts that **canonical `qa.md` and the npm-payload `qa.md` are byte-identical**
> (`:71` `PAYLOAD_QA_MD`, `:278` existence, `:283-290` s5_b byte compare, `:304-315` s6 `diff -q`).
> Editing `cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/qa.md` without refreshing the payload produces
> **2 failures**: `s5_b: canonical qa.md and payload qa.md are byte-identical` and
> `s6: diff -q canonical vs payload qa.md exits 0`.

Measured both ways: with the canonical edit and a stale payload → 2 new failures;
after `node scripts/copy-planning-payload.mjs` → that file runs **18 pass / 0 fail / 0 skipped**.
And the asymmetry is real — `architect.md` and `developer.md` have **no** payload-parity assertion,
and neither do any of 054-06's four canonical files. `qa.md` alone is pinned.

**This collides with Cross-Cutting Rule 2** ("payload regeneration is a Gate-4/close step, not a
per-story one"). Resolution, ruled here: **run `node scripts/copy-planning-payload.mjs` from
`cleargate-cli/` in the same turn — not `npm run prebuild`.** Reasons:
- The copier writes only into `cleargate-cli/templates/cleargate-planning/`
  (`copy-planning-payload.mjs:21-31`), which is gitignored in the cli repo
  (`.gitignore:5`, verified with `git check-ignore -v`). **Zero commits.** Rule 2 forbids
  hand-editing and committing the payload; regenerating it commits nothing and hand-edits nothing.
- `npm run prebuild` also runs `build-manifest.ts`, which rewrites the **tracked**
  `cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json`. That is a DevOps post-merge action, not a Developer one.
  Running the copier alone avoids the side effect.
- The alternative — leave the suite red until Gate 4 — is not available. A story cannot land red.

**Third landmine on the same path:** `copy-planning-payload.mjs` derives its source as
`path.resolve(pkgRoot, '..')/cleargate-planning` — the **main checkout**, always. Run from or after
a worktree edit, it copies the unmodified main-checkout canonical and the parity failure persists
(or, worse, passes against content nobody edited). This is the second independent reason 054-07 runs
on the main checkout.

**Required §3.1 amendment (orchestrator, before cutting wave7):**
- Relabel: Primary File → `cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/developer.md`; Related →
  `cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/{architect,qa}.md`; and name the three live paths as
  *same-turn hand-port, untracked, contributes nothing to the commit*.
- Add `cleargate-cli/templates/cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/qa.md` as **generated — refreshed
  by `copy-planning-payload.mjs`, never hand-edited, never committed.**
- `cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json` — three rows change SHA: `:20` (architect.md), `:55`
  (developer.md), `:69` (qa.md). **DevOps post-merge**, deliberately not in §3.1.

Same commit-before-cut rule as 054-06 (`file_surface_diff.sh:150-157`).

### Reach audit — checked, and the answer is that three files is enough

M1's C2 lesson (a type registered in one list and missing from the next one down) and FLASHCARD
2026-08-28 `#doctrine #gate` say to grep every list that enumerates the *other* agents, not just the
one the requirement names. Done:

- `git grep "Per-story blueprint" cleargate-planning/.claude/` → 3 hits: `architect.md:44`, `:64`,
  `:136`, plus `skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md:229`.
- `architect-synth.md` and `architect-reader.md` exist and are dispatched for N > 2 stories — but
  they produce the **Sprint Design Review §§2.1-2.5**, not the per-story milestone blueprint. The
  milestone plan (this document's own shape) is produced by `architect.md`. **They are correctly out
  of scope.** Recorded so nobody re-opens it.
- `SKILL.md:229` already says *"The plan should explicitly tie each story to the goal under
  'Per-story blueprint'"* — it references the section, does not enumerate its contents, and needs no
  edit. **Ruled out of scope**; CR-111 (wave13) already owns a SKILL.md §C.3 edit and can carry any
  later need.
- `devops.md` and `reporter.md`: neither reads nor writes story-file sections. Out of scope.

### Execution route

**MAIN CHECKOUT on `sprint/S-39`. No worktree.** Because (a) the payload copier resolves the main
checkout regardless of where the edit was made, (b) the live `.claude/` hand-port is a
main-checkout-only artifact (untracked, per-machine), and (c) the story's own §1.5 mitigation
("the Architect runs before worktree cut") is about story files, not about this story's own route.

**`cleargate-cli` IS involved — but with ZERO cli commits.** The only cli-side action is running
`copy-planning-payload.mjs`, which writes a gitignored build artifact. Nothing is staged and nothing
is committed in that repo. Cross-Cutting Rule 6's ungated-commit hazard therefore does **not** apply
to 054-07 — there are no cli commits to be ungated. What does apply: the **cli test suite must be
run by hand after the copier**, and QA-Verify must confirm the numbers, because no hook will.

**Live-tree hand-port is REQUIRED, in the same turn.** All three live/canonical pairs are identical
today; editing only canonical makes them drift. The live tree is untracked (`git ls-files .claude/`
= 0), so the hand-port cannot appear in the commit and cannot be staged — `git status` stays silent
either way. Doing it now, rather than deferring to Gate 4, closes the exact hole M1's Gate-4 item #1
describes for SKILL.md, where **two independent blind spots** (integration-tier tests excluded from
the default suite, plus a fully untracked tree) meant nothing would ever surface the drift.

Second-order effect, accepted: the QA agent dispatched *after* this story merges will read the new
advisory contract and may report unchecked Task Breakdown rows on subsequent stories. That is
correct dogfood behaviour and it is advisory (R3) — it flags, it does not bounce. M3/M4 items are
already authored without the section, so in practice there is nothing to flag.

### Commit plan — one commit, and an inert intermediate is irrelevant

**Single commit, outer repo, `sprint/S-39`:**
`feat(epic-054): STORY-054-07 wire architect/developer/QA to Task Breakdown`
- `cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/architect.md`
- `cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/developer.md`
- `cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/qa.md`

That is the whole tracked diff — three files. The three live files are edited in the same turn and
contribute nothing to the commit (untracked). The payload refresh contributes nothing (gitignored).

There is **no intermediate state to sequence**: unlike 054-06 this story spans one repo and one
commit. The question "is there an inert intermediate?" has the answer *not applicable* — say it
plainly so nobody constructs a two-commit dance.

**Order within the turn:** edit canonical → hand-port to live → run
`node scripts/copy-planning-payload.mjs` from `cleargate-cli/` → run the cli suite → state touch →
commit. The copier must run **before** the suite, or the qa.md parity assertion is red.

**State touch before the commit** (same reason and same form as 054-06):
```
CLEARGATE_STATE_FILE="$PWD/.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/state.json" \
  node .cleargate/scripts/update_state.mjs STORY-054-07 Bouncing
```

### Schema changes (verbatim placement)

R5 is binding: each rule goes into the agent's own `.md`, never into a template `<instructions>`
block. Placement is chosen to require **zero renumbering** of any existing list — a renumbered step
is a diff no reviewer can scan.

**(1) `architect.md`.** R1 says "at the 'Per-story blueprint' section". The literal `## Per-story
blueprint` heading at `:44` sits **inside the fenced plan template** the Architect copies — an
instruction added there would be copied into every plan. Insert instead immediately **after** the
closing fence and immediately **before** `5. **Record flashcards on any gotcha…`:

```markdown
**Task Breakdown (EPIC-054 WS7).** In addition to the `## Per-story blueprint` section above,
write the same task rows into each story file's own `## Task Breakdown` section — one
`- [ ] <action>` row per executable step, in execution order, optionally suffixed
`-> <requirement-id>`. Do this **before the worktree is cut**: the story file is a work item, and
an uncommitted amendment never reaches the surface gate, which reads the worktree's copy as
primary. A story below L3 that carries no `## Task Breakdown` section needs no rows — an absent
section is valid and passes the gate.
```

**(2) `developer.md`.** Insert immediately before `## Output shape` (`:48`):

```markdown
**Task Breakdown (EPIC-054 WS7).** If the story file carries a `## Task Breakdown` section, tick
each `- [ ]` row to `- [x]` as you complete it, in the same commit as the work it describes. If a
row is dropped, write one sentence in that section saying why, rather than leaving it silently
unchecked. **You are the box-ticking actor** — no other agent ticks these rows, and QA will report
any that stay unchecked.
```

The bolded clause is R2's own emphasis: *"omitting it is the failure mode, because QA would
otherwise flag stories for unchecked boxes no agent was told to check."*

**(3) `qa.md`.** Insert inside the `## Mode Dispatch — Red vs Verify` section, immediately before
`**Mode: CONSOLIDATION**`, so it lands in the VERIFY block:

```markdown
**Task Breakdown (EPIC-054 WS7).** In VERIFY mode, if the story file carries a `## Task Breakdown`
section, assert that every `- [ ]` row is checked or that the story states why the row was dropped.
**Advisory in v1** — report unchecked rows in your findings; do not bounce the story on them alone.
```

All three paragraphs land in **both** trees, byte-identical.

### Test shape — **no red baseline is authorable. Do NOT dispatch QA-Red.**

§4.1 asks for **0 unit tests**, 3 grep assertions and 3 parity assertions. §3.1 says New Files
Needed: No. This is the STORY-054-03 shape exactly, and M1 ruled it correctly there
(Mid-Sprint Amendment `2026-08-27 · M1/wave3 · ORCHESTRATOR DECISION — QA-Red SKIPPED for
STORY-054-01`): dispatching QA-Red would either produce nothing or produce a file the plan forbids.

**Ruling: QA-Red is skipped for 054-07.** Developer is dispatched directly; the six assertions are
executed by QA-Verify against the kick-back list below. Stated explicitly so the orchestrator does
not spend a dispatch on an agent with nothing to write.

The six assertions, as commands:

```
# 3 grep assertions (§2.2 verification step 1)
grep -n "Task Breakdown" cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/architect.md
grep -n "Task Breakdown" cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/developer.md
grep -n "Task Breakdown" cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/qa.md

# 3 parity assertions (§2.2 verification step 2) — each must be silent
diff cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/architect.md .claude/agents/architect.md
diff cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/developer.md .claude/agents/developer.md
diff cleargate-planning/.claude/agents/qa.md        .claude/agents/qa.md
```

**These three `diff`s are legitimately byte-identical pairs** — unlike the `CLAUDE.md` pair, where
the M1 ORCHESTRATOR CORRECTION established that canonical (64 lines, the injection spec) and root
(186 lines, this repo's own document) have never been and cannot be identical. Agent files have no
injection semantics: canonical is copied verbatim into `.claude/agents/` by `cleargate init`. Byte
equality is the correct expectation here and it holds today on all three pairs.

Plus one assertion the story does not name and this plan requires:

```
npm --prefix cleargate-cli exec -- tsx --test cleargate-cli/test/docs/readme-qa-doc-truth-043-06.red.node.test.ts
# acceptance: pass 18 · fail 0 · skipped 0
```

Three numbers, not one — a `skip:`-guarded test reports SKIPPED, never FAILED, when a root resolves
wrongly, so "fail 0" alone is satisfied by a run that asserted nothing (FLASHCARD 2026-08-27
`#test-harness #gate #danger`, STORY-054-04 P6).

**Gherkin coverage.** All four §2.1 scenarios are prose-contract scenarios, verified by inspection,
not by execution:

| # | Scenario | Verified by |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The Architect populates the section | `architect.md` paragraph present + names `## Task Breakdown` + names the pre-worktree timing |
| 2 | The Developer ticks its own rows | `developer.md` paragraph present + names `- [x]` + asserts sole-actor |
| 3 | QA flags an unchecked row | `qa.md` paragraph present + carries the literal word **advisory** + explicitly says *do not bounce* |
| 4 | Error — a rule placed only in the template | **Negative assertion**, and the one QA is most likely to skip: `grep -n "Task Breakdown" .cleargate/templates/*.md` must return **only** the R3 `<instructions>` line and the section heading from 054-06 — and **no agent-behaviour rule**. If a ticking/flagging instruction appears in any template, scenario 4 has been violated by the fix itself. |

### QA kick-back criteria (specific)

1. Any of the three `diff` commands above is non-silent — canonical and live drifted.
2. `readme-qa-doc-truth-043-06.red.node.test.ts` is not `18 pass · 0 fail · 0 skipped`. Means the
   payload copier was skipped or run from the wrong root.
3. `cleargate-cli/templates/cleargate-planning/**` appears in **any** commit, in either repo.
4. `cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json` was regenerated by the Developer (i.e. `npm run prebuild` was
   run instead of the copier). MANIFEST is DevOps post-merge.
5. The `qa.md` paragraph omits the literal word **advisory**, or fails to say it does not bounce.
   R3 and §1.4's human decision are explicit; a blocking check is out of scope.
6. The `developer.md` paragraph does not identify the Developer as the sole ticking actor. R2 names
   this as *the* failure mode.
7. The `architect.md` paragraph does not state the pre-worktree-cut timing (§1.5's own mitigation).
8. A ticking or flagging rule appears in any `<instructions>` block of any template — §2.1
   scenario 4 inverted, and R5 violated.
9. Any new `## ` heading was added to any agent file. Paragraphs only.
10. A commit contains anything under `.claude/` (live). It is untracked; a staged live file means
    someone forced it.
11. The cli suite was not run after the copier, or its numbers are absent from the report.

## Cross-story risks

1. **wave6 → wave7 is a hard sequence, and the coupling is semantic, not textual.** 054-07's three
   paragraphs name `## Task Breakdown` and `- [ ]` rows. Nothing breaks if 054-07 lands first —
   which is precisely the danger: the contracts would be green, greppable, and describe a section
   that does not exist. `waves.json:122` is right that 07 "cannot be verified until 06's section
   exists". **Merge 054-06 first; do not reorder for convenience.**

2. **The `## Task Breakdown` string must match across the two stories exactly.** 054-06 ships
   `## Task Breakdown` (N3). 054-07's paragraphs must quote that string verbatim. A `## Task
   breakdown` or `## Tasks` in either half silently decouples the predicate from the contract —
   `headingTitleOf` matching is exact-equality after numeric-prefix stripping.

3. **054-07's Architect contract writes into story files, which are gate-checked on every edit.**
   A future Architect adding `## Task Breakdown` to a story file triggers the PostToolUse hook →
   `gate check` → `task-breakdown-complete`. If the Architect writes the heading and no rows, the
   story's own gate goes red. **The paragraph must therefore say "write the rows", never "add the
   section" —** the wording in §Schema does. Verified safe against the surface gate:
   `file_surface_diff.sh:179-188` ranges `### 3.1` → next `### `, so a new H2 anywhere in a story
   file cannot break §3.1 extraction.

4. **Neither story writes task rows into its own story file this sprint.** 054-06's and 054-07's
   own files have no `## Task Breakdown` section, the template does not yet ship one when they are
   dispatched, and adding one mid-flight would change their own gate results while they are in
   flight. The contract takes effect at the **next** sprint's Architect dispatch. This plan's
   per-story `### Task rows` lists are the transitional form.

5. **CR-111 (wave13, M4) collides with BOTH M2 stories.** Its `## 3. Execution Sandbox` names
   `.cleargate/templates/{story,CR,Bug}.md`, `readiness-gates.md`, `.claude/agents/developer.md`,
   `.claude/agents/qa.md` and their mirrors — the union of 054-06's and 054-07's surfaces. CR-111's
   own §3 already records the ordering (*"after BUG-042 → 054-05 → 054-02 → 054-06 on
   readiness-gates.md, and after 054-06 → CR-108 on the three templates. This CR is the last
   template edit in the sprint"*). Two obligations M2 creates for it, which its author cannot know
   yet: **(a)** if `test-layers-declared` is a `section(N)` criterion, it must use the POST-054-06
   positions in N4's table, not today's; **(b)** its developer.md/qa.md edits must be additive to
   054-07's paragraphs, not a rewrite of the surrounding block.

6. **CR-108 (wave12) generalises the work-item scaffold to all types.** It will render `story.md`,
   `CR.md` and `Bug.md` — which, after 054-06, ship a section that **fails an enforcing gate until
   filled**. If CR-108 strips `<instructions>` on render (its own open design point, per Mid-Sprint
   Amendment `2026-08-27 · STORY-054-05/post-flight R12`), the R3 guidance line disappears from the
   rendered instance and the author never learns the L3/L2/L1 rule or the delete-at-L1 escape. The
   guidance block inside the **section body** survives stripping — which is why N2 puts it there and
   not only in `<instructions>`. Flag to CR-108's planner.

7. **BUG-057's 24 non-conforming filenames are inert against M2** — one line, per instruction: M2
   touches templates, the gate registry, one predicate and three agent prompts; nothing in that set
   reads a filename, calls `deriveBucket`, or resolves a work-item id. **No interaction.** (Checked,
   not assumed: `deriveBucket` is reached only from the wiki path; the one id-resolving predicate,
   `frontmatter(<ref>)`, is not added or modified by either story.) `story.md:8` teaches the same
   hyphen shape `CLAUDE.md:162` does and is in a file 054-06 opens — **still BUG-057's, still out
   of scope**; taking it would widen a 12-file surface for a defect already filed.

8. **The whole sprint runs on a stale `dist/cli.js` until N6 executes.** Every M3/M4 wave after
   wave6 uses the hooks, which prefer that binary. If N6 is skipped, every subsequent work-item edit
   in the sprint emits a spurious `⚠️ gate failed` line. QA-Verify's kick-back #10 is the guard.

## Open decisions for orchestrator

1. **`story.md` placement is R4-mandated, and a cheaper alternative exists that I am not taking
   unilaterally.** Placing the section immediately **after** `## 4. Quality Gates` (position 5)
   instead of before it would cost **zero** registry edits, **zero** corpus effect and **zero**
   pinning-test involvement. It is not the "append below the ambiguity gate" form the human
   rejected at Gate 1 (§1.4) — it would sit above `## Existing Surfaces`, `## Prior work`,
   `## Why not simpler?` and the Ambiguity Gate. But R4 says *"directly after §3 The Implementation
   Guide"*, and that is approved scope; changing it is a `CR:scope-change` and therefore yours, not
   mine. **The plan proceeds on R4 as written** (N4), and N5's measurement — zero item-level verdict
   flips — is why I did not escalate this as a blocker. Raised only because it is a one-line
   decision that removes the sprint's last index movement. Answer "keep R4" and nothing changes.

2. **Cross-Cutting Rule 2 gets a narrow, measured exception in wave7.** 054-07 must run
   `copy-planning-payload.mjs` or land the suite 2-red. I have ruled it compliant (regeneration is
   neither hand-editing nor committing, and the artifact is gitignored) and written it into the
   commit plan. If you read Rule 2 as forbidding *any* payload touch before Gate 4, the only other
   route is to accept 2 red tests through the wave and regenerate at close — which I do not
   recommend and have not planned for. **Confirm the exception, or say so and I will amend.**
   If confirmed, this wants a Mid-Sprint Amendment line; I will append it on your word, not before.

3. **`task-breakdown-complete` severity is inherited, not chosen, and nobody has decided it.**
   It joins three blocks that are all `severity: enforcing`. R5 and §2.1 scenario 3 only require
   absence-tolerance; they say nothing about severity. Consequence, measured: from the moment
   054-06 merges, **every newly template-authored Story, CR or Bug hard-fails its readiness gate
   until the author writes rows or deletes the section**. That is N2's intended teeth and it matches
   `existing-surfaces-verified` / `prior-work-recorded`, which already behave this way in the same
   blocks — but M1 shipped the spike gate deliberately **advisory** (`lint-checks.ts:303
   ENFORCING_TYPES` was explicitly not extended, sprint-context), so the sprint has a precedent
   pointing the other way. **The plan proceeds with enforcing** (inheriting the block, adding no new
   severity mechanism — BUG-056 records that severity has no machine witness at all, so a per-
   criterion severity does not exist to be set). Flagged because it is the one behaviour change a
   human will notice the day after this ships.

4. **The `positional gate` class defect stays open and wants a home.** N5 accepts the residue;
   it does not fix the reason a heading insertion can invalidate an index at all. The durable fix is
   to re-express `dod-declared` (and the other eight already-vacuous positional criteria) as
   heading-anchored named predicates — the shape `existing-surfaces-verified` already uses.
   Out of scope here: `evalSection` is frozen sprint-wide, and it is a registry-wide refactor, not a
   line in this story. **Candidate follow-on CR for the sprint report**, alongside BUG-054 and the
   already-recorded S8 vocabulary-example pin.

5. **`test_template_gate_correctness.red.sh` T2-D's label is stale after this story** — it reads
   "(section 4 unshifted)" while the criterion becomes `section(5)`. The assertion is correct and
   measured green; only the parenthetical is dated. **Ruled out of scope** to keep the surface at
   twelve files. It is exactly the class of stale prose this sprint exists to remove, so it belongs
   in the sprint report as residue with a named owner, not as silent debt.

No repo write was performed. Reproduction (read-only against the real tree):

```bash

S=/tmp/m2-measure; R=/Users/ssuladze/Documents/Dev/ClearGate
mkdir -p "$S/cleargate-cli"
cp -R "$R/cleargate-cli"/{src,test,scripts} "$S/cleargate-cli/"
cp "$R/cleargate-cli"/{package.json,tsconfig.json,tsup.config.ts} "$S/cleargate-cli/"
ln -s "$R/cleargate-cli/node_modules" "$S/cleargate-cli/node_modules"
cp -R "$R/cleargate-cli/templates" "$S/cleargate-cli/templates"   # copy, NEVER symlink —
                                                                  # copy-planning-payload.mjs rm -rf's its dst

cp "$R/CLAUDE.md" "$R/README.md" "$S/"

# 2. baseline
cd "$S/cleargate-cli" && npm test > /tmp/base.txt 2>&1     # 2295 pass / 198 fail (scratch noise)

# 3. apply N1-N4 to $S only, then
npm test > /tmp/mut.txt 2>&1                               # 2301 pass / 192 fail — zero NEW failures

# 4. differential
awk '/^test at /{f=$3} /^✖ /{sub(/ \([0-9.]+ms\)$/,""); print f" :: "$0}' /tmp/base.txt | sort -u > /tmp/b
awk '/^test at /{f=$3} /^✖ /{sub(/ \([0-9.]+ms\)$/,""); print f" :: "$0}' /tmp/mut.txt  | sort -u > /tmp/m
comm -13 /tmp/b /tmp/m                                     # empty

  full criteria list before deciding accept-vs-migrate. 74/231 criterion flips here produced **0**

  `.cleargate/template` have no such pin. One file in eleven is load-bearing.

  Adding a predicate to `readiness-gates.md` without rebuilding dist makes every work-item edit in
  the repo emit `predicate error: unsupported predicate shape` — `gate.ts` catches the throw, so it
  is a silent failing criterion, not a crash, and no test sees it.

  the criterion, and S3a/S3b red on *finding-count pollution* with a message that reads like a
  `CR.md` problem. Measured; a plan naming only S1b sends the Developer to the wrong file.

> **TPV RULING — STORY-054-06** (Architect, 2026-08-28, against QA-Red commit `c9d44ba`)
>
> Verdict `rulings-required`. All five wiring checks pass — the red file does **not** go back to
> QA-Red. Every claim below was measured in an out-of-tree scratch mirror and re-run; the real
> repos were only read. Full evidence: `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/STORY-054-06-tpv.md`.
>
> **These ten rulings bind the Developer and supersede this plan where they conflict.**
>
> **A. Two corrections to this plan's own measurements.**
>
> **A1 — §N7 / §Commit plan: "Commit A is inert" is NO LONGER TRUE.** It was measured before
> QA-Red's file existed. With `c9d44ba` on the branch, cli code alone (predicate present,
> templates untouched) reds **Scenario 7** — the non-vacuity pin — because canonical `story.md`
> has no `## Task Breakdown` yet, so the predicate correctly returns `pass:true` on absence and
> the assertion demands `pass:false`. Measured twice: 7 pass / 1 fail. There is now **no**
> intermediate at which either repo alone is green. Order is still cli-first (registry-first
> hard-fails the outer pre-commit — that half of N7 stands), but "inert intermediate" is no longer
> a safety property. **054-06 is now the same R19 shape as 054-02: both commits in ONE turn, one
> suite run after.**
>
> **A2 — §N7's registry-first red set is 5 tests, not 2.** Measured, registry edited with no
> templates and no cli code: `gate-unit` *"all criterion check strings … parse"* (1),
> `readiness-predicates` *"every criterion.check parses"* (1), **plus pinning S1b, S3a and S3b**
> (3). S3a/S3b's messages name `CR.md`; the fix is the `story.md` `dod-declared` bump. This plan's
> §Blast radius already warns of that misread for the forgotten-bump case — it applies to the
> ordering case too. Full per-state table in the TPV report §T5.
>
> **B. The red baseline is honest but incomplete — three additive test rulings.**
>
> Applying §Schema changes (4) verbatim out-of-tree flips all six reds green (8/8). An 8-mutant
> battery killed six mutants; **two survive all eight assertions**, and both are properties this
> plan wrote normative rulings about:
>
> - **`listed-item` instead of checkbox rows survives** (`/^\s*- /gm` in place of
>   `/^\s*- \[[ xX]\]/gm`) — the exact implementation **§N2 forbids** on BUG-054 grounds. Every
>   must-fail body in the file has zero bullets of any kind and every must-pass body has a
>   checkbox row, so the two counters are indistinguishable.
> - **A literal `firstLine.trim() === '## Task Breakdown'` compare survives** — no body uses a
>   numeric-prefixed heading, so the "numeric prefixes tolerated" promise in **registry edit 3's
>   vocabulary entry**, the `evalTaskBreakdownComplete` docstring, and §Gotchas is unpinned.
>
> **R1.** Add one `it()`: `## Task Breakdown` + `- a plain bullet` + **no** `- [ ]`/`- [x]` →
> `pass:false`, `detail` matching `/no .* task rows/`. Red-phase-valid (throws today).
> **R2.** Add one `it()`: heading `## 3.5 Task Breakdown` + one `- [ ]` row → `pass:true`,
> `/1 task row/`.
> **R3.** Implement the locator with `headingTitleOf` + `body.split(/^(?=## )/m)` and count rows
> **within the located section**, never over `doc.body`. §Schema changes (4) already writes it
> correctly — do not "simplify". A whole-body counter is caught by Scenario 7 alone (canonical
> `story.md`'s §4 DoD checkboxes flip it green); R2 doubles that single witness.
>
> **C. Two prose corrections at promotion (assertions unchanged).**
>
> **R4.** Retitle Scenario 3b's second `it()` to name the **synthetic** body and point at pinning
> S1b as the real bump witness. Measured: on its own `legacyBody`, `section(4)` passes and
> `section(5)` fails, so 3b pins the **post**-bump index and stays true — but it never reads the
> registry or a corpus item, so it stays green if the bump is forgotten. Its "the accepted N5
> residue" title claims a meaning it does not carry: the same impersonates-a-witness shape this
> plan requires be de-staled in `readiness-predicates-prior-work-ambiguity.node.test.ts:274/:356`.
> **R5.** Correct Scenario 5's comment: it pins a **hardcoded constant**, not
> `readiness-gates.md:36`'s citation (that is the pinning test's own S7). Its value is that it is
> the only witness **external** to `gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` — S7 dies with that
> file, Scenario 5 does not. It reads `CLI_ROOT` only; **do not** make it read the registry, which
> would import cross-branch fragility for a check S7 already performs.
>
> **D. Sequence, verbatim.**
>

> does: *"no other agent ticks these rows, and **QA** will report any that stay unchecked."* The
> seam is `architect.md` → nobody and `qa.md` → nobody; the Developer→QA edge is already narrated.

>   `/^…$/m` anchoring a CRLF or trailing-whitespace `CLAUDE.md` makes `readBlock` return `null` and
>   `doctor --drift` then reports `claude-md-block-mismatch`. Also missing: the two existing tests

>   six CLAUDE.md-touching default-tier files — baseline 90 tests / 88 pass / 0 fail / 2 skipped;

>   content."* Measured: the canonical file is 64 lines with `<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->` at **line 7**
>   — six lines of payload-wrapper preamble precede it, and they must stay, because `extractBlock`
>   ships only the bounded block. The confirmation as written can only fail. Worse, canonical

## How M3 relates to the Sprint Goal — say it plainly

The Sprint Goal is: *"Give ClearGate the two decomposition surfaces it lacks — a pre-sprint SPIKE
charter for bounded discovery, and a Task Breakdown section inside Story/CR/Bug — and repair the
gate-index defect that blocks the second one."*

**M3 serves none of those three clauses.** M1 shipped clause 1 (SPIKE charter). M2 shipped clauses
2 and 3 (Task Breakdown + the `section(N)` index repair), and the M2 post-flight ruled the
milestone `MET` with machine witnesses. M3 adds no decomposition surface, touches no template, no
`readiness-gates.md` criterion, no predicate, no `section(N)` index, and no gate. It is
**CLAUDE.md marker-handling hygiene**, scheduled into SPRINT-39 because the two items were already
gate-green and the sprint had wave capacity — not because the goal needs them.

Manufacturing a goal linkage here would be the exact stale-prose failure this sprint exists to
remove. So, per item, what it *does* serve:

- **BUG-043 serves scaffold-integrity.** `injectClaudeMd` / `readBlock` / `writeBlock` /
  `removeBlock` are the only four functions in the product that rewrite a file the user owns and
  did not ask ClearGate to manage. The greedy unanchored `BLOCK_REGEX` splices the block into the
  middle of a user's sentence when the file mentions the markers in prose (measured below, case
  `D`). That is the product's single highest-blast-radius silent write. It serves the same value
  BUG-042 served — *"a silent, green-looking failure in a correctness surface"* (BUG-043
  `## Prior work`) — which is a sprint **theme**, not a goal clause. Call it what it is.
- **CR-105 serves prompt-cache prefix stability**, a token-cost property of every session in every
  repo that installs ClearGate. It is grounded in this repo's own file (block at 129–186 of 186,
  re-measured) and is otherwise unrelated to decomposition.
- **Both serve the release**, in the narrow sense that they are the last two `cleargate-cli`
  behaviour changes before the Gate-4 publish that M2 P3 already requires for an unrelated reason
  (the global 0.24.2 binary cannot parse `task-breakdown-complete`).

Nothing in M3 is a prerequisite for closing the sprint on its stated goal. If M3 slips, the goal is
still met. Record that in the sprint report rather than folding M3 into the goal narrative.

## Order

**wave8 (BUG-043) → wave9 (CR-105). Strictly sequential. Not parallelisable, for two independent reasons.**

1. **File collision.** Both edit `cleargate-cli/src/init/inject-claude-md.ts` — BUG-043 changes its
   `BLOCK_REGEX` (`:18`), CR-105 rewrites the body of `injectClaudeMd` (`:46-52`) six lines below
   it. `waves.json:128` states the same edge.
2. **Semantic dependency, and it is real rather than ceremonial.** CR-105's new contract is
   remove-then-prepend, which runs the match over the whole file on **every** `init`, including the
   idempotent re-run. Under today's unanchored regex the `D_inline_only` case (measured) splices the
   new block into the middle of a user's prose line. CR-105 raises the frequency of that from
   "occasional" to "every install", so BUG-043 is a hard predecessor. CR-105 `§2` already says this;
   it is confirmed, not assumed.

**Merge order:** BUG-043 merges to `cleargate-cli` `main` **before** CR-105 is cut. `cleargate-cli`
has no sprint branch — STORY-054-02 merged straight to `main` (`507f67c`) and 054-06 likewise
(`9e46ce5`). Follow that. Do not invent a `sprint/S-39` branch in `cleargate-cli` for two items.

**Nothing in M3 is parallelisable.** There is no second lane to fill.

## NORMATIVE RULINGS — bind both items, supersede the item text where they conflict

### N1 — The recorded premise "`$` under `/m` does not match before a `\r`" is FALSE in JavaScript. Measured.

BUG-043's Q2 human decision carries this rider:

> *"New failure mode this introduces, and it must be handled: `$` under the `m` flag does not match
> before a `\r`, so a CRLF-converted `CLAUDE.md` — or a marker line with trailing whitespace —
> silently stops matching… the fix must normalise line endings and trim trailing whitespace on the
> marker line before matching, and the test suite must cover a CRLF fixture."*

**The CRLF half of that is wrong.** In ECMAScript, `LineTerminator` includes U+000D CARRIAGE
RETURN, so `^` and `$` under `/m` are CRLF-safe. Proven directly:

```
/^b$/m.test("a\r\nb\r\nc")  ->  true
/^b$/m.test("a\rb\rc")      ->  true
```

and confirmed end-to-end: a strict `/^<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->$([\s\S]*)^<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->$/m`
returns `"\r\nBLOCK\r\n"` — a correct match — on a fully CRLF fixture. The premise is true of
Python's `re.MULTILINE` and of several other engines; it is not true here.

**The trailing-whitespace half IS real.** A marker line with trailing spaces returns `null` under a
strict anchor.

**Consequence — and this matters, because the literal instruction is destructive.** "Normalise line
endings before matching" is only implementable as *whole-file* normalisation, and the write path
(`writeBlock` / `removeBlock` / `injectClaudeMd`) operates on the same string it matched. So a
literal implementation rewrites a CRLF user file to LF wholesale — a silent, whole-file content
change to a file the user owns, which is the precise harm class BUG-043 exists to close. Rejected.

**RULING: tolerance goes in the pattern, not in a mutation of the file.** This delivers the decision's
stated outcome (CRLF and trailing-whitespace files keep matching) with zero bytes rewritten. The
CRLF fixture the decision asks for is still required — it now pins that CRLF was never broken,
which is worth having. Listed in `## Open decisions` as reversible by the human; not blocking.

### N2 — The two regexes, verbatim. Copy; do not paraphrase.

`cleargate-cli/src/lib/claude-md-surgery.ts:7` becomes:

```ts
const BLOCK_REGEX = /^<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->[ \t]*$([\s\S]*)^<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->[ \t]*$/m;
```

`cleargate-cli/src/init/inject-claude-md.ts:18` becomes (no capture group — matches its current shape):

```ts
const BLOCK_REGEX = /^<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->[ \t]*$[\s\S]*^<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->[ \t]*$/m;
```

Greedy `[\s\S]*` is **retained** per the Q2 human decision. `[ \t]*` is horizontal whitespace only —
never `\s*`, which would swallow newlines and re-open the very span the anchor exists to bound.
Never `\r?$` — `$` already handles `\r` (N1), and consuming the `\r` corrupts the captured body
(measured: `\r?$` returned `"\nBLOCK\r\n"` where today returns `"\r\nBLOCK\r\n"`).

### N3 — The `includes()` guards diverge from the anchored regex and produce a SILENT NO-OP. This is the fix's real hazard, and neither `§4` nor its amendment names it.

`writeBlock` (`claude-md-surgery.ts:25-31`) and `removeBlock` (`:39-45`) guard with
`content.includes(CLEARGATE_START)` / `includes(CLEARGATE_END)` — **substring** checks — and then
call `content.replace(BLOCK_REGEX, …)`. Today the two agree, because the regex is itself
substring-shaped. **Under anchoring they diverge**, and `String.replace` with a non-matching regex
returns the original string. Measured:

| fixture | `writeBlock` today | `writeBlock` anchored, guards left as `includes` |
|---|---|---|
| `A` block alone on its lines | changed | changed |
| `C` marker with trailing spaces | changed | **NO-OP, silent** |
| `D` markers only quoted inline in prose | changed | **NO-OP, silent** |
| `G` markers indented 2 spaces | changed | **NO-OP, silent** |
| `F` no markers at all | throws | throws |

`removeBlock` is identical. The downstream consequence is worse than the function's own return
value: `uninstall.ts:437` does `const stripped = removeBlock(claudeMdContent); await
writeAtomic(claudeMdPath, stripped);` then pushes `'CLAUDE.md (CLEARGATE block)'` onto
`removedPaths`. A silent no-op therefore **writes the file back unchanged and reports the block as
removed.**

**RULING: add a third guard, keyed on the regex, after the two existing `includes` guards, in both
`writeBlock` and `removeBlock`:**

```ts
const NOT_ANCHORED =
  'CLAUDE.md has CLEARGATE markers but no anchored block (both markers must sit alone on their own line)';
…
  if (!BLOCK_REGEX.test(content)) {
    throw new Error(NOT_ANCHORED);
  }
```

**The two existing `includes` guards and their exact messages stay, in their current order.** That
is not stylistic: three existing tests assert those two message strings verbatim
(`test/lib/claude-md-surgery.node.test.ts:168`, `:175`, `:204`). Unifying them into one message reds
all three for no gain. Additive is both correct and free.

`uninstall.ts:332-337` carries its own duplicate pair of `includes` guards. Leave them: they run
*before* classification and their job is to abort early on a file with no markers at all. With N3's
third guard in place, the unanchored case now surfaces at `:441` as
`Warning: could not strip CLAUDE.md block: <NOT_ANCHORED>` and the file is left untouched — loud,
not silent. **This is what makes BUG-043 `§4`'s "uninstall.ts inherits the regex fix for free; no
separate change" true.** Without N3 that line is false. It is conditionally correct, and the
condition is N3.

### N4 — `upgrade`'s CLAUDE.md branch is UNREACHABLE. Measured across the repo's entire history.

`upgrade` iterates `pkgManifest.files` (`upgrade.ts:476` → `:633` filter → work items) and routes on
`isClaudeMd(entry.path)` (`:211-213`: `path.basename(filePath) === 'CLAUDE.md'`). **No manifest has
ever contained a `CLAUDE.md` row.**

```
this repo's .cleargate/.install-manifest.json : 65 entries, 0 ending in CLAUDE.md
committed cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json    : 70 entries, 0 ending in CLAUDE.md
every historical commit of that file          : 0 hits for '"path": "CLAUDE.md"'
```

The cause is deliberate and pinned in two places: `build-manifest.ts:333-341`
`INTENTIONALLY_UNTRACKED` contains `'CLAUDE.md'` ("never copied verbatim… bounded-block-injected"),
and `test/scripts/build-manifest.node.test.ts:258-261` asserts `classifyPath('CLAUDE.md') === null`.
`copy-payload.ts:63` `SKIP_FILES` does the same on the copy side.

**Consequences, all three of which change how M3 is dispatched:**

1. **BUG-043 Defect A is latent, not live.** `§1`'s *"the user's entire `CLAUDE.md` is replaced by
   the payload"* is not reachable through any CLI invocation. `§3`'s evidence is honest about this —
   it says *"Trace `upgrade.ts:364-378` with those two results"*, a trace, not an execution — but
   `§1` and the `P1-High` severity read as a live data-loss bug. They are not. `§1`'s stated
   preconditions ("classified as drifted and the operator chooses take-theirs") omit the binding
   one: *`CLAUDE.md` must be a manifest entry, and it never has been.*
2. **The fix is still correct and still in scope.** It removes a landmine that arms itself the
   instant anyone adds `CLAUDE.md` to the manifest — which is exactly what a naive reading of
   CR-105 `§2`'s last bullet would prompt (see N5). Defense-in-depth on a dead branch is cheap;
   leaving a `mergedContent = theirs` in a file whose docstring promises *"leaving user prose
   intact"* (`upgrade.ts:12-13`) is not.
3. **CR-105 `§0.5` Q2's human decision — "Both `init` and `upgrade`" — applies to dead code.**
   Implementing relocation on the take-theirs branch delivers nothing observable. Escalated in
   `## Open decisions`; it does not block wave9, because the `init` half is the whole value.

### N5 — CR-105 `§2`'s manifest bullet and `§4`'s manifest assertion are UNSATISFIABLE. Replace, do not attempt.

`§2`: *"`.cleargate/.install-manifest.json` records a sha per tracked file. Relocation changes
`CLAUDE.md`'s content, so the snapshot entry must be updated by the same run or the next `upgrade`
reports spurious drift."*
`§4`: *"Assert the install manifest sha for `CLAUDE.md` is refreshed by the run that relocates."*

There is no entry to refresh (N4). `upgrade` cannot report drift on a path it never classifies.

**RULING — the acceptance criterion becomes, verbatim:**

> Assert `classifyPath('CLAUDE.md')` still returns `null` and that no code path added by this CR
> writes a `CLAUDE.md` row into `MANIFEST.json` or `.install-manifest.json`. Relocation must not
> make `CLAUDE.md` a tracked file.

This is the same intent — "a follow-up `upgrade` reports no drift" — expressed against the
mechanism that actually exists. `test/scripts/build-manifest.node.test.ts:258-261` already pins it;
CR-105 must not touch that test.

`init.ts:555` `POST_PROCESSED_FILES` is the machinery `§2` was reaching for. **Do not add
`CLAUDE.md` to it.** It re-hashes files that init post-processes *and that are manifest entries*;
a non-entry is skipped by `pkgManifest.files.map` before it ever reaches the set. Adding it there
does nothing and is one edit away from someone "fixing" that by adding the manifest row.

### N6 — `doctor`'s drift guard has no `--drift` flag. Name the real command.

BUG-043's `§4 AMENDMENT` and the M2 post-flight both say `doctor --drift`. **No such flag exists** —
`selectMode` (`doctor.ts:103-121`) knows `checkScaffold`, `sessionStart`, `pricing`, `canEdit`, and
returns `'hook-health'` otherwise. `checkCanonicalDrift` is called at `doctor.ts:272`, inside
`runHookHealth`, which the switch dispatches for mode `'hook-health'` (`:1039-1041`). So the
reachable command is **bare `cleargate doctor`** — and when `isAdvisory()` is false it sets
`outcome.blocker = true` (`:279`), i.e. `doctor` **fails**. Use the real command in every report,
test title and comment.

Second, non-obvious property: `checkCanonicalDrift` returns `{skipped:true}` when
`<cwd>/cleargate-planning/` is absent (`drift-check.ts:145-148`). It is **meta-repo-only**. Whatever
M3 does to drift behaviour is a dogfood-surface change, not an end-user one.

### N7 — Both regexes stay duplicated (human deferred unification), so PIN the duplicate.

BUG-043 `§4` defers unifying the two `BLOCK_REGEX` constants: *"desirable and is the BUG-041 pattern,
but is deferred so this fix stays minimal."* Honour that. But FLASHCARD 2026-08-24 `#id-parsing
#dogfood` is explicit: *"A parser duplicated into a payload `.mjs` … is how the 15th divergent
grammar gets born. Pin the duplicate with a shared-corpus test that runs BOTH, or don't duplicate."*

**RULING: BUG-043 ships a shared-corpus equivalence test.** One table of ~9 fixtures, run through
`readBlock` (surgery) and through `injectClaudeMd`/`extractBlock` (inject), asserting the two
modules agree on *which span is the block* for every fixture. This is the cheapest possible
insurance against the two copies drifting when CR-105 edits one of them one wave later — which is a
concrete, scheduled risk, not a hypothetical.

There is a **third** copy: `test/commands/init.node.test.ts:268` defines its own
`GREEDY_BLOCK_REGEX` and asserts against it. It is not `src`, so it will keep asserting the old
grammar and stay green while `src` changes underneath it. BUG-043 replaces that literal with an
import of the exported matcher (see N8). Removing a test-local duplicate is not the unification the
human deferred.

There is a **fourth**, out of scope: `root-gitignore.ts:37-41` `ROOT_BLOCK_REGEX`, same greedy
rationale, different markers (`# >>> cleargate >>>`). Not `CLAUDE.md`, not BUG-043's. Note it in
the report as the next instance of the class; do not touch it.

### N8 — Export a matcher, not the regex.

To let the test import the grammar without exporting a mutable `RegExp` (`/m` regexes are stateless
here — no `/g` — but exporting the object invites `.lastIndex` misuse and in-place edits), export a
predicate from `claude-md-surgery.ts`:

```ts
/** True when the file carries a well-formed block: both markers alone on their own lines. */
export function hasAnchoredBlock(content: string): boolean {
  return BLOCK_REGEX.test(content);
}
```

`init.node.test.ts` scenario 2 uses `hasAnchoredBlock` plus `removeBlock` in place of its private
regex. CR-105 uses it in wave9 to express "exactly one anchored block" (N9). `uninstall.ts` may
adopt it for its `:332-337` pre-check, but is not required to.

### N9 — "Exactly one block in the file" MUST be counted on ANCHORED MARKER LINES, never on substring occurrences.

CR-105 `§4` requires *"exactly one block in the file."* A naive
`content.split(CLEARGATE_START).length - 1 === 1` **fails on the block ClearGate itself ships**.
Measured, both trees:

```
root CLAUDE.md      : anchored START [129]  anchored END [186]  inline-mention line [178]
canonical CLAUDE.md : anchored START [7]    anchored END [64]   inline-mention line [56]
```

Line 178 (root) / 56 (canonical) is the block's own sentence *"Content OUTSIDE this
`<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->…<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->` block takes precedence…"*, which sits **inside**
the block and must keep doing so. Every installed repo therefore has two `START` substrings and two
`END` substrings and always will. The count that means anything is:

```ts
const anchoredStarts = content.split('\n').filter(l => /^<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->[ \t]*$/.test(l)).length;
```

The same trap bites BUG-043's `§5` Defect-B assertion *"no user line is fused onto a marker"*. Express
it as: the output contains the exact string `I documented the `, **and** `anchoredStarts === 1`,
**and** `anchoredEnds === 1`.

### N10 — Anchoring narrows Defect B; it does not eliminate it. Pin the residual as KNOWN, or it gets rediscovered.

Measured:

| fixture | today | anchored (N2) |
|---|---|---|
| `E` stray `END` quoted **inline** in prose below the block | eats `I documented the ` | **fixed** — body is `"\nBLOCK\n"` |
| `H` stray `END` **alone on its own line** below the block | eats `user line one` | **still eats it** |

Greedy plus anchored means the match still runs to the **last anchored** `END`. A user who writes a
bare `<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->` on its own line below the block still loses the text between. That is
the accepted consequence of the Q2 decision to retain greedy, and it is correct — non-greedy would
truncate the real block at line 178. **BUG-043 must ship `H` as an explicitly-titled
known-limitation test** (`documents the residual: a stray END alone on its own line still extends
the greedy match`), asserting the current behaviour. Silence here is how it gets refiled in six
months as a new bug.

# Per-story blueprint

## BUG-043 — CLAUDE.md marker handling loses user prose (wave8)

- **Cross-story coupling:** CR-105 (wave9) rewrites `injectClaudeMd`'s body six lines below the
  `BLOCK_REGEX` this item changes, and consumes `hasAnchoredBlock` (N8) and the anchored grammar.
  No other in-sprint item touches these files (M2 post-flight Q7.1, re-verified: zero overlap with
  `work-item-type.ts`, `derive-bucket.ts`, `page-schema.ts`, `readiness-predicates.ts`).

### `## 4. Execution Sandbox` verdict: **INCOMPLETE**, and the amendment left three things open.

The orchestrator's `§4 AMENDMENT` correctly added `drift-check.ts`,
`test/lib/claude-md-surgery.node.test.ts` and `test/commands/init.node.test.ts:263-278`. Still
missing after it:

| # | Site / claim | Status | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The `includes()` guards in `writeBlock` (`:25-30`) and `removeBlock` (`:39-44`) | **MISSING — this is the fix's real hazard** | N3. The named files are right; the specific lines that must change inside them are not named, and the omission is the difference between a fix and a new silent no-op. |
| 2 | `cleargate-cli/src/commands/uninstall.ts` | **Listed as NOT in scope; conditionally correct** | N3. `§4` says it *"inherits the regex fix for free; no separate change."* True **iff** the third guard throws. Without N3, `uninstall.ts:437-441` writes the file back unchanged and reports the block removed. The claim needs the condition attached, not deletion. |
| 3 | `upgrade`'s CLAUDE.md branch is unreachable | **MISSING, and it re-scopes the item** | N4. `§1`'s severity framing and `§5`'s Defect-A acceptance both read as live. Neither `§4` nor the amendment mentions `MANIFEST.json`, `build-manifest.ts:334`, or `copy-payload.ts:63`. |
| 4 | `test/lib/claude-md-surgery.node.test.ts:211-218` hardcoded absolute path | **Correctly noted; correctly OUT of scope** | See `## Question 2` below. Filed as **BUG-060**. Census found **four** live sites, not one. |
| 5 | `doctor --drift` | **Wrong command name** | N6. |
| 6 | The CRLF rationale in Q2's recorded decision | **Wrong premise** | N1. Measured false for JavaScript. |

**Confirmed correct as written:** the three `Modify` targets; the deferral of `BLOCK_REGEX`
unification (N7 adds a pin rather than reversing it); both Open Questions' recorded human decisions
(refuse-with-a-named-error, anchor-to-own-line); the `§5` Defect-B probe-4 fixture.

### Execution route

  (`9e46ce5`). **NOT an outer worktree** — FLASHCARD 2026-08-26 `#worktree #collision-surface
  #danger`: `cleargate-cli/` has 0 tracked files in the outer repo and does not exist inside

  (`template-claude-md.node.test.ts:17`, `claude-md-surgery.node.test.ts:214`); parking the outer
  checkout on `main` produces one spurious Scenario 7 failure that is not yours.
- **`cleargate-cli` IS INVOLVED, therefore every commit is UNGATED.** Cross-Cutting Rule 6 +

  `*.sample`, no `core.hooksPath`. **Zero** hooks. The surface gate, `check:no-vitest`,

- **Forbidden for the whole of M3:** running `cleargate init` in the meta-repo. It rewrites

### Source changes, verbatim

`src/lib/claude-md-surgery.ts` — three edits:

```ts
// :7 (replaces the existing constant; keep the comment above it, amended per N1)
const BLOCK_REGEX = /^<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->[ \t]*$([\s\S]*)^<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->[ \t]*$/m;
const NOT_ANCHORED =
  'CLAUDE.md has CLEARGATE markers but no anchored block (both markers must sit alone on their own line)';

// new export (N8)
export function hasAnchoredBlock(content: string): boolean {
  return BLOCK_REGEX.test(content);
}

// inside writeBlock, AFTER the two existing includes-guards, BEFORE the replace (N3)
  if (!BLOCK_REGEX.test(content)) {
    throw new Error(NOT_ANCHORED);
  }

// inside removeBlock, AFTER the two existing includes-guards, BEFORE the replace (N3)
  if (!BLOCK_REGEX.test(content)) {
    throw new Error(NOT_ANCHORED);
  }
```

The `:4-6` comment block currently reads *"IMPORTANT: regex MUST be GREEDY… Non-greedy would stop at
the first inline END marker in prose."* Keep the greedy rationale, **append** the anchoring one, and
state N1's correction so the next reader does not re-derive it:

> Anchored to line-alone markers (`^…$` with `/m`) so an inline prose mention is not a candidate
> match. `[ \t]*` tolerates trailing whitespace on the marker line. CRLF needs no handling: in
> ECMAScript `\r` is a LineTerminator, so `^`/`$` under `/m` already match around it — verified,
> not assumed.

`src/init/inject-claude-md.ts` — one edit at `:18` (N2), plus the `:5` docstring line
`Detection regex: …` updated to the new form. **Do not touch `:10`'s `If existing no match: append`
line in wave8** — that is CR-105's, and editing it here creates a needless wave8/wave9 conflict in
the same docstring.

`src/commands/upgrade.ts:361-378` — replace the whole `try/catch` in the `isClaudeMd` branch:

```ts
      try {
        const ourBlock = readBlock(ours);
        const theirBlock = readBlock(theirs);
        if (ourBlock === null) {
          stderr(
            `[skip] ${entry.path}: no <!-- CLEARGATE:START --> block present; upgrade will not ` +
            `rewrite this file. Run \`cleargate init\` to install the block.`
          );
          return { updated: false, newSha: null };
        }
        if (theirBlock === null) {
          return { updated: false, newSha: null };
        }
        mergedContent = writeBlock(ours, theirBlock);
      } catch (err) {
        stderr(
          `[skip] ${entry.path}: CLAUDE.md surgery failed (${(err as Error).message}); ` +
          `file left unchanged.`
        );
        return { updated: false, newSha: null };
      }
```

`return { updated: false, newSha: null }` is the established "skip this file, continue the run"
idiom in this function (`upgrade.ts:425`, `:435`, `:444`) — the run does not fail and other files
continue, which is exactly what the Q1 human decision asks for. Do not invent a new signal.
**Both** destructive routes — the `ourBlock === null` branch and the bare `catch` — are gone, which
is what the decision requires ("Both routes to the current full overwrite… are replaced by this
refusal. No code path may substitute the payload for a user's file").

`test/commands/init.node.test.ts:268` — replace the private `GREEDY_BLOCK_REGEX` with the imported
`hasAnchoredBlock` + `removeBlock` (N7). The scenario's intent (exactly one contiguous block after a
re-run) is preserved; express the count per N9.

### QA kick-back criteria — BUG-043 fails on any one of these

1. **Any `includes()`-only guard survives in `writeBlock` or `removeBlock`.** Grep the diff: both
   functions must carry a `BLOCK_REGEX.test(...)` guard. Absent ⇒ the fix ships a new silent
   corruption in `uninstall`. (N3)
2. **The two existing error message strings changed.** `test/lib/claude-md-surgery.node.test.ts:168`,
   `:175`, `:204` assert them verbatim. A unified message is a kick-back even if the suite is green
   because the tests were edited to match.
3. **`\s*` or `\r?$` appears in either regex.** `\s*` swallows newlines and un-bounds the span;
   `\r?$` corrupts the captured body. (N2)
4. **Non-greedy `[\s\S]*?` anywhere.** Reverses a recorded human decision and truncates the real
   block at `CLAUDE.md:178`.
5. **`upgrade` still has any path assigning `mergedContent = theirs` inside the `isClaudeMd`
   branch.** Both routes must be refusals. (N4)
6. **No CRLF fixture in the new tests.** Required by the Q2 human decision, independent of N1.
7. **The `H` residual (stray `END` alone on its own line) is not pinned as a known limitation.** (N10)
8. **Any new test uses a literal `/Users/` path.** That is BUG-060; adding a fifth site while
   BUG-060 is open is a kick-back on its own.
9. **A "green suite" is offered as evidence the fix works.** The full fix is measurably inert on
   the existing suite (107/107 before and after). QA must verify the *new* tests fail on
   `9e46ce5` and pass after — a red-before transcript, not a green-after one.
10. **The by-hand typecheck + suite numbers are absent from the Dev report.** `cleargate-cli` has
    zero installed hooks; nothing else can catch this. (Cross-Cutting Rule 6)
11. **`test/scripts/build-manifest.node.test.ts:258-261` was touched**, or `CLAUDE.md` appears in
    `INTENTIONALLY_UNTRACKED`'s complement, or in `POST_PROCESSED_FILES`. (N4/N5)
12. **`cleargate init` was run in the meta-repo.**

- `claude-md-surgery.ts:25-30` / `:39-44` — `includes()` guards; `String.replace` on a non-match

- `uninstall.ts:437-441` — `removeBlock` result is written unconditionally and success is pushed to

- `doctor.ts:272` + `:279` — the drift guard sets `outcome.blocker = true` when not advisory.

- `run-default-tests.mjs:24-28` — the default tier excludes `*.integration.node.test.ts`. Name the

- `CLAUDE.md:178` / `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md:56` — the block quotes its own markers inline.

The orchestrator's `§3 AMENDMENT` correctly demolished the `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md` row
("no content change, but confirm the block is the first content"): measured, that file is 64 lines
with `START` at `:7`, six lines of payload-wrapper preamble precede it, `extractBlock` ships only
the block, and `:3` carries the obsolete *"init **appends** the bounded block"* sentence. All
independently re-verified. It also correctly added `inject-claude-md.ts:10`'s docstring.

**Two further defects the amendment did not reach:**

| # | Claim | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | `§2` bullet 5 + `§4` *"Assert the install manifest sha for `CLAUDE.md` is refreshed"* | **WRONG — unsatisfiable** | N4/N5. `CLAUDE.md` is in `INTENTIONALLY_UNTRACKED` (`build-manifest.ts:334`); 0 rows in the current manifest (70 entries), 0 in the current install snapshot (65 entries), 0 in **every** historical commit of `MANIFEST.json`. There is nothing to refresh, and `upgrade` cannot report drift on it. |
| B | `§3` *"`cleargate-cli/src/commands/upgrade.ts` — apply relocation on the take-theirs branch"* + `§0.5` Q2's *"Both `init` and `upgrade`"* | **CORRECT-BUT-INERT** | N4. The branch never executes. The decision's own rationale — *"none of the three known installs would ever receive the change without being re-initialised"* — is inverted by the mechanism: `init` is precisely how they receive it. |
| C | `§3` **omits** `test/commands/init.node.test.ts` scenario 3 | **MISSING — and it is the one existing test CR-105 reds** | Measured: applying the relocate contract to the mirror turns the seven-file run from 107/107/0/0 to **107 tests / 106 pass / 1 fail / 0 skipped**, the single failure being *"scenario 3: existing CLAUDE.md without markers — appends bounded block, preserves user content"* (`init.node.test.ts:294-314`), whose assertion `startIdx > userIdx` (`:308`) is the evicted logic in executable form. |

**Confirmed correct as written, re-verified rather than inherited:** root `CLAUDE.md` is 186 lines

### Execution route

- **Repos: TWO.** `cleargate-cli` (src + tests) **and** the outer meta-repo (root `CLAUDE.md`,
  `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md`).
- **cli half:** `cleargate-cli` main checkout, branch `story/CR-105` cut from cli `main`
  **after BUG-043 has merged there**. Ungated — same by-hand typecheck + suite obligation, same
  QA-Verify confirmation requirement.
- **Outer half: the MAIN outer checkout, on a branch cut from `sprint/S-39`. NOT a worktree.**
  This is load-bearing, not preference. Four cli tests read the outer tree **by filesystem path at
  run time** — `template-claude-md.node.test.ts:17-20` (`REPO_ROOT` = `resolve(<test file>,…)` = the
  main checkout), `dogfood-split-integrity.node.test.ts:97`, `no-exec-mode-vocab.node.test.ts:46`,
  `enforcement-doc-coherence.node.test.ts:50-51` — plus
  `claude-md-surgery.node.test.ts:214`, which reads the main checkout by absolute literal and can
  *never* see a worktree. An outer edit made in a worktree is invisible to every one of them: the
  suite would certify the un-relocated file. This is the same class as FLASHCARD 2026-08-27
  `#test-harness #cross-repo #danger` and BUG-042's `gate check <abs-path>` case.
- **Cross-Cutting Rule 1 DOES apply here** — but not as byte-parity. FLASHCARD 2026-08-28
  `#dogfood-split #danger`: *"`cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md` IS the injection spec (64 lines); root
  `CLAUDE.md` is the repo's own doc with that block injected (186). A dispatch demanding byte-parity
  on this pair manufactures a false kick-back — verify the EDITED LINE, not the file."* The real
  invariant is narrower and machine-checked: **the bounded-block bodies must hash-match**, because
  `drift-check.ts:118-128` compares `hashNormalized(canonicalBlock)` to `hashNormalized(rootBlock)`
  and a mismatch makes `cleargate doctor` set `outcome.blocker = true`. Verify with:
  ```
  node -e "const fs=require('fs');const R=/^<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->[ \t]*\$([\s\S]*)^<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->[ \t]*\$/m;const c=s=>R.exec(fs.readFileSync(s,'utf8'))[1];const a=c('cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md'),b=c('CLAUDE.md');console.log('block-equal:',a===b,a.length,b.length)"
  ```
  Expected today and after: `block-equal: true 11762 11762`.
- **`§3`'s `:3` sentence rewrite lives OUTSIDE the block** (canonical line 3, block starts at line 7),
  so it changes no hash and creates no drift. Good — but it also means nothing machine-checks it.
  QA must read the line.
- **Forbidden:** `cleargate init` in the meta-repo (it would relocate root `CLAUDE.md` *and* rewrite
  the live `/.claude/**` tree, colliding with Gate-4 R31 and the concurrent session). Relocate by
  editing the file directly.

### Commit plan

Three commits, in this order. **The order is a discipline rule, not a hook-enforced one** — M2 P1
established that the outer pre-commit hook does **not** run the cli suite, and cli commits run no
hook at all.

1. **cli, red:** `test(CR-105): red baseline for block-leads contract` — new red file; plus the
   `init.node.test.ts` scenario-3 inversion staged **here**, so the eviction is visible as a red
   rather than as a green edit buried in the fix commit.
2. **cli, fix:** `feat(CR-105): the ClearGate block leads CLAUDE.md` — `inject-claude-md.ts`
   (`:5`/`:10` docstring + `:46-52` body), `init.ts` if the call site needs it (it does **not** —
   see below), `upgrade.ts` only if the human resolves Open Decision 2 in favour of implementing the
