3. **outer:** `docs(CR-105): relocate the ClearGate block to lead CLAUDE.md` — root `CLAUDE.md`
   relocation + canonical `CLAUDE.md:3` sentence rewrite, **in one commit** (they are the two halves
   of one contract, and splitting them opens a window where `:3` documents `append` while the tree
   demonstrates `prepend`).

**`init.ts` needs no change.** `§3` lists it as *"the call site that must adopt the new contract"*;
measured, `init.ts:377-388` calls `extractBlock` then `injectClaudeMd(existingClaudeMd,
claudeMdBlock)` and writes the result. The contract change lives entirely inside `injectClaudeMd`.
The three `stdout` branches at `:390-396` ("Created / Updated / unchanged") still classify
correctly because they compare `existingClaudeMd !== newClaudeMd`. Touching `init.ts` here is
scope creep; if the Developer believes otherwise, that is a report line, not a silent edit.

**INERT INTERMEDIATE: NO. Re-measured.** Commit 2 alone reds exactly one existing test —
`init.node.test.ts` scenario 3, measured 107/106/1/0 — which is why the scenario-3 inversion is
pulled forward into commit 1. With that ordering there is no red intermediate at any commit
boundary. Commit 3 (outer) is inert with respect to the cli suite: all four outer-reading tests are
content-indexed, not offset-indexed (verified above), and the block body hash is unchanged.

### Schema changes (verbatim)

None. No template, no heading, no `section(N)`, no frontmatter, no migration.

### Source change, verbatim

`src/init/inject-claude-md.ts`, replacing `:46-52`:

```ts
  // CR-105: the ClearGate block always LEADS the file. Remove any existing
  // block, then prepend the current one; user content follows. Rationale is
  // prompt-cache prefix stability — the stable half must physically precede
  // the volatile half.
  const stripped = BLOCK_REGEX.test(existing) ? existing.replace(BLOCK_REGEX, '') : existing;
  const rest = stripped.trim();
  return rest.length === 0 ? block + '\n' : block + '\n\n' + rest + '\n';
```

`:7-10`'s Rules docstring becomes:

```
 *   - If existing === null:  create file with block as full content (+ trailing newline)
 *   - Otherwise:             remove any existing block, then PREPEND the current block;
 *                            user content follows. The block always leads the file (CR-105).
```

**The `rest.length === 0` branch is not decoration.** Measured: without it, a file that reduces to
the block alone is not idempotent — the naive form appends a growing run of blank lines on each
re-run. Idempotence is a `§4` acceptance criterion; this is where it is won or lost.

**Do not `trimStart()` only.** `stripped.trim()` on both ends is what makes the
already-at-top → already-at-top round-trip byte-identical (measured across all five scenarios below).

`cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md:3` — rewrite the sentence. Current text, verbatim:

> *"If one already exists, init **appends** the bounded block below without touching the user's
> existing content."*

Replacement must describe prepend-and-relocate and must **not** move the markers, must **not**
delete the six preamble lines (`:1-6`), and must **not** claim the block is the first content of
*this* file (it is the first content of the *injected* file). Suggested, and the Developer may
improve the prose but not the semantics:

> *"If one already exists, init removes it and **prepends** the current bounded block, so the block
> always leads the file; the user's existing content follows it untouched."*

Root `CLAUDE.md` — relocate lines 129–186 to the top, everything else preserved byte-for-byte
below. Verify with the `block-equal` one-liner above **and** with
`git diff --stat` showing `CLAUDE.md | 116 +++---…` and no content deletions
(`git diff -U0 CLAUDE.md | grep '^-' | grep -v '^---'` should show only relocated lines, each of
which reappears as a `+`).

### Test scenarios — a red baseline IS authorable. **Dispatch QA-Red.**

File: `cleargate-cli/test/init/claude-md-block-leads.red.node.test.ts`. All five `§4` "new logic"
bullets and both "old logic evicted" bullets are directly expressible against `injectClaudeMd`.
Measured expected outcomes, all five green under the N-mandated implementation:

| # | Fixture | Assertion |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `existing === null` | output === `block + '\n'`; starts with `START` |
| 2 | prose, **no** block | starts with `START`; every byte of the user's prose present below; `anchoredStarts === 1` |
| 3 | block at the **bottom** | starts with `START`; prose intact below; `anchoredStarts === 1`; old block body absent |
| 4 | already in the new shape | **byte-identical** on a second run (idempotence) |
| 5 | block replacement | new body present; old body appears nowhere |
| 6 | **evicted logic** | for fixture 2, the final non-empty line is **not** `<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->` |
| 7 | **evicted logic, direct** | `output.indexOf(START) < output.indexOf('# My Project')` — the exact inversion of `init.node.test.ts:308` |
| 8 | **inline-mention-only file** | `anchoredStarts === 1` even though `output.split(START).length - 1 === 2`. Measured. This is N9 in a test, and it is the assertion a naive implementation of `§4`'s "exactly one block" gets wrong. |
| 9 | manifest invariant (N5) | `classifyPath('CLAUDE.md') === null`; no `CLAUDE.md` row in the payload manifest after the change |
| 10 | handler-level | `initHandler` against a tmpdir with pre-existing user prose leaves the block leading and the prose intact — the rewritten `init.node.test.ts` scenario 3 |

**No red baseline is authorable for:** the root-`CLAUDE.md` relocation itself and the canonical

the outer commit; there is nothing to write.** The cli half above is where QA-Red's whole budget goes.

### Reuse (no duplication)

`hasAnchoredBlock`, `removeBlock`, `extractBlock`, `CLEARGATE_START/END`, `hashNormalized`, the
`expect()` shim, and `template-claude-md.node.test.ts:17`'s `REPO_ROOT` idiom — all as in wave8.

### QA kick-back criteria — CR-105 fails on any one of these

1. **`cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md:1-6` preamble deleted, or the markers moved.** The amendment's
   whole point. `extractBlock` ships only the block; the preamble is deliberately outside it.
2. **Canonical `:3` still says "appends."** The single content change the amendment mandates.
3. **`inject-claude-md.ts:10`'s docstring still says "append block with 2 leading newlines."**
4. **The block bodies of root and canonical `CLAUDE.md` no longer hash-match.** Run the
   `block-equal` one-liner; `false` is a hard fail — `cleargate doctor` will block on it.
5. **`init.node.test.ts` scenario 3 was deleted rather than inverted**, or its title still says
   "appends", or scenario 4's title still says "above and below".
6. **"Exactly one block" asserted by substring count.** N9. Measured to be wrong on the shipped block.
7. **A second `BLOCK_REGEX` introduced anywhere.** `§3` forbids it and N7 pins the existing two.
8. **`CLAUDE.md` added to `MANIFEST.json`, `POST_PROCESSED_FILES`, or `classifyPath`.** N5.
9. **Idempotence not asserted**, or asserted only on the block-at-bottom fixture. The
   already-at-top → already-at-top round-trip is the one that catches the missing
   `rest.length === 0` branch.
10. **The `§0.5` Q1 relocation notice is missing from `init`'s stdout.**
11. **The outer edit was made in a worktree.** Re-run `template-claude-md.node.test.ts` from the
    main checkout; if it green-lights an un-relocated file, the certification is void.
12. **Any content in root `CLAUDE.md` lost or reordered other than the block move.**
    `git diff -U0` must show a pure relocation.
13. **The by-hand typecheck + suite numbers are absent from the Dev report** (Cross-Cutting Rule 6).
14. **`cleargate init` was run in the meta-repo.**

### Gotchas surfaced from code inspection

- `init.ts:377-388` — the call site needs **no** change; `§3` says it does. Verified by reading it.
- `init.ts:390-396` — the only place the `§0.5` Q1 notice can live. Currently unnamed by `§3`.
- `inject-claude-md.ts:41-43` — the `existing === null` branch already prepends by construction.
  Leave it alone; only `:46-52` changes.
- `CLAUDE.md:162` ↔ `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md:40` — the same sentence, **inside** the block.
  Relocation renumbers the root side from 162 to ≈34. See `## Cross-story risks`.
- `build-manifest.ts:333-341` + `copy-payload.ts:63` — the two `INTENTIONALLY_UNTRACKED` /
  `SKIP_FILES` sets that make `CLAUDE.md` special. Read both before touching anything manifest-shaped.
- `upgrade.ts:12-13` — the docstring promises *"leaving user prose intact."* Whatever wave9 does or
  does not do to that branch, the docstring must not become a lie.

## Cross-story risks

Things a Developer working on only one of these two items will miss.

1. **wave9 inherits wave8's grammar and can silently un-fix it.** `injectClaudeMd`'s body
   (`:46-52`, CR-105's) sits six lines below its `BLOCK_REGEX` (`:18`, BUG-043's). A Developer
   rewriting the body who "tidies" the regex, or who re-derives it from
   `claude-md-surgery.ts` by hand, re-opens Defect B in one module while the other stays fixed —
   which is precisely the divergent-grammar shape BUG-041 was about. **N7's shared-corpus
   equivalence test is the mechanical guard.** It must land in wave8, before wave9 is cut, or it
   guards nothing.
2. **BUG-057 collides with CR-105 on root `CLAUDE.md`. It is a LINE-NUMBER collision, and it is
   one-directional.** BUG-057 (open, not in any SPRINT-39 wave) targets `CLAUDE.md:162` —
   *"Save drafts to `.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/{TYPE}-{ID}-{Name}.md`"* — which is **inside**
   the bounded block (129–186), with its canonical twin at `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md:40`.
   CR-105 does not change that sentence, but it renumbers root's copy from **162 to ≈34**.
   Handling, in order of preference:
   - **Do not run BUG-057 concurrently with wave9.** (M2 post-flight says the same; confirmed here
     with the line read.)
   - If BUG-057 runs **after** CR-105, its `:162` grounding is stale and must be re-grepped. Add a
     one-line note to BUG-057's file at wave9 merge so the next dispatch does not chase a dead
     citation. That note is a defect-file edit, permitted; it is not a scope change.
   - If BUG-057 runs **before** CR-105, nothing breaks — but its fix must edit **both** trees
     (root + canonical), or `cleargate doctor` blocks on `claude-md-block-mismatch`
     (`drift-check.ts:118-128`, `doctor.ts:279`). This is the trap: BUG-057's own text cites only
     the root line.
3. **The block-body hash coupling is invisible until `doctor` runs.** Any edit to text inside
   `CLAUDE.md:129-186` that is not mirrored in `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md:7-64` makes bare
   `cleargate doctor` set `outcome.blocker = true`. No test in the cli suite catches it — the four
   outer-reading tests all check phrase *presence*, not block equality. Neither M3 item changes
   block text, so neither trips it; but both items sit one careless keystroke away, and the
   `block-equal` one-liner is the only cheap detector. Put it in both Dev reports.
4. **wave8's measured inertness is a trap for wave9's QA.** BUG-043 is 107/107 → 107/107. CR-105 is
   107/107 → 107/106/**1**. A QA reviewer who calibrates on wave8 ("this class of change doesn't move
   the suite") will read wave9's single red as a regression rather than as the evicted logic doing
   exactly what it should. State the expected red in the wave9 dispatch.
5. **`upgrade` unreachability spans both items.** BUG-043 fixes a dead branch; CR-105 is asked to
   extend the same dead branch. If wave8's Developer discovers N4 independently and "simplifies" by
   deleting the branch, wave9's `§3` instruction has no target and CR-105's Q2 decision is silently
   voided. **Do not delete the branch.** Fix it, note it as latent, and let the human decide
   (Open Decision 2).
6. **Both items are dispatched into a repo whose commits are ungated.** Four separate protections
   (surface gate, `check:no-vitest`, `check:no-inline-id-regex`, typecheck/test) that a Developer
   may reasonably assume are running are **not** running for any commit either item makes. This has
   already bitten once this sprint (STORY-054-05's three ungated commits). The by-hand run is the
   entire safety net for M3.
7. **The global `cleargate` binary is poisoned for the rest of the sprint.** M2 P3:
   `/opt/homebrew/bin/cleargate` is a real 0.24.2 install with no parser for
   `task-breakdown-complete`, so `cleargate gate check` emits an identical `predicate error` on
   every Story, CR and Bug in this repo — including correctly authored ones. Zero signal. Use
   `node cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js gate check <file>` for every gate check in M3. Neither M3 item
   changes this; both will hit it if anyone forgets.

## Question 1 — Does BUG-043's fix change `doctor`'s drift behaviour?

**Answer: it is a FIX, with zero regressions. Measured end-to-end through `checkCanonicalDrift`, not
reasoned from `readBlock`.**

Method: three out-of-tree copies of `drift-check.ts` were built, each importing a different copy of
`claude-md-surgery.ts` — today's, strict-anchored (`/^…$/m`, no tolerance), and the N2 tolerant
anchor. Each was run against seven tmpdir fixtures with a fixed canonical `CLAUDE.md` and a varying
root `CLAUDE.md`. `DRIFT reported` means `checkCanonicalDrift(tmp).drifted` contains
`{path:'CLAUDE.md', reason:'claude-md-block-mismatch'}`.

| root `CLAUDE.md` fixture | today | strict-anchored | **N2 tolerant** |
|---|---|---|---|
| LF, block mid-file (today's real shape) | clean | clean | clean |
| LF, block **at top** (post-CR-105 shape) | clean | clean | clean |
| **CRLF** throughout | clean | clean | clean |
| marker line with **trailing spaces** | **DRIFT** | **DRIFT** | **clean** |
| markers **indented** 2 spaces | DRIFT | DRIFT | DRIFT |
| block body genuinely diverged | DRIFT | DRIFT | DRIFT |
| no block at all | DRIFT | DRIFT | DRIFT |

Three findings:

1. **CRLF is a non-event.** All three variants report clean. The recorded premise that anchoring
   breaks CRLF is false (N1), so the mitigation it demanded — normalise-before-match — is
   unnecessary, and implementing it literally would have been actively harmful (it rewrites the
   user's file).
2. **The only row that moves is trailing-whitespace, and it moves from a FALSE POSITIVE to CLEAN.**
   Today's greedy `readBlock` captures the trailing spaces *into* the block body, so
   `hashNormalized(rootBlock) !== hashNormalized(canonicalBlock)` and `doctor` reports drift on a
   file whose block is materially identical. `hashNormalized` (`sha256.ts:32`) normalises CRLF but
   not marker-line trailing whitespace, so today's behaviour here is simply wrong. N2's `[ \t]*`
   fixes it.
3. **Everything that should report drift still does.** Indented markers, a genuinely diverged body,
   and an absent block are all unchanged.

**Ruling: a fix, and a note is warranted but not a caveat.** The Dev report should state
"`cleargate doctor` stops reporting a false `claude-md-block-mismatch` on a `CLAUDE.md` whose marker
line carries trailing whitespace; all other drift verdicts are byte-identical." Two corrections
belong in the same sentence: the command is bare `cleargate doctor`, not `doctor --drift` (N6), and
the guard is meta-repo-only (`drift-check.ts:145-148`) so no end-user install is affected.

**Had the fix shipped strict-anchored with no `[ \t]*` tolerance, it would still not have been a
drift regression** — the strict column matches today's column on all seven rows. The `[ \t]*` earns
its place elsewhere: in `writeBlock`/`removeBlock`, where strict-anchoring alone produces the silent
no-op of N3. That distinction is worth carrying, because it means the tolerance is a
*correctness* requirement for the surgery functions, not a *compatibility* requirement for drift.

## Question 2 — Is the `:211-218` hardcoded absolute path in scope, or a separate defect?

**Answer: SEPARATE DEFECT. Filed as BUG-060. And it is four sites, not one.**

Census, `command grep -rn "/Users/ssuladze" cleargate-cli/{src,test,scripts}` at `9e46ce5`:

```
DEFAULT TIER (runs on `npm --prefix cleargate-cli test`)
  test/hooks/session-start.node.test.ts:127
  test/lib/claude-md-surgery.node.test.ts:214            <- the one the amendment named
INTEGRATION TIER (excluded by run-default-tests.mjs:26)
  test/scripts/protocol-section-14.integration.node.test.ts:18
  test/lib/agent-developer-section.integration.node.test.ts:21
ARCHIVED (excluded by both tiers)
  test/scripts/_archive/protocol-section-24.integration.node.test.ts:39
DOC COMMENT ONLY (harmless as written, teaches the idiom)
  test/scaffold/pre-commit-downstream-safe.node.test.ts:51-52
  test/scaffold/file-surface-gate-e2e.node.test.ts:55-56
```

**Why separate, on the merits and not for tidiness:**

1. **It is a class, not a line.** Scoping it into BUG-043 repairs 1 of 4 and leaves the idiom
   duplicated three more ways — the same "fixing the instance instead of the class" error that
   FLASHCARD 2026-08-24 `#gate #dogfood` warns about (*"Unifying N parsers by READING finds only the
   ones you can think to grep"*). BUG-043's Developer is scoped to marker regexes and has no reason
   to census the other three.
2. **It is a different kind of change.** BUG-043 changes a regex. This changes test root-resolution,
   which is the exact area where this repo has already been burned twice (BUG-046; STORY-054-04
   P6a/P6b, where a wrong root reported as *skipped* rather than *failed*). The obvious repair —
   `skip: !existsSync(root)` — converts red to skip and reads as coverage. That hazard needs its own
   acceptance contract (`pass N` **and** `fail 0` **and** `skipped 0`), which is a specification, not
   a diff.
3. **BUG-043 keeps it green either way, measured.** The anchored regex still matches the real root
   `CLAUDE.md` byte-identically (`today === anchored`, body length 11762), so `:211-218` passes
   unchanged. There is no forcing function pulling it into wave8.

**Filed:** `.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/BUG-060_Tests_Hardcode_Absolute_Repo_Path.md` —
`bug.ready-for-fix`, **7/7 PASS** under `node cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js gate check`, severity
`P2-Medium`, `approved: false`, `Current Status: 🟡` (four of five gate criteria met literally; the
fifth is human approval, which an Architect cannot grant). Carries a 7-row `## Task Breakdown` and
one recorded open decision (shared helper vs. fix-in-place). Authored via heredoc, so the PostToolUse
ingest hook did **not** fire and no wiki page was written — verified: `.cleargate/wiki/bugs/` is
unchanged and the only new untracked path is the item file itself.

**Obligation on BUG-043's Developer:** keep `:211-218` green, and do **not** add a fifth site. Kick-back
criterion 8.

## Question 3 — Should M3 dogfood the Task Breakdown loop?

**Recommendation: AGAINST. And yes, it is blocked by BUG-059 Defect A.**

Four reasons, in descending weight.

**1. It is blocked, literally, by an unamended Guardrail — and this Architect hit the block while
writing this plan.** `architect.md:60-65` orders the Architect to write task rows into each work
item's own `## Task Breakdown`. `architect.md:224`, under `## Guardrails`, says *"You write one
markdown plan file. Nothing else."* `Nothing else` is categorical. That contradiction is BUG-059
Defect A, it is present identically in canonical, payload and live, and it is **open**. An Architect
that writes the rows anyway is choosing which of its two contradictory instructions to honour, on its
own authority, in order to produce a demonstration. That is precisely the interpretive leap the
Ambiguity Gate exists to catch, applied to the agent contract instead of a work item. It also sets
the worst possible precedent: the sprint that *found* the broken contract also *normalised* ignoring
it. The dispatch for this milestone independently narrows the write set to
`plans/M3.md` plus defect files, so the block is doubled.

**2. The demonstration would be vacuous, because it runs through the hole rather than through the
gate.** The loop's steps and their measured status: (1) template ships the section — **works**;
(2) author writes rows at drafting time — **works**, witnessed by BUG-058 at 7/7 with 6 rows;
(3) Architect writes rows — **broken**, Defect A; (4) Developer ticks in the same commit — **broken**
on Story waves, Defect B; (5) QA verifies — read-only, works; (6) gate enforces — **works**,
witnessed by a rendered template instance hard-failing
``## Task Breakdown is present but carries no `- [ ]` task rows``.

The *only* reason M3 is proposed as the venue is that `file_surface_diff.sh:96-104` globs
`STORY-<num>_*.md`, so `${story_id#STORY-}` leaves `BUG-043` intact, the glob
`STORY-BUG-043_*.md` matches nothing, and the script exits **0** with
`No active story file found`. In other words: step 4 is "unblocked" for M3 **because the enforcement
is switched off for Bug/CR ids**. Exercising a loop through a known hole in its own gate proves that
the hole exists — which is already filed — and proves nothing about the loop. It is the same
green-through-a-hole failure class this sprint spent six waves removing (FLASHCARD 2026-08-28
`#gate #danger`; and 2026-08-27 `#test-harness #gate #danger`, *"a run that asserted nothing
satisfies fail 0"*).

**3. The witness the sprint actually needs already exists, and cost nothing.** BUG-058 was authored
with six rows and passes `bug.ready-for-fix` 7/7 including `task-breakdown-complete` — the first
real work item authored against the new surface, end to end, through the *working* half of the
chain. A second, weaker witness produced by bypassing a Guardrail does not strengthen the sprint
report; it dilutes it. **BUG-060, filed by this dispatch, is a third such witness** — freshly
authored, 7 rows, gate-green — and it required no contract violation, because authoring a new item
with the section the template ships is step 2, not step 3.

**4. Retrofitting the section onto BUG-043 and CR-105 carries real, avoidable cost.** Both items are
gate-green **today** (`bug.ready-for-fix` 7/7, `cr.ready-to-apply` 9/9, measured with the local
dist), passing `task-breakdown-complete` as *not-applicable*. Adding the section converts a
guaranteed pass into a pass conditional on row content, immediately before dispatch, for zero
delivery value. And the edit would have to be made via heredoc to avoid firing the ingest hook into
the concurrent session's wiki pages — which leaves each item's `cached_gate_result` block stale
relative to its new body until someone re-runs the gate.

**If the orchestrator overrides**, the mechanics are fixed and verified independently here — do not
re-derive them:

- **BUG-043 → position 5**, immediately before `## 5. Verification Protocol` (currently line 150).
  Measured heading order: `1 §1 Anomaly · 2 §2 Repro · 3 §3 Evidence · 4 §4 Execution Sandbox ·
  5 §5 Verification · 6 Prior work · 7 Context Source · 8 Ambiguity Gate`.
  The only gated position is `bug.repro-steps-deterministic` = `section(2)`
  (`readiness-gates.md:208`) → `## 2. Reproduction Protocol`, which is **above** the insertion.
  Matches `Bug.md`'s own layout, where `## Task Breakdown` is position 5 (`Bug.md:102`).
- **CR-105 → position 7**, immediately before `## 4. Verification Protocol` (currently line 118).
  Measured heading order: `1 §0.5 · 2 §1 · 3 §2 Blast Radius · 4 Existing Surfaces · 5 Prior work ·
  6 §3 Execution Sandbox · 7 §4 Verification · 8 Context Source · 9 Ambiguity Gate`.
  Gated: `cr.blast-radius-populated` = `section(3)` (`:183`) and `cr.sandbox-paths-declared` =
  `section(6)` (`:187`) — both **above** the insertion. Any earlier insertion breaks a live
  criterion. Matches `CR.md`, where `## Task Breakdown` is position 7.
- **Heading text is `## Task Breakdown`, exactly.** M2 P5 measured eight variants: a lowercase `b`,
  `## Tasks`, a parenthetical suffix, or `###` all return *not-applicable* — **a silent pass with no
  failing message to notice.** Row grammar is `- [ ]` / `- [x]`, optional trailing
  `-> <requirement-id>`.
- Edit via heredoc, then re-run
  `node cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js gate check <file>` on both and refresh `cached_gate_result`.

**The right venue** is the first Story of the next sprint, after BUG-059 A and B land — where the
Architect is permitted to write the rows, the Developer's tick commit is *actually* gated, and a
green means something.

## Open decisions for orchestrator

1. **N1's mechanic deviates from the letter of a recorded human decision.** BUG-043's Q2 decision
   says *"the fix must normalise line endings and trim trailing whitespace on the marker line before
   matching."* N1 rules that tolerance goes in the pattern instead, because whole-file normalisation
   is the only way to "normalise before matching" on a write path and it silently rewrites the
   user's file — the harm class BUG-043 exists to close. The decision's stated *outcome* (CRLF and
   trailing-whitespace files keep matching) is delivered exactly. The premise the decision rested on
   is measurably false for JavaScript. **Reversible by the human; not blocking.** If reversed, the
   Developer must also decide what to do about rewriting a CRLF user file, which the decision does
   not address.
2. **CR-105 `§0.5` Q2's "Both `init` and `upgrade`" applies to unreachable code (N4).** Options:
   (a) implement the relocation on the take-theirs branch anyway, as defense-in-depth, and record it
   as latent; (b) skip the `upgrade` half and record why; (c) expand scope so `upgrade` handles root
   `CLAUDE.md` outside the manifest walk — a genuine behaviour change with its own blast radius.
   **This Architect will not choose (c).** Default if the human is silent: **(a)** — it is what the
   decision says, it costs ~15 lines, and it removes a landmine that arms the moment anyone adds a
   `CLAUDE.md` manifest row. Flagged because the recorded rationale ("none of the three known
   installs would ever receive the change without being re-initialised") is inverted by the
   mechanism, and the human may want to revisit it knowing that.
3. **BUG-043's `severity: P1-High` is not supported by the reachable behaviour (N4).** Defect A is
   latent; Defect B is live but bounded (`§1`: *"only text between the real END and the last stray
   END"*). Not the Architect's call — severity is a human field and re-grading it mid-sprint changes
   nothing about execution. Raised so the sprint report does not carry an unqualified "P1 data-loss
   bug fixed."
4. **Whether BUG-060 is scheduled into SPRINT-39 or deferred.** It is `approved: false` and there is
   no wave for it. It is not a blocker for either M3 item. Default: **defer**; it needs a human
   approval pass and one open decision resolved (shared helper vs. fix-in-place).
5. **Whether BUG-057 is scheduled before or after CR-105, or deferred.** See `## Cross-story risks`
   item 2. Default: **defer past M3**, and add the stale-line-citation note to its file at wave9
   merge.
6. **M3's milestone verdict language.** M3 cannot be judged against the Sprint Goal, because it does
   not serve it. Recommend the M3 post-flight report `GOAL_RELATION: off critical path` explicitly
   rather than emitting a `met`/`partial` verdict that implies a goal linkage.

## Measurement appendix — how to reproduce every number in this plan

```bash
R=/Users/ssuladze/Documents/Dev/ClearGate
SB=/tmp/m3-measure

# 1. out-of-tree cli mirror under a fake meta-root, so cross-repo reads resolve
rm -rf "$SB" && mkdir -p "$SB"
rsync -a --exclude node_modules --exclude dist --exclude .git "$R/cleargate-cli/" "$SB/cleargate-cli/"
ln -s "$R/cleargate-cli/node_modules" "$SB/cleargate-cli/node_modules"
ln -s "$R/cleargate-cli/dist"         "$SB/cleargate-cli/dist"
for d in cleargate-planning .cleargate .claude CLAUDE.md; do ln -s "$R/$d" "$SB/$d"; done

# 2. baseline — the seven CLAUDE.md-touching files
cd "$SB/cleargate-cli" && npx tsx --test \
  test/lib/claude-md-surgery.node.test.ts test/commands/init.node.test.ts \
  test/commands/init-from-source.node.test.ts test/commands/uninstall.node.test.ts \
  test/commands/doctor-drift-guard.node.test.ts test/scripts/template-claude-md.node.test.ts \
  test/commands/upgrade.node.test.ts
#   -> tests 107 · suites 47 · pass 107 · fail 0 · skipped 0

# 3. apply N2 + N3 + the upgrade refusal to the MIRROR only, re-run
#   -> tests 107 · pass 107 · fail 0 · skipped 0   AND  npx tsc --noEmit clean

# 4. apply CR-105's injectClaudeMd body on top, re-run
#   -> tests 107 · pass 106 · fail 1 · skipped 0
#      fail = init.node.test.ts "scenario 3: … appends bounded block, preserves user content"

# 5. drift matrix: three copies of drift-check.ts importing three copies of
#    claude-md-surgery.ts (today / strict-anchored / N2-tolerant), each run against
#    seven tmpdir fixtures. Table in "## Question 1".

# 6. manifest census
node -e "const m=require('$R/.cleargate/.install-manifest.json');console.log(m.files.length, m.files.filter(f=>f.path.endsWith('CLAUDE.md')).length)"   # 65 0
git -C "$R" show HEAD:cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json | node -e "let s='';process.stdin.on('data',d=>s+=d).on('end',()=>{const m=JSON.parse(s);console.log(m.files.length, m.files.filter(f=>f.path.endsWith('CLAUDE.md')).length)})"   # 70 0
git -C "$R" log --format=%h -- cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json | while read c; do git show $c:cleargate-planning/MANIFEST.json 2>/dev/null | grep -q '"path": "CLAUDE.md"' && echo "HIT $c"; done   # no output

# 7. JS LineTerminator proof
node -e "console.log(/^b\$/m.test('a\r\nb\r\nc'), /^b\$/m.test('a\rb\rc'))"   # true true

# 8. real-file marker positions
node -e "const fs=require('fs');for(const p of ['$R/CLAUDE.md','$R/cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md']){const L=fs.readFileSync(p,'utf8').split('\n');const f=re=>L.map((l,i)=>[l,i+1]).filter(([l])=>re.test(l)).map(([,n])=>n);console.log(p, 'START',f(/^<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->[ \t]*\$/), 'END',f(/^<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->[ \t]*\$/));}"
#   -> CLAUDE.md START [129] END [186] ; cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md START [7] END [64]

# 9. gate checks (NEVER the bare `cleargate` binary — M2 P3)
node "$R/cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js" gate check "$R/.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/BUG-043_*.md"   # 7/7
node "$R/cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js" gate check "$R/.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/CR-105_*.md"    # 9/9
node "$R/cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js" gate check "$R/.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/BUG-060_*.md"   # 7/7

# 10. hardcoded-path census
command grep -rn "/Users/ssuladze" "$R/cleargate-cli/src" "$R/cleargate-cli/test" "$R/cleargate-cli/scripts"
```

## Flashcards — PROPOSED, deliberately NOT written

`FLASHCARD.md` is contended by the concurrent session (` M` in the outer working tree). Following
the STORY-054-07 post-flight's discipline, these are proposed here and appended in one pass at
Gate 4, newest on top, after a dupe grep.

- `2026-08-28 · #regex #danger · JS ^/$ under /m ARE CRLF-safe — \r is a LineTerminator in ECMAScript, unlike Python's re.MULTILINE. A recorded decision demanded CRLF normalisation before matching on a false premise; implementing it literally would have rewritten every CRLF user file to LF on a write path whose whole purpose is not touching user bytes. Test the engine, don't port the folklore. [SPRINT-39 M3 / BUG-043]`
- `2026-08-28 · #danger #test-harness · Anchoring a regex while leaving its includes() guard makes replace() a SILENT NO-OP: uninstall would write the file back unchanged and report the block removed. When you narrow a regex, narrow every guard that gates it — a substring guard and an anchored pattern agree until the day they don't. [SPRINT-39 M3 / BUG-043]`
- `2026-08-28 · #dogfood-split #danger · upgrade's CLAUDE.md surgery branch has been DEAD since it was written: CLAUDE.md is INTENTIONALLY_UNTRACKED (build-manifest.ts:334), so no manifest in the repo's entire history has a CLAUDE.md row and isClaudeMd() never fires. A P1 "user's file gets overwritten" bug was traced, never executed. Trace-derived severity needs an execution witness. [SPRINT-39 M3 / BUG-043+CR-105]`
- `2026-08-28 · #test-harness #danger · "Exactly one block in the file" counted by substring is wrong for every ClearGate install: the shipped block quotes its own START/END inline (CLAUDE.md:178, canonical:56), so every installed repo has 2 of each, forever. Count ANCHORED marker LINES. [SPRINT-39 M3 / CR-105]`
- `2026-08-28 · #test-harness #cross-repo #danger · FOUR cleargate-cli test files hardcode /Users/ssuladze/... — 2 in the DEFAULT tier. `npm test` cannot pass on any other machine and nothing has ever contradicted that, because cleargate-cli has no CI. Green means "green here". [SPRINT-39 M3 / BUG-060]`
- `2026-08-28 · #gate #process · Dogfooding a loop through a hole in its own gate proves the hole, not the loop. M3 could have "exercised" the tick loop only because file_surface_diff.sh is inert for Bug/CR ids — and only by an Architect overriding its own Guardrail. Declined; the witness that counts (BUG-058, 6 rows, 7/7) came from the working half of the chain. [SPRINT-39 M3]`

> **ORCHESTRATOR RULINGS — M3 open decisions (2026-08-28).** All six resolved. Binding.
>
> **1. CRLF mechanic — N1's ruling STANDS, human-confirmed.** BUG-043's recorded Q2 decision
> ("normalise line endings and trim trailing whitespace on the marker line before matching")
> rests on a premise that is measurably false for JavaScript: `/^b$/m.test('a\r\nb\r\nc')` is
> **true** — `\r` is a LineTerminator in ECMAScript. Verified independently by the orchestrator.
> Normalising would mean whole-file rewriting on a write path, silently converting a user's CRLF
> `CLAUDE.md` to LF — the exact harm class BUG-043 exists to close. **Put the tolerance in the
> pattern (`[ \t]*$`); mutate nothing.** The decision's stated *outcome* is delivered exactly:
> CRLF files already match unaided, and trailing-whitespace files start matching. The Developer
> must record this deviation-from-literal-mechanic in its report and in the work item.
>
> **2. CR-105 Q2 dead branch — option (a), implement as latent defense-in-depth.** ~15 lines on
> the take-theirs branch, recorded as latent. It removes a landmine that arms the moment anyone
> adds a `CLAUDE.md` manifest row. Option (c) is explicitly refused. Note in the item that the
> recorded rationale is inverted by the mechanism N4 measured.
>
> **3. BUG-043 severity — stays `P1-High`, human-confirmed; the SPRINT REPORT must qualify it.**
> Defect A is latent-not-live (`CLAUDE.md` is in `INTENTIONALLY_UNTRACKED`, `build-manifest.ts:334`;
> **0** rows in the current 70-entry manifest, the 65-entry install snapshot, and every historical
> commit of `MANIFEST.json`). Defect B is live but bounded to text between the real END and a
> stray END. **No unqualified "P1 data-loss bug fixed" line may appear in the sprint report** —
> state both facts.
>
> **4. BUG-060 — deferred.** `approved: false`, no wave, blocks neither M3 item. Needs a human
> approval pass and its shared-helper-vs-fix-in-place decision.
>
> **5. BUG-057 — deferred past M3.** Add the stale-line-citation note to its file at wave-9 merge:
> its text cites root `CLAUDE.md:162`, which CR-105 renumbers to ~34, and its fix must edit
> **both** trees or bare `cleargate doctor` blocks on `claude-md-block-mismatch`.
>
> **6. M3 milestone verdict — `GOAL_RELATION: off critical path`.** No `met`/`partial` verdict;
> that framing would imply a goal linkage M3 does not have. M3 serves scaffold integrity and
> prompt-cache prefix stability. If M3 slips, the Sprint Goal is still met.

> `writeBlock(C, …)` changes the content without throwing. The table's `C -> NO-OP, silent` row models
> `^…$` **without** `[ \t]*`. **Only `D` (inline-only) and `G` (indented) diverge.** The third guard
> stays mandatory — D and G alone justify it — but any code or test that makes `C` throw is a
> kick-back. **Do not "fix" QA-Red's two C tests to match the old table.**

> identical `ours`, so `ourBlock === null` strictly precedes every `NOT_ANCHORED` condition and
> `writeBlock` is not reached for an unanchored `ours`. **Keep the `catch` verbatim** — it is the
> "no code path may substitute the payload" guarantee — but **do not author a test asserting the
> catch route fires.** Scenario 10 stands as QA-Red wrote it: same refusal route as scenario 9,

> the file; it matches neither `ours` nor `theirs`).

> **TPV RULING — CR-105 (Architect, 2026-08-29).** Verdict: **`TPV: rulings-required`**. Binding on
> the wave-9 Developer and QA-Verify. Supersedes post-flight **P5**'s red-set claim and this plan's
> CR-105 blast-radius table where they conflict; everything else in the POST-FLIGHT and ORCHESTRATOR
> blocks stands. Every number was measured in an out-of-tree mirror of `cleargate-cli` @ `851d4df`
> (mutants destroyed; both repos verified untouched). Full evidence:
> `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/CR-105-tpv.md`.
>
> **Headline — three holes, before the detail.** (1) **M5**, an implementation that decides "does a
> block exist" by counting START **substrings**, survives all 250 tests in the 23 affected files with
> **zero witnesses** and stacks a second block on every `init` (measured on this repo's own root
> `CLAUDE.md`: 187 → 246 → 305 → 364 lines, anchoredSTART 1 → 2 → 3 → 4, not idempotent).
> (2) **M6b**, the outer relocation dropping a line of user prose, survives with **zero witnesses**.
> (3) The `§0.5` Q1 relocation notice — a recorded human decision — has **zero witnesses**: the
> otherwise-correct implementation with the notice deleted is `pass 248 · fail 0`.
>
> **Measured ladder (23 files / 250 tests / 88 suites — a complete import census, validated against
> the full mirror suite, which shows no collateral anywhere in 2500+ tests):**
> `base 250/240/8/2` → `commit 2 (cli) 250/245/3/2` → `commit 3 (outer) 250/247/1/2`.
> The `base` 8 are exactly QA-Red's 8 — zero collateral in the red baseline.
>
> **T1 — M5 SURVIVED. Two tests are required in `test/init/claude-md-block-leads.red.node.test.ts`.**
> No fixture in the file feeds a `CLAUDE.md` whose **existing** block is the real shipped one;
> scenario 8 passes the real block as the *argument* only. Add, reusing the file's own
> `CANONICAL_CLAUDE_MD` and `extractBlock`:
> ```ts
> test('8b: a file that ALREADY contains the REAL block is stripped, not stacked — the strip decision must not be made by substring count (N9, implementation side)', () => {
>   const realBlock = extractBlock(fs.readFileSync(CANONICAL_CLAUDE_MD, 'utf8'));
>   const existing = `# My Project\n\nUser prose.\n\n${realBlock}\n`;
>   assert.strictEqual(existing.split(CLEARGATE_START).length - 1, 2, 'precondition: the real block quotes its own START inline, so a substring count reads 2');
>   const output = injectClaudeMd(existing, realBlock);
>   assert.strictEqual(countAnchoredLines(output, CLEARGATE_START), 1, 'the pre-existing real block must be stripped, not stacked');
>   assert.strictEqual(countAnchoredLines(output, CLEARGATE_END), 1);
>   assert.ok(output.startsWith(CLEARGATE_START));
>   assert.ok(output.includes('User prose.'));
>   assert.strictEqual(injectClaudeMd(output, realBlock), output, 'and idempotent');
> });
>
> test("8c: this repo's own root CLAUDE.md round-trips — one anchored block, no growth, idempotent", () => {
>   const realBlock = extractBlock(fs.readFileSync(CANONICAL_CLAUDE_MD, 'utf8'));
>   const root = fs.readFileSync(path.join(REPO_ROOT, 'CLAUDE.md'), 'utf8');
>   const out1 = injectClaudeMd(root, realBlock);
>   assert.strictEqual(countAnchoredLines(out1, CLEARGATE_START), 1);
>   assert.ok(out1.startsWith(CLEARGATE_START));
>   assert.strictEqual(injectClaudeMd(out1, realBlock), out1, 'idempotent on the real file');
>   assert.ok(out1.split('\n').length <= root.split('\n').length + 2, 'no line growth');
>   assert.ok(out1.includes('# ClearGate Meta-Repo'), "the repo's own H1 survives");
> });
> ```
> Measured across four states: **2 red on `base`** (both `startsWith`), **0 red under the correct
> implementation**, **6 red under M5**, and they redundantly kill M1, M4 and M6. Both belong in
> commit 2a (test-only) so the red is visible.
>
> **T2 — M6b SURVIVED. One test is required in
> `test/docs/claude-md-block-leads-relocation.red.node.test.ts`.** Kick-back criterion 12 ("any
> content lost or reordered … `git diff -U0` must show a pure relocation") has no machine backstop in
> the one commit that rewrites a 186-line file the user owns. Assertions 1–3 pin where the block IS
> and that its body is unedited; nothing pins what SURVIVES around it.
> ```ts
> const OUTSIDE_HEADINGS = [
>   '# ClearGate Meta-Repo',
>   '## Product vision in one line',
>   '## Repo layout',
>   '## Dogfood split — canonical vs live',
>   '## How work gets done',
>   '## Flashcard protocol (mandatory)',
>   '## Test + commit conventions',
>   '## Deploy targets',
>   '## Active state (as of 2026-04-18)',
>   '## Stack versions (canonical — see INDEX.md for full table)',
>   '## Guardrails for the conversational agent (me)',
> ];
>
> test('4 (GREEN today, MUST STAY GREEN): relocation is a PURE MOVE — every line of the repo\'s own prose outside the block survives, in order', { skip: !fs.existsSync(ROOT_CLAUDE_MD) }, () => {
>   const lines = fs.readFileSync(ROOT_CLAUDE_MD, 'utf8').split('\n');
>   const s = lines.findIndex((l) => l.trimEnd() === '<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->');
>   const e = lines.findIndex((l) => l.trimEnd() === '<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->');
>   const outside = [...lines.slice(0, s), ...lines.slice(e + 1)].filter((l) => l.trim() !== '');
>   assert.strictEqual(outside[0], '# ClearGate Meta-Repo', 'the repo H1 must remain the first line of the surviving prose');
>   let idx = -1;
>   for (const h of OUTSIDE_HEADINGS) {
>     const i = outside.indexOf(h);
>     assert.ok(i > idx, `heading missing or reordered by the relocation: ${JSON.stringify(h)}`);
>     idx = i;
>   }
>   assert.ok(outside.length >= 90, `expected ~97 non-empty lines of prose outside the block; got ${outside.length} — the relocation lost content`);
> });
> ```
> Measured: **green** on today's un-relocated tree, **green** after a correct relocation (97 non-empty
> lines both times), **2 of 3 assertions red under M6b**. Green-by-design on the baseline is correct
> here — FLASHCARD 2026-08-28 `#test-harness #qa #danger`: a gap-closing test pins a rejected future
> mutant, not a current defect.
>
> **T3 — the `§0.5` Q1 notice has no witness. Implementation AND assertion, both verbatim, both
> measured.** Discharges P7(d) and kick-back criterion 10. In `src/commands/init.ts`, replacing the
> `else if` branch at `:392-393` (add
> `import { CLEARGATE_START, CLEARGATE_END } from '../lib/claude-md-surgery.js';` — the marker
> constants must never be re-typed):
> ```ts
>   } else if (existingClaudeMd !== newClaudeMd) {
>     stdout(`[cleargate init] Updated CLAUDE.md (bounded block injected/replaced)\n`);
>     // CR-105 §0.5 Q1 (recorded human decision, 2026-08-26): the block relocates ONCE and the
>     // run says so — how many lines of user content now follow it, and why.
>     const firstNonEmpty = (text: string): string =>
>       (text.split('\n').find((l) => l.trim() !== '') ?? '').trimEnd();
>     const ledBefore = firstNonEmpty(existingClaudeMd) === CLEARGATE_START;
>     const ledAfter = firstNonEmpty(newClaudeMd) === CLEARGATE_START;
>     if (!ledBefore && ledAfter) {
>       const claudeMdLines = newClaudeMd.split('\n');
>       const endLineIdx = claudeMdLines.findIndex((l) => l.trimEnd() === CLEARGATE_END);
>       const following =
>         endLineIdx === -1
>           ? 0
>           : claudeMdLines.slice(endLineIdx + 1).filter((l) => l.trim() !== '').length;
>       stdout(
>         `[cleargate init] Moved the ClearGate block to the top of CLAUDE.md; ${following} lines of ` +
>           `your content now follow it. Why: prompt-cache prefix stability — the block changes only on ` +
>           `upgrade, so it must physically precede your more volatile prose.\n`
>       );
>     }
>   }
> ```
> The `!ledBefore && ledAfter` guard is load-bearing: it fires on the run that MOVES the block and
> stays silent on every idempotent re-run, which is what "relocate once, print a notice" means. The
> **first anchored END line** locates the boundary — `indexOf(CLEARGATE_END)` would be wrong the day
> a user quotes the marker in prose, and the shipped block already quotes it inline at
> `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md:56`. Counting **non-empty** lines is the specified semantics.
> Matching test, in scenario 10's `describe` so it inherits `tmpDir`:
> ```ts
> test('11: the relocation notice — init emits ONE line naming how many lines of user content now follow the block, and why (CR-105 §0.5 Q1)', async () => {
>   fs.writeFileSync(path.join(tmpDir, 'CLAUDE.md'), '# My Project\n\nUser content here.\n', 'utf8');
>   const out: string[] = [];
>   await initHandler({ cwd: tmpDir, payloadDir: META_ROOT_PLANNING, stdout: (s: string) => out.push(s), stderr: () => {} });
>
>   const notices = out.filter((l) => l.includes('Moved the ClearGate block to the top of CLAUDE.md'));
>   assert.strictEqual(notices.length, 1, `expected exactly one relocation notice; got: ${JSON.stringify(out)}`);
>   const m = notices[0].match(/(\d+) lines of your content now follow it/);
>   assert.ok(m, `the notice must name how many lines of user content follow the block; got: ${JSON.stringify(notices[0])}`);
>   assert.strictEqual(Number(m[1]), 2, 'the fixture leaves two non-empty lines of user content below the block');
>   assert.match(notices[0], /prompt-cache prefix stability/, 'the notice must say WHY, per the recorded decision');

>   assert.strictEqual(out2.filter((l) => l.includes('Moved the ClearGate block')).length, 0, 'the notice fires only on the run that actually moves the block');
> });
> ```
> Executed end-to-end through the real `initHandler`: run 1 emits exactly one notice reading
> `"…; 2 lines of your content now follow it. Why: prompt-cache prefix stability — …"`; run 2 emits
> **0**; on `base` run 1 emits **0** (red). The Developer may improve the prose but not the three
> asserted properties, and must change the test in the same commit if the wording moves.

> inverted in commit 1) and `upgrade-claude-md.red.node.test.ts` scenario 11.** Nothing else, at

> — latent defense-in-depth is not defense without a test, and scenario 11 is what will speak on the
> day a `CLAUDE.md` manifest row is added; scenario 11 is the **only** test that reaches the write at

> this branch's first-ever coverage precisely because a traced-never-executed defect needs an

> test('scenario 11 (CR-105: the block leads): both well-formed, choice=t -> block RELOCATED to the top, body replaced, prose preserved below', async () => {
>   const { finalContent } = await runUpgrade(OURS_WELL_FORMED);

>   assert.ok(finalContent.indexOf(CLEARGATE_START) < finalContent.indexOf('# My Project'), 'the block must precede all user prose');
>   assert.ok(finalContent.includes('# My Project'), 'prose that was above the block must survive, below it now');
>   assert.ok(finalContent.includes('My own rules.'), 'prose that was above the block must survive');
>   assert.ok(finalContent.includes('## Footer'), 'prose below the block must survive');
>   assert.ok(finalContent.includes('NEW SCAFFOLD'), 'block body must be replaced with the payload');
>   assert.ok(!finalContent.includes('OLD SCAFFOLD'), 'old block body must be gone');
>   assert.strictEqual(finalContent.split('\n').filter((l) => l.trimEnd() === CLEARGATE_START).length, 1, 'exactly one anchored block');
> });
> ```
> The last assertion uses a regex-free line filter deliberately (T7) — do **not** import or re-copy
> `countAnchoredLines` into this file. **The inversion lands in commit 2a (test-only), so the eviction
> is visible as a red** — the same discipline the plan applied to `init.node.test.ts` scenario 3.

> **T7 — M9: `countAnchoredLines` IS a marker-matching pattern. Not a kick-back; the grammar is
> DELETABLE and must be deleted.** `claude-md-block-leads.red.node.test.ts:44-48` builds
> `new RegExp('^' + escaped + '[ \\t]*$', 'gm')` — the single-marker half of BUG-043's convention,
> `[ \t]*` decision included, byte-identical to `claude-md-anchoring.red.node.test.ts:304-308`. It is
> a fourth instance of the **marker-line** grammar but not of the **block-span** grammar CR-113 names,
> and that distinction holds only until someone changes the tolerance — at which point both test
> copies keep asserting the old convention and stay green. **Nothing in the tree detects this:** N7's
> shared-corpus probe compares `hasAnchoredBlock` against `extractBlock`, both from `src/`, and is
> structurally blind to a test-local copy; `check:no-inline-id-regex` is scoped to work-item ids;
> there is no marker-grammar gate; and `cleargate-cli` has zero installed hooks anyway. Required
> remedy — replace the body of **both** copies with a regex-free line filter:
> ```ts
> function countAnchoredLines(content: string, marker: string): number {
>   return content.split('\n').filter((l) => l.trimEnd() === marker).length;
> }
> ```
> Verified **exactly equivalent** over 10 fixtures × 2 markers — LF, CRLF, trailing-whitespace,
> indented, inline-only, stray-END, no-final-newline, two-block, and both real `CLAUDE.md` files —
> **20/20 agreement, zero disagreements.** This deletes the grammar rather than deduplicating it, and
> is why the copy is not a kick-back. **Widen [[CR-113]]'s scope note to "one marker grammar,
> including the marker-line form used by tests"**; it is `approved: false` and deferred, so this is a
> one-line edit to the item, not sprint work.

> — it does not accumulate — and no fixture asserts byte-equality of the remainder, so nothing reds.

> pre-release it appends under the existing `## Unreleased` heading and must not open a second one.
>
> **Q5.3 — nothing invalidated or blocked; one item provably incomplete, and M3 is its
> counter-example.** **CR-110** ("the sprint goal gets an acceptance check") fixes the Reporter's
> vocabulary at `met | partial | missed`, plus `not-mechanically-verifiable`. **There is no slot for a
> milestone that serves no clause of the goal**, which is what M3 is and why this ruling had to coin
> `GOAL_RELATION: off critical path`. Under CR-110 as drafted, M3 would be forced into `met` /
> `partial` / `missed` against a goal it does not serve — the manufactured linkage O5 forbade in this
> same sprint. The two terms are orthogonal: `not-mechanically-verifiable` is about *how* a goal is
> checked; `off critical path` is about *whether the work relates to it*. Route as a scope question for
> CR-110 with SPRINT-39 M3 as the worked example — a vocabulary addition, cheapest before execution.
> Lighter: **BUG-046**'s worktree finding held (both M3 halves ran in main checkouts; `git worktree
> list` shows one entry) — M3 is a confirming witness, not a counter-example. **CR-111** inherits the
> M2 close state exactly: M3 inserted no template heading, `gate-section-index-pinning` is `14/14`, the
> three BUG-042 indices are unmoved. **No M4 wave may verify through `dist/cli.js`** until the Gate-4
> rebuild.
>
> **Gate-4 obligations this close hands forward.** (1) Rebuild `dist/` and **confirm** with four
> greps, do not assume. (2) Rename `## Unreleased` → `## [X.Y.Z] — <ISO date>` in the same commit as
> the `package.json` bump if releasing, and add that line to `sprint-closeout-checklist.md` §2.
> (3) `MANIFEST.json` regeneration, DevOps, coordinated with the concurrent session. (4) Append the six
> proposed flashcards (in `CR-105-arch-postflight.md` §Flashcards) after a dupe grep — `FLASHCARD.md`
> was forbidden to this dispatch. (5) BUG-057's stale-citation note is already applied and
> arithmetically confirmed; nothing further. (6) Fix `upgrade-claude-md.red.node.test.ts:10`'s new
> off-by-one (`:368-388` → `:369-389`), comment-only.
>
> **M3 CLOSED.** Both items pass. No open decision remains from the M3 set: 1 and 3 were resolved by
> the human, 2 was closed as (a) by O2 and shipped, 4 and 5 are deferred per O4, 6 is discharged by
> this ruling's `GOAL_RELATION` line.

## NORMATIVE RULINGS — bind all eight items; supersede the item text where they conflict

### N1 — `.claude/**` live paths are NOT a committable surface. Canonical is primary, always.

`git ls-files .claude/` returns **0**. `git check-ignore -v .claude/agents/architect-synth.md` →
`.gitignore:13:/.claude/`. The live tree is fully untracked as of CR-099 and cannot be staged
without a forbidden `git add -f`.

**Ruling.** For every M4 item that names a `.claude/**` path:

- The **primary, committable** surface is `cleargate-planning/.claude/<same-path>`.
- The **live** `.claude/<same-path>` is a Gate-4 re-sync obligation, not a story deliverable.
- A Developer who edits only the live copy produces a commit containing **nothing**.

Affected: **BUG-046** (`architect-reader.md`, `architect-synth.md`, `sprint-execution/SKILL.md`),
**CR-107** (`SKILL.md`), **CR-110** (`SKILL.md`, `reporter.md`), **CR-111** (`developer.md`,
`qa.md`, `SKILL.md`). All four declare the live path first and the mirror second — the
**inversion** mode STORY-054-07 shipped (`M2.md:773`). Flipping the labels is the whole fix.

**Exception, and it is the only one:** `.cleargate/**` paths (scripts, knowledge, templates,
config) ARE tracked in the outer repo. `git ls-files .cleargate/scripts/` → 59;
`cleargate-planning/.cleargate/scripts/` → 39. Both are primary; Cross-Cutting Rule 1 applies to
`knowledge/` and `templates/`, and the same-commit discipline is required for `scripts/` by every
item's own `## Parity check`.

### N2 — `.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md` HAS DRIFTED. Every live line number in an M4 item is wrong for the tree you will commit.

Measured 2026-08-29: live **777** lines, canonical **787**. `diff` is exactly two hunks, both
canonical-only, both from STORY-054-03 (`afdf7feb`), whose Gate-4 live re-sync never happened:

```
canonical :100-108   ### 2.1 Spikes run before the loop, not inside it   (9 lines incl. blank)
canonical :775       - **Spike charter, timebox and gate criteria** -> ...  (1 line)
```

**Offset: canonical = live + 9 for lines 100–765; canonical = live + 10 for lines ≥ 766.**

Verified conversions for every SKILL.md citation in the eight items:

| Cited in | live | canonical | Text (verified identical in both) |
|---|---|---|---|
| CR-106 §1 | `:252` | **`:261`** | `**Idempotent segments.** … belt-and-suspenders safety net…` |
| BUG-046 §1 | `:277` | **`:286`** | `…visible as a subdirectory of the outer worktree.` |
| CR-107 §1 | `:445` | **`:454`** | `**Serial barrier merge.**…` |
| CR-107 §1 | `:714` | **`:723`** | `git merge sprint/S-NN --no-ff -m "Sprint S-NN: <goal>"` |

**Ruling.** Locate every SKILL.md edit by **heading title and sentence text**, never by line
number. Do not "fix" the live copy to match canonical mid-sprint — that is CR-099's forbidden
re-add and the M1 wave5-prep note (*"all three copies byte-identical at 777"*) is now stale, so the
Gate-4 re-sync is a **judgement call, not a clean copy**, and must be surfaced. Add it to the
Gate-4 list.

The **M1 wave5-prep amendment's** stated precondition — *"All three copies of `sprint-execution/SKILL.md`
are byte-identical today at 777 lines … re-verify immediately before Gate 4; if they have diverged
by then, the re-sync becomes a judgement call and must be surfaced"* — **has now fired.** This is
that surfacing.

### N3 — `.cleargate/scripts/state-scripts.test.mjs` has ONE PRE-EXISTING FAILURE and NO AUTOMATIC RUNNER. Both are load-bearing for BUG-044 and CR-106.

Measured on a clean `sprint/S-39` @ `5a33eae5`, `git status --porcelain` empty for both files:

```
node --test .cleargate/scripts/state-scripts.test.mjs
  tests 8 · suites 6 · pass 7 · fail 1 · cancelled 0 · skipped 0 · todo 0

FAIL  state-scripts.test.mjs:77  "creates state.json with schema_version=1, …"
      AssertionError at :90   schema_version must be 1   ->  3 !== 1
```

Cause, traced: `state-scripts.test.mjs:90` asserts `state.schema_version === 1`;
`init_sprint.mjs:221` writes `SCHEMA_VERSION`, which `constants.mjs:31` defines as `3`. The test
has not been touched since `588aff2a` (STORY-013-02); `b87f6ac0` (STORY-070-01) bumped the schema
to v3 without updating it. **This is the exact stale-assertion class this sprint exists to remove,
sitting in the acceptance harness of two M4 items.**

Second measurement: **nothing invokes this file.** `grep -rn "state-scripts"` across
`.cleargate/scripts/`, `.claude/`, `cleargate-planning/`, `cleargate-cli/package.json`,
`cleargate-cli/scripts/`, `.github/` returns exactly two hits, both inside the file's own docstring
(`:2`, `:6`). `config.yml:26` `gates.precommit` runs the **cli** suite. The only installed hook is
`.claude/hooks/pre-commit-surface-gate.sh` (via the `.git/hooks/pre-commit` dispatcher symlink).

**Ruling, three parts:**

1. **BUG-044 fixes `:90` in its FIRST commit, before the red test lands.** The file is a declared
   BUG-044 surface. Leaving it makes the acceptance signal `fail 1`, which is indistinguishable
   from "the new red test is still red".
2. **The corrected assertion is two lines, not one.** Do NOT write `assert.ok(state.schema_version)`
   and do NOT write `assert.strictEqual(state.schema_version, SCHEMA_VERSION)` alone — the second
   is self-fulfilling and would have hidden the v3 bump exactly as the literal hid it. Write both:
   `assert.strictEqual(state.schema_version, SCHEMA_VERSION)` (the contract) **and**
   `assert.strictEqual(SCHEMA_VERSION, 3)` (the deliberate pin, which the next bump must break on
   purpose). Import `SCHEMA_VERSION` from `./constants.mjs`.
3. **Acceptance for BUG-044 and CR-106 is three numbers, run by hand, reported verbatim:**
   `node --test .cleargate/scripts/state-scripts.test.mjs` → `pass N · fail 0 · skipped 0`.
   Both items' reports MUST carry that line. Neither may claim "the suite is green" without it.

### N4 — The surface gate is INERT for all eight M4 items. This plan is the only control.

FLASHCARD 2026-08-28 `#gate #danger` (BUG-059): `file_surface_diff.sh` globs `STORY-<num>_*.md`
only, so a `BUG-`/`CR-` active item resolves to `STORY-BUG-044_*.md`, finds nothing, and exits 0.
**Every M4 item is `BUG-` or `CR-`.** So the mechanical check that caught STORY-054-06's and
STORY-054-07's undeclared surfaces **cannot fire once in M4**.

Consequence: the surface-declaration defects reported per item below are not advisory. They are
the entire enforcement, and QA-Verify must read the diff against the corrected surface in this
plan, not against the item's `§3`/`§4`.

Cross-Cutting Rule 6 compounds it for the two cli-touching items (BUG-045, CR-108): commits made
inside `cleargate-cli/` bypass every hook. Those two must run `npm --prefix cleargate-cli run
typecheck` and the full suite by hand and report both numbers.

Rule 4 names **CR-111** as a heading-adder. That expectation is superseded. Ruling per item:

**Therefore, for all of M4: `EXPECTED_HEADINGS` needs ZERO edits, S1a's `18`/`16` are unchanged,
S6's `16` is unchanged, and `gate-section-index-pinning` must stay `18 / 18 / 0 / 0`.**
Per the STORY-054-05 post-flight fact carried in `sprint-context.md`: *"Opening the fixture 'to

### N7 — Citations decay. Re-measure after your own edit, in your own commit.

FLASHCARD 2026-08-29 `#citations #process`. Four M4 items carry citations that this plan has

## §Q5-A — CR-105 post-flight §Q5.2 resolved: the CR-108 collision

**All three consequences confirmed and measured. All three are handled in CR-108's blueprint.**

1. **CR-108's `## Prior work` line 92 is FALSE.** It reads *"[[CR-105]], [[BUG-043]] — also
   SPRINT-39, also two-tree template/marker edits, but on `CLAUDE.md` handling rather than the
   templates themselves. **No overlap.**"* Its own `§3` declares `CLAUDE.md` (root +
   `cleargate-planning/`) — exactly the pair `71037e5a` rewrote. **File-level overlap is real.**
   Correct the sentence to name the overlap and the invariant it inherits (the `block-equal`
   check). This is the stale-prose class this sprint removes, sitting in an unexecuted item.

2. **Target line positions, re-derived this dispatch, not inherited:**

   ```
   root CLAUDE.md                     186 lines · block :1-58
     :33  - Use the templates in `.cleargate/templates/` (…, `spike.md`).   <- CR-108's target
     :34  - Save drafts to `.cleargate/delivery/pending-sync/{TYPE}-{ID}-{Name}.md`.  <- BUG-057's
   cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md        64 lines · block :7-64
     :39  (same sentence, byte-identical)
     :40  (same sentence, byte-identical)
   ```

   Both inside the bounded block. BUG-057 is `approved: false` and in no wave — **no in-sprint
   collision.** If both ever run they collide on adjacent lines of one list.

3. **The two-tree hash coupling binds CR-108 and nothing warns at test time.** An edit inside root
   `CLAUDE.md:1-58` not mirrored into `cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md:7-64` sets
   `outcome.blocker = true` on bare `cleargate doctor` (`drift-check.ts:118-128` → `doctor.ts:279`).
   The only block-equality assertion in the tree is CR-105's doc-truth file, which will catch it —
   but only if someone runs it.

   **The `block-equal` one-liner, verbatim, for CR-108's verification protocol — and read the
   warning under it, because I got this wrong once on purpose to measure it:**

   ```bash
   node -e '
   const fs=require("fs");
   const RE=/^<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->$([\s\S]*?)^<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->$/m;
   const g=p=>{const m=RE.exec(fs.readFileSync(p,"utf8")); if(!m) throw new Error("no anchored block: "+p); return m[1];};
   const a=g("CLAUDE.md"), b=g("cleargate-planning/CLAUDE.md");
   console.log("block-equal:", a===b, a.length, "/", b.length);'
   ```

   Expected today: `block-equal: true 11762 / 11762`.

   **The anchors are load-bearing and their absence is silent.** The same one-liner written with an
   UNANCHORED `/<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->([\s\S]*?)<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->/` returns
   `block-equal: true 10606 / 10606` — because `CLAUDE.md:50` mentions
   `` `<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->...<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->` `` inside the "Project overrides" prose,
   so the non-greedy match terminates 8 lines early on **both** files. It still prints `true`.
   This is BUG-043's defect reproduced live, in the very check meant to guard against it, and it
   **passes** — so a Developer who writes the quick version gets a green that measures the wrong
   block. Use the anchored form; assert the `11762` length as well as the equality.

## §Q5-B — CR-105 post-flight §Q5.3 resolved: the CR-110 vocabulary question

**RULING: in scope, but NOT as CR-110's §1 would have it — the two vocabularies are on different
axes and must stay separate.**

The post-flight is right that `met | partial | missed` has no slot for M3, and M4 is about to be a
second instance across all eight items. But widening the **sprint verdict** with a **milestone
relation** would be a category error:

- `met | partial | missed | not-mechanically-verifiable` answers *"was the sprint's goal
  achieved, and could that be checked mechanically?"* — one verdict, one per sprint.
- `advances | off critical path` answers *"does this unit of work relate to the goal at all?"* —
  one relation, one per milestone, N per sprint.

A sprint whose goal is `met` can contain milestones that are `off critical path`. SPRINT-39 is
exactly that. Folding the second into the first would force the Reporter to score the *sprint*
`partial` because a *milestone* was unrelated — manufacturing precisely the linkage O5 forbade.

**Therefore CR-110 ships two things, not one:**

1. The sprint verdict vocabulary stays `met | partial | missed`, plus CR-110's own
   `not-mechanically-verifiable` for the check itself. Sites: canonical `SKILL.md` §0.5 touchpoint
   5, §E.2's Brief line, §E.2's 🎯 note.
2. A **new, separate, per-milestone** line: `GOAL_RELATION: advances | off critical path`, emitted
   by the Architect in each milestone plan and quoted by the Reporter in §1. Site: `reporter.md`
   (which is where CR-110's `reporter.md` row belongs — see the CR-110 blueprint, finding F1).

Cost: one enum, one line of Reporter instruction, and CR-110 §4 case 3 already asserts that a
literal token is accepted as populated — asserting a second literal is the same test with a
different string. **SPRINT-39 M3 and M4 are the worked example; put them in the CR body.**

### Source change — the lock, and the two things that will be got wrong

Human decision (§Open Questions): **lockfile**, not a shard. The shard is CR-106's.

**Shape:**

```
lockPath  = `${stateFile}.lock`
acquire   = fs.openSync(lockPath, 'wx')     // O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_WRONLY — atomic create-or-EEXIST
payload   = JSON.stringify({ pid: process.pid, at: new Date().toISOString() })
release   = fs.unlinkSync(lockPath)
```

`'wx'` is the load-bearing flag. `'w'` truncates-or-creates and provides no exclusion.

**GOTCHA 1 — `process.exit()` does NOT run `finally`. A `finally`-only release leaks the lock on
the most common path in the file.** `update_state.mjs` calls `process.exit()` **thirteen** times:
`:42` (usage), `:94`, `:102`, `:110`, `:130`, `:135`, `:146`, `:166`, `:184`, `:201`, `:223`, and —
critically — **`:229`, the idempotency no-op**, which is the single most frequently taken exit in
normal operation (`No-op: <id> is already in state "<state>"`). Node's `process.exit()` emits the
`'exit'` event and then calls `reallyExit`; it does **not** unwind the stack, so `finally` blocks do
not run. Release MUST be registered as `process.on('exit', () => { try { fs.unlinkSync(lockPath) }
catch {} })`, installed immediately after a successful acquire. A `finally` block alone ships a
tool that self-deadlocks on its second no-op.

**GOTCHA 2 — the read at `:99` and BOTH migration writes must be inside the critical section.**
`:99` is the read half of the race. But `:114-117` (`migrateV1ToV2` + `atomicWrite`) and `:120-123`
(`migrateStateToV3` + `atomicWrite`) are **two more full-document writes that happen before the
action dispatch**. A lock that wraps only the action-branch `atomicWrite` calls (`:155`, `:176`,
`:193`, `:210`, `:241`) leaves two unguarded writers. The critical section is `:97` through the end
of `main()` — acquire immediately after `resolveStateFile()` (`:90`) and the `existsSync` guard
(`:92-95`), which is before the first read.

**GOTCHA 3 — no synchronous sleep exists in Node's fs API.** The retry spin needs
`Atomics.wait(new Int32Array(new SharedArrayBuffer(4)), 0, 0, ms)` — the standard synchronous
sleep. `setTimeout` is unusable: `main()` is synchronous top-to-bottom (`:246`).

**Stale-lock policy (§2 edge condition 3, §5 case 4).** Two independent gates, both required:
- **Liveness:** read `pid` from the lock payload; `process.kill(pid, 0)` throws `ESRCH` when the
  process is gone → steal. Note it throws `EPERM` when the process exists but is owned by another
  user — **`EPERM` means alive, do not steal.**
- **Age ceiling:** a lock older than a fixed budget is stealable regardless. Pick a value and state
  it in the code comment; a bare liveness check fails when the pid has been recycled.

**Single-invocation performance (§2 edge condition 3).** One `openSync` + one `unlinkSync` per run.
Do not add a `statSync` on the happy path.

| # | Scenario | Mutant it must kill |
|---|---|---|
| S0 | (commit A) `init_sprint` writes `schema_version === SCHEMA_VERSION` **and** `SCHEMA_VERSION === 3` | replacing the literal with `assert.ok(...)`, or with the import alone — both make the assertion self-fulfilling and would have hidden the v3 bump |
| S1 | **The red.** 20 concurrent `spawn`s, 20 distinct story ids, one `Bouncing` each, via `Promise.all` over `child_process.spawn` (never shell `&`) → all 20 transitions present. Uses `Bouncing`, not `--state` (D1). | `'wx'` → `'w'`; lock acquired **after** the `readFileSync` at `:99`; lock wrapping only the action-branch writes and not `:116`/`:122` |
| S2 | Two concurrent invocations against the **same** story id → file is valid JSON, one write wins, neither corrupts | releasing the lock before `renameSync` |
| S3 | **Idempotent no-op leaves no lock.** Transition a story to `Bouncing`, then run the identical command again (hits `:227-229`, exits 0 with `No-op:`), then assert `!fs.existsSync(stateFile + '.lock')` — and that a **third** invocation succeeds without hanging | **release via `finally` instead of `process.on('exit')`** (Gotcha 1). This is the mutant that ships a self-deadlocking tool and passes S1, S2, S4 and S5. |
| S4 | **Stale lock, dead pid.** Write a lock payload with a pid that is not running; assert the next invocation steals it and succeeds within the retry budget | removing the liveness check (deadlock) — **and** removing the *guard* on the steal, i.e. stealing unconditionally, which re-reds S1 |
| S5 | **Live lock is respected.** Write a lock payload with `process.pid` (this test's own, definitionally alive); assert the invocation does NOT steal it and exits non-zero within the budget rather than proceeding | treating every existing lock as stale; treating `EPERM` from `process.kill(pid,0)` as "dead" |
| S6 | Existing scenarios 1–6 stay green | — |

S3, S4 and S5 are the three the item's §5 does not fully specify; S3 is the highest-value case in
the milestone and nothing in the item text implies it.

That try/catch is §2 edge condition 1 (*"archive absent → treat as empty"*), already written.

### Blast radius — measured

Gates every story dispatch in every sprint, and ships to every install. Current exposure measured
by the item and re-confirmed: 9 of 15 in-flight items reference `cleargate-cli/src` paths; SPRINT-39
places at most one such item per wave, so it is **safe by planning, not by enforcement**.

### QA kick-back criteria

1. Any live `.claude/**` path appears in `git diff --name-only` (impossible without `git add -f` —
   its presence proves a forbidden flag was used).
2. C3 absent, or passing against an `existsSync`-based classifier.
3. C12 absent — the scoping decision is unpinned and a future wave plan can be retroactively voided.
4. C9's fixture path contains no hyphen.
5. `dep_predecessors` added as a `## ` heading rather than a frontmatter key (N6).
6. `gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts` is not `18 / 18 / 0 / 0`.
7. Any mirrored file diverges from its `cleargate-planning/` counterpart.
8. `file_surface_diff.sh` touched (BUG-052's).

### Gotchas — file:line, non-obvious only

- `collision_surface.sh:104` — `for (c=1; c<=n; c++)` scans **column 1**, the label column. That is
  why §3.5(d) exists and why a label-aware skip must run before the loop, not inside it.
- `collision_surface.sh:109` — `gsub(/`/, "", val)` strips backticks from the **whole cell** before
  the `", "` split at `:121`, which is what lets a multi-word prose cell survive as one token.
- `collision_surface.sh:23` — `set -euo pipefail`. Any new `git` call must tolerate non-zero exit
  (`git check-ignore` exits 1 on "not ignored", which is the **normal** case) or the script dies.
  This is the single most likely way to break the script silently.
- `architect-synth.md:32` — a digest missing a required field is already **fail-safe-serialized**.
  The new refusal must not be routed into that branch, or an unreachable story becomes a *serialized*
  story, which is the outcome the human rejected.
- `architect-synth.md:62-64` — the rationale strings are **exact-match contracts**. A new refusal
  needs its own third string; reusing `"unknown collision metadata — fail-safe-serialized"` makes
  the refusal indistinguishable from BUG-033's case in every downstream artefact.
- `cleargate-planning/.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md:286` — not `:277` (N2).
