# WAVE 11 — CR-106 ‖ CR-107

---

## CR-106 — execution state becomes an append-only event log (w11)

**Advances:** removes the shared-document layout that made BUG-044 reachable. Architectural
successor, not a second fix.

**Execution route:** outer repo, worktree safe. Hard predecessor **BUG-044** (w10) must be merged.

### `## 3. Execution Sandbox` verdict: **the file list is the most complete in M4; §1 carries one wrong premise and §3 has two omissions.**

Seven modify rows + two create rows, all correct, both trees named explicitly. `state.schema.json`
and the 27 readers correctly in Do-NOT-modify. Citations `:78-79` and `:99` are **exact**. The
"27 non-test consumers" figure is **exactly reproducible**: `grep -rln "state\.json"` non-test →
**17** in `.cleargate/scripts/` + **10** in `cleargate-cli/src/` = **27**.

**WRONG PREMISE (§1 New Logic).** *"POSIX `O_APPEND` writes below `PIPE_BUF` (4096 bytes) are
atomic."* Two errors in one sentence:

- `PIPE_BUF` governs **pipes and FIFOs**. It says nothing about a regular file. A `.jsonl` append is
  a regular-file write and `PIPE_BUF` is irrelevant to it.
- On this platform `getconf PIPE_BUF /` returns **512**, not 4096. So even the number is wrong for
  the machine the sprint runs on.

**The conclusion survives; the reason does not.** The property actually relied on is that `O_APPEND`
makes the seek-to-end and the write a single atomic operation **with respect to other writers on
the same file** — which POSIX does guarantee for regular files, and which Node reaches via
`fs.appendFileSync(path, line, 'utf8')` (flag `'a'`). **Record the real guarantee in the code
comment.** Do not let a Developer size a record against 4096 and call it safe; that number has no
meaning here, and a comment citing it will be cargo-culted by the next reader of `state-events.mjs`.

Per the CR-105 rule (**replace the justification, do not delete the row**): the design is correct
and stays. Only the stated reason is rewritten.

**OMISSION 1 — `close_sprint.mjs`.** Not a modify target, and it should not be one, but §2's
*"Invalidate/Update: **none**"* is only true if the fold's output is byte-compatible with what
`update_state.mjs` writes today. `close_sprint.mjs` runs the lifecycle reconciler and the Step 2.6d
backsync against `state.json`, and **is the largest single reader**. §4 case 4 asserts schema
conformance; that is weaker than byte compatibility. **Add case 7** (below).

**OMISSION 2 — the two pre-dispatch migration writes.** `update_state.mjs:114-117`
(`migrateV1ToV2` + `atomicWrite`) and `:120-123` (`migrateStateToV3` + `atomicWrite`) are two full
`state.json` writes that happen **before** the action branch. Under CR-106 they are also
`state.json` writes and must either route through the fold or be explicitly exempted with a reason.
§3 does not mention them and §4's eviction check
(`grep -n "readFileSync.*stateFile" update_state.mjs` returns nothing) does not reach them —
they call `atomicWrite`, not `readFileSync`. **Add to the eviction check:**
`grep -n "atomicWrite(stateFile" .cleargate/scripts/update_state.mjs` must return **only** the
fold's own call site.

**OMISSION 3 — `init_sprint.mjs` has no shared `atomicWrite`.** It seeds `state.json` with its own
inline tmp+rename at `:231-233`, duplicating the idiom rather than importing it. CR-106's *"seed
`events.jsonl` at sprint init"* must not assume a shared helper exists. Either import from
`state-events.mjs` (preferred — that is the new module's job) or duplicate deliberately and say so.

### Corrected file surface

As declared in §3 (seven modify + two create), **plus** nothing. The three omissions above are
**scope clarifications inside already-declared files**, not new files. That is worth stating
plainly: CR-106 is the only M4 item whose declared file list needs no additions.

### Cross-Cutting Rule 4: not engaged. No template, no heading, no `section(N)`.

### Test scenarios, with the mutants each must kill

Command: `node --test .cleargate/scripts/state-scripts.test.mjs`. **See N3** — the file has no
automatic runner and had a pre-existing failure that BUG-044 (w10) fixes in its commit A. By the
time CR-106 runs, the baseline is `12 / 12 / 0 / 0` (8 original + BUG-044's four). Acceptance is
three numbers, reported verbatim.

| # | Scenario | Mutant it must kill |
|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Inherited from BUG-044, not authored here.** The 20-way concurrency test stays green **after** the lock is removed and replaced by the single-writer fold. Do not delete or weaken it. | deleting the test with the lock; keeping the lock "just in case" (then the CR proves nothing) |
| E2 | **Fold determinism.** `fold(events)` twice over the same log yields byte-identical output | iterating with `Object.keys` / `Set` ordering instead of log order; embedding `Date.now()` in the fold's `updated_at` instead of deriving it from `max(event.ts)` |
| E3 | **Replay idempotency.** Appending a duplicate event with an already-seen `run_id` leaves the fold unchanged | keying the dedupe on `ts`, or on `(story_id, to)`, instead of `run_id`. `SKILL.md` canonical `:261` is the property this exists to move out of prose. |
| E4 | **Schema conformance.** The fold's output validates against `state.schema.json` **unchanged** | editing the schema to fit the fold |
| E5 | **Legacy immutability.** A sprint dir with `state.json` and no `events.jsonl` is not rewritten and does not throw — assert the file's bytes are identical before and after | synthesising genesis events on read (the human chose **legacy-immutable**, §2 last bullet). SPRINT-03…SPRINT-38 all have `state.json` and no log. |
| E6 | **Atomic append.** N concurrent appends produce no interleaved or truncated lines; every line parses as JSON | using `writeFileSync` with a manual offset; opening without flag `'a'` |
| E7 | **NEW — byte compatibility with the current writer.** Drive a real transition sequence through the OLD path and the NEW path against identical seeds; assert the resulting `state.json` files are **byte-identical**. | any fold that reorders keys, changes indentation from `JSON.stringify(state, null, 2) + '\n'` (`update_state.mjs:78`), or drops a field the schema permits but does not require. This is the one that protects the 27 readers, and §4 case 4 does not reach it. |
| E8 | **NEW — the vacuity mutant.** Assert `fold()` receives only the event array and performs **no** read of `state.json`. Grep the module: `readFileSync` must appear only for `events.jsonl`. | **a "fold" that reads the existing `state.json` and merges into it.** It passes E2, E3, E4 and E6, produces correct-looking output, and **reintroduces the exact read-modify-write race the CR exists to remove.** Nothing in §4 as written catches it. This is CR-106's most dangerous mutant. |
| E9 | **NEW — eviction, both halves.** `grep -n "readFileSync.*stateFile" update_state.mjs` → nothing (the item's own check) **AND** `grep -n "atomicWrite(stateFile" update_state.mjs` → only the fold's call site (Omission 2) | keeping `:116`/`:122`'s migration writes on the old path |

### Reuse (no duplication)

- `atomicWrite` (`update_state.mjs:76-80`) — retained as the fold's output writer, per §1. Move it
  into `state-events.mjs` and import it back, or leave it and call it; either is fine, but there
  must be **one** copy. `init_sprint.mjs:231-233` currently duplicates the idiom — reuse is the
  chance to collapse that, and doing so is in scope because `init_sprint.mjs` is a declared surface.
- `_migrate-schema-v3.mjs:1-20` — the versioned, idempotent, tmp+rename, strip-on-read migrator
  shape. §"Existing Surfaces" names it as the model for genesis/legacy handling. **Verified: it is
  the right model** — it is the only existing precedent in the tree for "read old, write new,
  idempotent, never destructive".
- `validateState` / `validateShapeIgnoringVersion` (`validate_state.mjs:98`, `:27` — both exported)
  — the drift check extends these, it does not fork them.
- `constants.mjs` `SCHEMA_VERSION`, `VALID_STATES`, `TERMINAL_STATES`, `BOUNCE_CAP` — never redefine.
- `token-ledger.jsonl` + `.claude/hooks/token-ledger.sh` — the existing append-only JSONL idiom in
  the same directory, correctly cited by §"Existing Surfaces". **But note the hook appends with
  shell `>>`, not `fs.appendFileSync`** — a different mechanism with the same guarantee. Do not cite
  it as a code precedent; cite it as a convention precedent.
- FLASHCARD `.cleargate/FLASHCARD.md:22` (`HOOK_LOG` is append-only and never rotated) is a **direct
  design input** the CR already names: the log is scoped by `sprint_id` per line for exactly that
  reason. Keep the scoping.

### Blast radius — measured

27 non-test readers, none touched. Every state transition in every sprint. `state.schema.json`
unchanged, so `validate_state.mjs`'s existing shape checks are unaffected.

### The self-hosting hazard — and it is why this item is flagged

`.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/state.json` is **live and being written by the running sprint**.
The main checkout is on `sprint/S-39`, and `.cleargate/scripts/**` executes from the main checkout
(that is the mechanism M1's wave3 amendment recorded for the surface gate). **So from the moment
CR-106 merges, the new writer is the writer for this sprint's own waves 12 and 13** — with zero
soak time and no automatic test runner (N3).

BUG-044's lock has the same property but is ~40 additive lines with an obvious revert. CR-106
**replaces the write path** for a file that eight remaining dispatches depend on. See
**Open decision OD-1**.

### QA kick-back criteria

1. E8 absent, or the fold reads `state.json`.
2. E7 absent — no byte-compatibility witness for the 27 readers.
3. E9's second grep absent, or `:116`/`:122` still write via the old path.
4. BUG-044's 20-way test deleted, weakened, or skipped.
5. The `PIPE_BUF` / 4096 rationale reproduced in a code comment.
6. `state.schema.json` modified.
7. Any mirrored file diverges from `cleargate-planning/`.
8. The report does not carry `pass N · fail 0 · skipped 0`.

### Gotchas — file:line, non-obvious only

- `update_state.mjs:227-229` — the idempotency no-op returns **without writing**. Under an event
  log, "no-op" must mean **no event appended**, not "an event appended whose fold is a no-op".
  Appending on a no-op turns every dispatch's redundant transitions into log growth, and
  `state-scripts.test.mjs` scenario 3's second case (`:172`) asserts *"file content unchanged"* —
  which will now be true of `state.json` and false of `events.jsonl` unless this is handled.
- `update_state.mjs:187-189` / `:204-206` — auto-escalation at `BOUNCE_CAP` mutates `story.state` as
  a **side effect of a counter increment**. In an event log this is one event with a derived
  consequence, or two events. Pick one and write it down; the fold must reproduce
  `qa_bounces >= 3 ⇒ state = 'Escalated'` identically or E7 fails.
- `update_state.mjs:233-235` — `newState === 'Done'` sets `worktree = null`. Another derived
  consequence the fold must reproduce.
- `init_sprint.mjs:231-233` — inline tmp+rename, not `atomicWrite`. Two copies of the idiom exist
  today.
- `getconf PIPE_BUF /` → **512** on this machine. If a comment must cite a number, cite this one and
  say why it is irrelevant.
- `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/.session-totals.json.tmp.G5Ptvh` exists on disk — a stray tmp
  file from a *different* writer (`.session-totals.json`, not `state.json`). Do not read it as
  evidence of a torn `state.json` write.

## CR-107 — sprint→main merge goes through a pull request (w11)

**Advances:** gives the walkthrough gate at `cleargate-enforcement.md:110` an external artefact with
a URL. Nothing else in the sprint loop changes.

**Execution route:** outer repo, worktree safe. Predecessor **BUG-046** (w10) on `SKILL.md` and
`cleargate-enforcement.md` — different sections, sequential waves.

### `## 3. Execution Sandbox` verdict: **file list CORRECT; one stale range, one measurably overstated claim, and one omission — and the overstated claim points at the wrong fix.**

**Citations, re-measured (N2 applies to both SKILL.md rows):**

| §"Existing Surfaces" | Live | Canonical | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SKILL.md:714` — `git merge sprint/S-NN --no-ff` | `:714` | **`:723`** | text verified verbatim in both |
| `SKILL.md:445` — story→sprint merge | `:445` | **`:454`** | text verified verbatim; correctly in Do-NOT-modify |
| `cleargate-enforcement.md:110-138` | `:110` | same (byte-identical trees) | **correct**; `:110` is the MUST-NOT sentence, verbatim |
| `close_sprint.mjs:588-631` — *"Step 2.7/2.8 preflight"* | — | — | **STALE/WRONG** — see F1 |
| `.cleargate/config.yml` — *"currently has no `vcs:` section"* | — | — | correct |

**F1 — `close_sprint.mjs:588-631` is Step 2.7 ONLY.** Measured: Step 2.7 opens at `:587` and runs to
~`:645`; `execSync('git worktree list --porcelain')` is at **`:609`**; the graceful
*"unavailable → non-fatal skip"* the CR wants to copy is at **`:630-631`**. **Step 2.8 begins at
`:647`.** The idiom CR-107 names IS inside the cited range; the claim that the range covers 2.8 is
false. Not fatal, but it is the row a Developer opens to copy the degradation pattern, and it sends
them to the wrong half of the file for the check they must change.

**F2 — §2's "Step 2.8 must accept 'merged via PR'" is overstated, and correcting it changes the
work.** Measured, `close_sprint.mjs:661-662` and `:677`:

```
:661  const sprintBranch = `refs/heads/sprint/S-${sprintNumMatch[1]}`;
:662  const mainBranch   = 'refs/heads/main';
:677  execSync(`git merge-base --is-ancestor ${sprintBranch} ${mainBranch}`, …)
```

A PR merged with a **merge commit** makes the sprint branch an ancestor of `main`. **The check
passes with zero code change** — provided local `main` carries the merge. So §2's framing ("must
accept merged-via-PR") describes a problem that does not exist for the default strategy. **The two
real gaps are different, and neither is named in the item:**

- **(a) Squash and rebase merges destroy ancestry.** `--is-ancestor` then fails on a correctly
  merged sprint, unconditionally. Fix by **mandating merge-commit strategy** in the `vcs:` config
  and asserting it, not by loosening the check.
- **(b) The check reads `refs/heads/main` — the LOCAL ref.** After a GitHub merge, local `main` is
  behind until `git fetch`. **A correctly PR-merged sprint fails Step 2.8** until the human pulls.
  Fix by fetching before the check, or by falling back to `refs/remotes/origin/main`.

**Do not "loosen" `--is-ancestor` to "a PR exists and is closed".** A closed-unmerged PR would then
satisfy the only gate protecting `main`. That is a fail-open on the sprint's terminal boundary.

**F3 — Step 2.8 ALREADY fails open, and §4 case 2 asks for the opposite of the idiom it says it is
copying.** `close_sprint.mjs:686-692`: any `git merge-base` exit other than 1 sets
`mergeCheckAvailable = false`, writes a warning, and `:694-695` comments
*"fail-open: refs missing or git unavailable — continue to Step 3"*. §4 case 2 requires that
`vcs.sprint_pr: true` + `gh` absent → **"halts with a named error … never falls through to a silent
local merge."** That is a **fail-closed** requirement implemented next to a **fail-open** branch
with a very similar shape. A Developer copying `:686-692` writes the wrong behaviour and case 2
passes if it only asserts the message. **Case 2 must assert a non-zero exit code, not just stderr
text.** Named in the kick-back list.

**F4 — `.cleargate/config.yml` and `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.yml` are NOT mirrors and
MUST NOT be made byte-identical.** Measured:

```
live      37 lines — wiki.{index_token_ceiling, bucket_pagination_ceiling, ingest_buckets} + gates: (:21-29) + worktree: (:31-37)
canonical 19 lines — wiki.ingest_buckets only
```

Both are **tracked** (`git ls-files` confirms both, plus both `config.example.yml`). The live file
carries this repo's own gate and worktree configuration; the canonical is the shipped first-install
seed. STORY-054-04's post-flight R22 measured this and recorded that *"a Developer who reads row 13
literally deletes this repo's gate and worktree configuration."* **CR-107 §4 correctly ships NO
parity check for config. Do not add one.** Add `vcs.sprint_pr` to **both**, with different
surrounding content, and diff nothing.

`config.example.yml` exists and is tracked in **both** trees and they **differ** — same reason.

**F5 — OMISSION: `.cleargate/scripts/test/test_close_pipeline.sh` is LIVE-ONLY.** It exists at
`.cleargate/scripts/test/test_close_pipeline.sh` and is **absent** from
`cleargate-planning/.cleargate/scripts/test/` (canonical ships 10 of the live 23 test scripts).
So CR-107's verification command is meta-repo-only: **the canonical `close_sprint.mjs` change ships
to every install untested.** Not a defect to fix here — mirroring the test script is out of scope —
but it must be stated, and it is why F2's fix must be conservative.

### Corrected file surface

| Path | Tracked | Role | Parity obligation |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cleargate-planning/.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md` | yes — **PRIMARY** (N1) | §6 Phase D (open PR), §E.5 (merge PR) | — |
| `.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md` | **no** | Gate-4 re-sync only (N1, N2) | — |
| `.cleargate/scripts/close_sprint.mjs` | yes | Step 2.8 | byte-identical |
| `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/scripts/close_sprint.mjs` | yes | mirror | byte-identical |
| `.cleargate/config.yml` | yes | `vcs.sprint_pr: false` | **NONE — do not diff against canonical** |
| `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.yml` | yes | `vcs.sprint_pr: false` | **NONE** |
| `.cleargate/config.example.yml` | yes | document the `vcs:` block | **NONE — the two example files already differ** |
| `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/config.example.yml` | yes | same | **NONE** |
| `.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-enforcement.md` | yes | §2, the PR as the gate's artefact | byte-identical (Rule 1) |
| `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-enforcement.md` | yes | mirror | byte-identical |
| `.cleargate/scripts/test/test_close_pipeline.sh` | yes, **live-only** | cases 1–5 | none possible (F5) |

**Do NOT modify:** the DevOps agent, story→sprint merge (canonical `SKILL.md:454`, §C.7),
worktree lifecycle, `cleargate-cli/**` (CR-107 touches no CLI source — therefore **no CHANGELOG
entry**, §Q5-C).

### Cross-Cutting Rule 4: not engaged. No template, no gated document.

### Test scenarios, with the mutants each must kill

Command: `bash .cleargate/scripts/test/test_close_pipeline.sh`. Existing test seams are already in
place and are the right harness: `CLEARGATE_SKIP_MERGE_CHECK`, `CLEARGATE_FORCE_MERGE_STATUS=merged|unmerged`
(`close_sprint.mjs:47-52`, `:666-673`), `CLEARGATE_REPO_ROOT`. **Reuse them; do not add a fourth
seam.**

| # | Scenario | Mutant it must kill |
|---|---|---|
| P1 | **`vcs.sprint_pr` absent or `false`** (the default) → close runs the local `git merge` path, behaviour byte-identical to today | defaulting to `true` on a missing key; treating a missing `vcs:` block as an error |
| P2 | `vcs.sprint_pr: true`, `gh` absent → close **halts with a named error naming the missing binary, exit non-zero** | **routing into the existing `mergeCheckAvailable = false` fail-open at `:686-692`** — the message is printed, close proceeds, and a message-only assertion passes. **P2 must assert the exit code.** (F3) |
| P3 | `vcs.sprint_pr: true`, no remote → same named-refusal, non-zero exit | falling back to the local merge silently |
| P4 | `vcs.sprint_pr: true`, **merge-commit** PR → Step 2.8 recognises the sprint branch as merged | — (this already works today; P4 is a **regression guard**, and saying so prevents a Developer from "fixing" a check that is not broken — F2) |
| P5 | **NEW.** `vcs.sprint_pr: true`, **squash-merged** PR → Step 2.8 **fails loudly**, naming squash as unsupported | loosening `--is-ancestor` to "a PR exists and is closed", which also accepts a **closed-unmerged** PR (F2). This is the fail-open that would matter most. |
| P6 | **NEW.** Local `main` behind the remote after a PR merge → the check either fetches first or consults `refs/remotes/origin/main`; it must not fail a correctly-merged sprint | using `refs/heads/main` unconditionally (F2b) — the shipped behaviour, which reads as "not merged" on every PR-merged sprint until the human pulls |
| P7 | **PR body generation is deterministic** from sprint goal + DoD + report; **no network call** required to build it | building the body by shelling out to `gh pr view`; embedding a timestamp |
| P8 | Existing `test_close_pipeline.sh` cases stay green | — |

**Eviction check (§4, verified reachable):** canonical `SKILL.md` §E.5 must contain no
*unconditional* `git merge sprint/S-NN` — the local merge is reachable only on the
`vcs.sprint_pr: false` branch.

### Reuse (no duplication)

- `close_sprint.mjs:604-631` — the `try { execSync(...) } catch { available = false }` degradation
  idiom, correctly identified by §"Existing Surfaces" as the model for a missing `gh`. **Reuse the
  shape, invert the outcome** (F3): unavailable-`gh` is fail-**closed**, unlike
  unavailable-`git worktree list`.
- `close_sprint.mjs:47-52` env test seams — reuse, do not add.
- `.cleargate/config.yml`'s existing `gates:`/`worktree:` blocks are the precedent for a top-level
  config section; `vcs:` sits beside them.
- **Nothing in `cleargate-cli/src` implements PR handling**, and nothing should — CR-107 is a
  scripts+docs change. `grep -rn "gh pr\|pull request" cleargate-cli/src` → confirm zero before
  claiming reuse anywhere.

### Blast radius — measured

Exactly one merge per sprint. `SKILL.md` §6 and §E.5, `close_sprint.mjs` Step 2.8, two config files
and two example files, one knowledge section. **Every existing install is protected by the
`vcs.sprint_pr` default being `false`** — that default is the whole regression story and P1 is the
test that guards it.

**Cross-repo caveat, restated as measured fact:** a framework sprint touches the meta repo **and**
`cleargate-cli` (own remote, `sandrinio/cleargate-cli`). CR-107 covers the meta repo's sprint branch
only. `cleargate-cli` has no sprint branch concept and no `.github/workflows` (`ls` confirms absent
in all three repos). Out of scope, and the item says so.

### QA kick-back criteria

1. P2 asserts only a message and not a non-zero exit (F3).
2. P5 or P6 absent.
3. `--is-ancestor` replaced by a PR-state query rather than supplemented by one.

7. `cleargate-enforcement.md` diverges between trees.
8. `cleargate-cli/**` touched.

### Gotchas — file:line, non-obvious only

- `close_sprint.mjs:686-692` — the fail-open branch. Adjacent, similar-looking, and the opposite of
  what §4 case 2 requires.
- `close_sprint.mjs:662` — `refs/heads/main`, the local ref (F2b).
- `close_sprint.mjs:659` — Step 2.8 **skips entirely** when the sprint id has no numeric portion.

## CR-108 — `cleargate new <type>`, one scaffolder for every work-item type (w12)

**Advances:** moves ~33 lines of hand-authored YAML and a three-grep ID discovery per work item from
token-priced agent authoring into a script.

**Execution route: CROSS-REPO.** `cleargate-cli` half in the cli main checkout (branch from cli
`main`, whatever `main` is after BUG-045 merges — **not** `e4cb49f`). Outer half (ten template
files across two trees, plus two `CLAUDE.md` files) in the outer repo. Cross-Cutting Rule 6: cli
commits are ungated; run typecheck + full suite by hand and report both.

Predecessors, all merged before w12: **BUG-045** (w10, corrected allocator), **STORY-054-02** (w4,
`spike` in the type registry), **STORY-054-06** (w6, `## Task Breakdown` in three templates).

### `## 3. Execution Sandbox` verdict: **INCOMPLETE, and its §2 inventory is measurably WRONG in a way that changes the design. Plus a FALSE `## Prior work`.**

This is the largest item in M4 and the one whose declared scope is furthest from its real scope.
Fourteen findings, ordered by consequence.

**F1 — `## Prior work` line 92 is FALSE.** §Q5-A. *"[[CR-105]], [[BUG-043]] … **No overlap.**"* Its
own §3 declares both `CLAUDE.md` files — exactly the pair `71037e5a` rewrote. Correct the sentence
before dispatch; it is the stale-prose class this sprint removes, sitting in an unexecuted item.

**F2 — the placeholder inventory in §2 is wrong on two of three clauses.** Measured 2026-08-29
across `.cleargate/templates/*.md` (see **Measurement appendix M-6** for the full table):

| §2's claim | Measured | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `{ID}` used by `Bug.md`, `CR.md`, `epic.md`, `hotfix.md`, `story.md` | **seven** files — those five plus `spike.md` and `Sprint Plan Template.md` | **incomplete** |
| `{NNN}` used by `epic.md` and `initiative.md` | `epic.md:11` and `initiative.md:23,29,63` | **correct** |
| `{ISO}` and `{SLUG}` exist **only** in `hotfix.md` | **`spike.md` carries both** — `:30` (`{ID}_{SLUG}.md`), `:61`, `:62` (`{ISO}`), `:88` | **FALSE** |
| *"all nine templates plus all nine canonical mirrors — 18 files"* | **ten** template files per tree = **20** | **stale** |

`spike.md` landed 2026-08-27 (STORY-054-01), one day **after** CR-108 was drafted. The inventory is
a pre-sprint snapshot.

There are **22** distinct `{Token}` forms across the ten templates, not four:
`{Action} {Benefit} {Description} {EpicID} {ID} {ISO} {NNN} {N} {PM_TOOL_SPRINT_ID} {Persona}
{SLUG} {Slug} {StoryID} {StoryName} {Title} {action} {epic_name} {name} {outcome} {precondition}
{semver} {short_name}`. §4 case 3 (*"no unrendered placeholder the renderer owns … body
placeholders the human fills are whitelisted"*) needs that list as its whitelist, and the whitelist
must be **enumerated and size-asserted**, not inferred — the `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE` / `KNOWN_BUCKET_GAPS`
shape this sprint has already used twice.

**F3 — THE DESIGN DEFECT. `{ID}` is not a naming divergence; it is a SEMANTIC divergence with three
incompatible meanings.** Measured:

| Meaning | Sites |
|---|---|
| **bare number** (`044`) | `Bug.md:30` `bug_id: "BUG-{ID}"` · `CR.md:30` `cr_id: "CR-{ID}"` · `epic.md:35` `epic_id: "EPIC-{ID}"` · `Sprint Plan Template.md:40` `sprint_id: "SPRINT-{ID}"` |
| **full id** (`HOTFIX-013`) | `hotfix.md:31` `hotfix_id: "{ID}"` · `spike.md:50` `spike_id: "{ID}"` — and `hotfix.ts:166` sets `idStr = "HOTFIX-013"`, so **full-id is what the renderer actually substitutes today** |
| **the PARENT's number** | `story.md:67` `parent_epic_ref: "EPIC-{ID}"` — `{ID}` here is the epic, not the story |

`epic.md` uses **both** conventions internally: `{NNN}` at `:11` (output location) and `{ID}` at
`:35`/`:75` for the same number.

**A "normalize the token" fix that does not first choose a semantic renders `bug_id: "BUG-BUG-044"`
or `hotfix_id: "013"`.** Ruling: **choose full-id** (it is what the one shipping renderer already
substitutes), then rewrite the seven `<PREFIX>-{ID}` sites to bare `{ID}`, and rewrite
`story.md:67` to a distinct token — `{PARENT_EPIC_ID}` — because it is not the item's own id at all.

**F4 — zero-pad width is per-type and `padStart(3,'0')` does not generalise.** Measured from the
live corpus: `HOTFIX-001`, `BUG-045`, `CR-114`, `EPIC-058`, `INITIATIVE-001`, `SPIKE-001` are
3-digit zero-padded; **`SPRINT-39` and `SPRINT-38` are 2-digit unpadded.** Lifting `hotfix.ts:166`
verbatim produces `SPRINT-040`. The type→template map must carry a **width** alongside the template
filename.

**F5 — `story` cannot use a max+1 allocator at all.** `numericStem("STORY-054-06")` returns
`"054"` — the **epic** number (`work-item-id.ts:240-246`; `deriveParentEpicId` at `:255-260` exists
precisely because of this). `max+1` over the story corpus yields `STORY-100`, an invalid shape.
Story allocation is `STORY-<parentEpic>-<nextSeqWithinEpic>` and requires the parent as an argument.
Measured corpus maxima (Appendix **M-7**) make it concrete: `STORY` max in `pending-sync/` is `054`
and in `archive/` is `099`.

**F6 — `proposal` is a registered type with NO template in either tree, so §4 case 2 is
unsatisfiable as written.** `work-item-type.ts:8` includes `'proposal'`; `.cleargate/templates/`
has ten files and none is `proposal.md`. `TEMPLATE_FOR`
(`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:111-118`) omits it deliberately and `KNOWN_UNPINNABLE`
(`:121-124`) records why. Ruling: **carry an explicit, size-asserted `KNOWN_UNSCAFFOLDABLE` set**
containing exactly `proposal`, with S6's escape-hatch closure copied verbatim — assert
`KNOWN_UNSCAFFOLDABLE.size === 1`, so adding a second type to make a test green is a visible edit.
Creating `proposal.md` is out of scope.

**F7 — `<instructions>` blocks are NOT stripped by the renderer, and CR-108 does not say whether
they should be.** All ten templates carry exactly one `<instructions>` block;
`grep -n "instructions\|strip" cleargate-cli/src/commands/hotfix.ts` → **zero**. So today's
`hotfix new` output ships the instruction block verbatim. `HOTFIX-001` in `archive/` carries none
and no unrendered token, so the current human workflow is hand-cleaning after scaffolding.
**Open decision OD-3.**

**F8 — `hotfix.md:11`'s Output-location line is already wrong today.** It reads
`HOTFIX-{ID}-{Slug}.md`. With `{ID}` = `HOTFIX-013` (F3) it renders `HOTFIX-HOTFIX-013-{Slug}.md`,
while `hotfix.ts:186-188` actually writes `HOTFIX-013_slug_with_underscores.md`. `{Slug}`
(capital-S, lowercase rest) is substituted by nothing — `hotfix.ts:180-182` handles only `{ID}`,
`{SLUG}`, `{ISO}`. Fix in CR-108; it is exactly the normalisation the CR exists for.

**F9 — the filename separator must be an UNDERSCORE, and it is load-bearing for the wiki.**
FLASHCARD 2026-08-28 `#id-parsing #danger`: `derive-bucket.ts:63-66` keys the wiki id on everything
before the **first underscore**; 24 of 490 items use hyphens and 4 wiki pages are already keyed on
the whole stem, so `[[BUG-035]]` links are dead and ingest exits 0 silently. `spike.md:30` already
ships the correct `{ID}_{SLUG}.md` shape. **`cleargate new` emits `<FULL-ID>_<Slug>.md`, always.**

**F10 — `initiative.md:38-39` ship `created_at_version: "cleargate@{semver}"` unsubstituted.**
Confirmed live. `hotfix.ts:180-182` replaces only `{ID}`, `{SLUG}`, `{ISO}`. Latent today (nothing
renders `initiative.md`); **CR-108 generalising the renderer to all types writes a literal
`cleargate@{semver}` into every new initiative.** M1's wave3 amendment already routed this here.
Ruling: normalise `initiative.md` to `"strategy-phase-pre-init"`, matching six of the other nine
(`Bug`, `CR`, `Sprint Plan Template`, `epic`, `spike`, `story`). `hotfix.md:39` hardcodes
`cleargate@0.5.0` and should join them.

**F11 — OMISSION: `cleargate-cli/CHANGELOG.md`.** A new user-facing command. One bullet under the
existing `## Unreleased`; do not open a second (§Q5-C).

**F12 — OMISSION: hardcoded censuses that read the registries CR-108 touches.** FLASHCARD
2026-08-27 `#test-harness #gate #danger` and the M1 wave4-prep amendment together name four:
`cleargate-cli/test/lib/work-item-type.node.test.ts:182` (hardcodes `WORK_ITEM_TRANSITIONS` count),
`gate-unit.node.test.ts:748` and `readiness-predicates.node.test.ts:714` (gate-block counts), and
`gate-section-index-pinning.node.test.ts:431/:634` (criteria counts). CR-108 adds no gate block and
no criterion, so only the first is at risk — **but census every hardcoded count that reads a file
you append to, not just the ones a plan names.**

**F13 — `cleargate-cli/src/cli.ts` registration.** 45 `.command(` registrations today
(`grep -n "\.command(" src/cli.ts`); `hotfix` is a group at `:711` with `new <slug>` at `:719`.
`cleargate new <type> <slug>` is a **top-level** command, so it sits beside `stamp` (`:168`) rather
than inside a group. §2's *"`cleargate hotfix new` becomes a thin alias"* is right and cheap: keep
`:719` and delegate.

**F14 — the `sprint_context` / `sprint_report` exclusion in §2 is correct and must be tested.**
Both have **zero** placeholders (measured) and neither is a work item in the ID sense. §3 correctly
lists them in Do-NOT-modify. But `Sprint Plan Template.md` **is** in §3's modify list and **is** a
work item (`SPRINT-{ID}`, F4's un-padded 2-digit case). Do not conflate the three.

### Blast radius — measured

`grep -rn "maxHotfixId" cleargate-cli/src` → 2 hits today; §4's eviction check requires ≤1 general
allocator after. Twenty template files across two trees (8 modified × 2, plus 2 untouched × 2).
Two `CLAUDE.md` files with a `doctor`-blocking hash coupling and no test-time warning. One new
top-level CLI command. **`cleargate new` becomes the front door for every work item authored in
every install — a defect here is a defect in every future item.**

### QA kick-back criteria

1. N8, N9, N10 or N11 absent — the four semantic/shape defects (F3, F4, F5, F9) ship silently.
2. N13 uses the unanchored regex, or asserts equality without the length.
3. `## Prior work`'s *"No overlap"* sentence unchanged (F1).
4. `KNOWN_UNSCAFFOLDABLE` absent, or present without a size assertion (F6).
5. A new ID regex anywhere in `cleargate-cli/src` (`npm run check:no-inline-id-regex` must pass).
6. `spike.md` or `Sprint Plan Template.md` omitted from the normalisation (F2).
7. `expected-headings.ts` opened, or `gate-section-index-pinning` ≠ `18 / 18 / 0 / 0` (N6 of the
   rulings).
8. Any template diverges between trees.
9. `cleargate-cli/CHANGELOG.md` untouched, or a second `## Unreleased` opened.
10. Typecheck + full-suite numbers absent from the report (Cross-Cutting Rule 6).

### Gotchas — file:line, non-obvious only

- `CLAUDE.md:50` mentions `<!-- CLEARGATE:START -->…<!-- CLEARGATE:END -->` **inline, inside the
  block**. This is why the `block-equal` check must be anchored (N13, §Q5-A) — and why the naive
  version returns a confident `true` about the wrong 10606 characters.
- `work-item-type.ts:66` — `PREFIX_MAP` matches by `basename.includes(prefix)`, and FLASHCARD
  2026-08-27 `#id-parsing #danger` (R24) proves **no ordering satisfies both collision directions**.
  `cleargate new` must take the type from **argv**, never infer it from a filename.
- `hotfix.ts:41-42` — the BUG-041 comment recording why the local regex was removed. Read it before
  writing any pattern.
- `hotfix.ts:143` — `repoRoot = cli?.cwd ?? resolveProjectRoot(process.cwd())`. The `cwd` seam is
  how every test isolates; `new.ts` must expose the same seam or its tests write into the real repo.
- `.cleargate/templates/Sprint Plan Template.md` has **spaces in the filename**. Any shell loop over
  templates must quote; an unquoted `for f in $(ls …)` splits it into three (I hit this while
  measuring — see Appendix M-6's note).
- `initiative.md:23` uses `OUTPUT LOCATION:` (caps, backticked path) where the other nine use
  `Output location:`. Another normalisation the CR should sweep.
- `spike.md:30` is the **only** template whose output-location line is already correct for both the
  underscore separator and the full-id semantic. Use it as the target shape.

## CR-110 — the sprint goal gets an acceptance check (w12)

**Advances:** turns a prose sprint goal into a recorded, checkable condition and gives the
Orchestrator a compaction-proof anchor in the file every agent already reads.

**Execution route:** outer repo, worktree safe, **with N1 applied** — the two `.claude/**` rows are
canonical-primary. Predecessors: **BUG-046** (w10) and **CR-107** (w11) on `SKILL.md`; **CR-106**
(w11) on `init_sprint.mjs`. All disjoint regions, all earlier waves.

### `## 3. Execution Sandbox` verdict: **file list CORRECT and complete; §"Existing Surfaces" contains one FALSE claim — the CR-105 "wrong justification" mode — and one conflation.**

**F1 — `reporter.md` §E.2 verdict "exists". FALSE, and this is the dangerous mode.**

Measured: `.claude/agents/reporter.md` is 274 lines and contains **zero** occurrences of
`verdict`, `met`, `partial`, `missed`, or any goal-verdict section. It has **no `§E.2`**. The only
goal-adjacent line is `:26` — the standard sprint-context preflight instruction. Its `## ` headings
are the agent-role set, not the sprint phases.

The `met | partial | missed` vocabulary lives **exclusively** in
`.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md`, at three sites (canonical numbering per N2):

```
:45   §0.5 touchpoint 5 — "Reporter brief MUST include a goal-achievement verdict — `met / partial / missed`"
:695  §E.2 Brief template — "> **Goal:** `<verbatim sprint goal>` — **Verdict: met | partial | missed.**"
:700  §E.2 🎯 note      — "`met` = goal achieved as written. `partial` = … `missed` = …"
```

**Per the CR-105 rule (FLASHCARD 2026-08-29 `#surface-declaration #danger`): replace the
justification, do NOT delete the row.** `reporter.md` **is** still needed — it is the only file that
tells the Reporter what to read, and it currently does not mention `## Goal Acceptance Check` at
all. Corrected justification: *"add the instruction to read `sprint-context.md` §Goal Acceptance
Check and derive the verdict from the recorded check rather than by judgement; and to quote each
milestone plan's `GOAL_RELATION` line in §1."* The **vocabulary** edit is a `SKILL.md` edit at the
three sites above.

A Developer following the row as written opens `reporter.md`, finds nothing matching the
description, and either invents a `§E.2` section that does not belong there or skips the file —
in which case CR-110 ships with the verdict still a judgement call and the CR's central claim
unrealised.

**F2 — §3's `init_sprint.mjs` row conflates two things, and half of it is free.** Measured,
`init_sprint.mjs:239-289`: the script reads the whole `sprint_context.md` template (`:243`) and
writes it out (`:284-286`), substituting only three frontmatter placeholders (`:257-259`) and one
goal placeholder string (`:270-275`). **It does not enumerate sections.** So adding
`## Goal Acceptance Check` to `.cleargate/templates/sprint_context.md` makes §4 case 1 pass with
**zero `init_sprint.mjs` change.** The only real script work is the **advisory emission** (case 2).
Say so, or the Developer looks for a renderer that does not exist.

**F3 — OMISSION (advisory): `.cleargate/templates/sprint_report.md`.** Grepped: **zero** goal or
verdict content in either tree. If the verdict is to be *recorded* rather than only spoken in the
close-gate Brief, the report template is where it lands. §3 does not list it. **Not mandated here**
— routed as **OD-4**, because adding a section to the report template interacts with
`close_sprint.mjs:397-409`'s required-row regex list, which is explicitly in Do-NOT-modify.

**F4 — `close_sprint.mjs:295` claim VERIFIED.** `:288-300` filters a list of regexes over the
report text and exits 1 on any missing **row**; it never evaluates a **value**. §3 correctly puts
`close_sprint.mjs` in Do-NOT-modify and §1's *"Declared intent is write-only"* is exactly right.

**F5 — the Q5.3 vocabulary question. RULED IN SCOPE, but on two axes, not one.** Full reasoning in
**§Q5-B**. Summary: keep the **sprint** verdict at `met | partial | missed` (+ CR-110's own
`not-mechanically-verifiable` for the *check*), and add a **separate per-milestone**
`GOAL_RELATION: advances | off critical path` line owned by the Architect and quoted by the
Reporter. Folding a milestone relation into a sprint verdict would force the Reporter to score
SPRINT-39 `partial` because M3 and M4 were unrelated — manufacturing the exact linkage O5 forbade.
**SPRINT-39 M3 and M4 are the worked example; put both in the CR body.**

```markdown
## Goal Acceptance Check
```

### Blast radius — measured

One new template section (ungated, unpinned), one advisory line in one script, three prose sites in
`SKILL.md`, one instruction in `reporter.md`. **Every existing sprint plan lacks a goal check**, so
G2's exit-0 advisory path is the entire backward-compatibility story. Closed sprints are untouched
— `init_sprint.mjs` only writes `sprint-context.md` when it is absent or `--force` is passed
(`:242`).

- `init_sprint.mjs:242` — `if (!fs.existsSync(ctxOut) || force)`. A re-init without `--force` writes
  nothing, so a test that runs init twice measures the first run's output.
- `init_sprint.mjs:270` — the goal regex is `/^- \*\*Sprint Goal:\*\* (.+)$/` against the **first
  200 lines** of the sprint plan (`:268`). A check-extraction that mimics it inherits both limits.
- `init_sprint.mjs:249` — a missing template is a **WARN and continue**, not a failure. An advisory
  that assumes the template was read will silently not fire.
- Canonical `SKILL.md:695` and `:700` are the two verdict sites in §E.2; `:45` is the third, in
  §0.5. Live numbering is `:686`, `:691`, `:45` (N2 — §0.5 is above the drift point, so `:45` is
  the same in both trees).
- `sprint_context.md` is **not** in `TEMPLATE_FOR` and **not** in `readiness-gates.md`. That is why
  Rule 4 is inert here, and it is worth writing into the CR so the next reader does not re-derive it.

```
cleargate-cli/test  *.integration.node.test.ts (excl. red)  : 24
cleargate-cli/test  *.red.integration.node.test.ts          :  6
cleargate-cli/test  *.red.node.test.ts                      : 56
mcp                 *.integration.node.test.ts              :  1

```

The thirty-first is **`cleargate-cli/test/hooks/cr-026-integration.node.test.ts`** — a **hyphen**,

**F6 — THE DECISIVE FINDING. `section(N)` cannot express `test-layers-declared` non-vacuously for
any of the three types. §"Existing Surfaces"' mechanism claim is false.**

§"Existing Surfaces" says: *"New criterion registered in the existing `story`/`cr`/`bug` blocks
using existing section-predicate machinery."* Measured with the real exported `evaluate()`
(`readiness-predicates.ts:160`) against the shipped templates, **both trees, identical results**:

| Template | Section | Heading | `declared-item` count today |
|---|---|---|---|
| `story.md` | `section(5)` | `## 4. Quality Gates` | **6** |
| `CR.md` | `section(8)` | `## 4. Verification Protocol` | **1** |
| `Bug.md` | `section(6)` | `## 5. Verification Protocol` | **1** |

- **`story.md`: any `section(5) has ≥N declared-item` with N ≤ 6 is green on the unedited template**
  — vacuous on day one, before an author writes a word. Adding the Integration row makes it 7.
  N ≥ 7 fails every honest story. **There is no threshold that works.** (`section(5)` is also
  already `story.dod-declared`'s target — one section, two criteria, and BUG-054's flashcard
  records that `story.dod-declared` scores 3 `listed-item` off the §4.2 DoD boxes, i.e. it is
  *already* one of the nine measured-vacuous criteria.)
- **`CR.md` / `Bug.md`: `≥4` is non-vacuous today at 1** — but the moment CR-111 ships the three-row
  table into the template, the score becomes 4 and `≥4` is vacuous too. **Shipping the table the CR
  exists to ship destroys the only threshold that would have worked.**

**F7 — the version guard §2 asks about DOES NOT EXIST and CANNOT be built.** §2 says: *"the
criterion applies to items whose `created_at_version` is at or after the shipping release …
**Confirm the predicate vocabulary supports a version guard before implementing**; if it does not,
ship the template rows first and the gate as a follow-up."*

**Measured answer: it does not.** `readiness-gates.md:7` — *"There are exactly **10** predicate
shapes."* Shape 1 is `frontmatter(<ref>).<field> <op> <value>` with `<op> ∈ {==, !=, >=, <=}`, and
`compareValues` (`readiness-predicates.ts:264-291`) implements the ordered operators as:

```ts
:290  case '>=': return Number(a) >= Number(expected);
:291  case '<=': return Number(a) <= Number(expected);
```

So `frontmatter(.).created_at_version >= 'cleargate@0.25.0'` evaluates `NaN >= NaN` → **false,
always**. And the corpus makes it worse: six templates ship the literal `"strategy-phase-pre-init"`,
`hotfix.md:39` ships `"cleargate@0.5.0"`, `initiative.md:38` ships the unsubstituted
`"cleargate@{semver}"`, and live items carry `cleargate@0.24.2` and `dff83bd3-dirty`. There is no
comparable ordering across those values under any operator the vocabulary has.

**F8 — but the grandfathering mechanism already exists, and it is free.** The three closed-set
predicates in the vocabulary all use the same idiom: **pass when the section is absent.**
`task-breakdown-complete` (`readiness-predicates.ts:1158-1163`):

```ts
:1158  if (!sectionContent) {
:1159    return { pass: true, detail: `not-applicable: ## Task Breakdown section absent` };
:1163  }
```

`prior-work-recorded` and `ambiguity-gate-resolved` do the same, and `readiness-gates.md:10`
records the reason: *"migration grace for items authored before this predicate existed."*
**That grandfathers every existing item without any version guard.** §2's escape clause does not
need to fire, provided CR-111 takes the closed-set shape.

**F9 — the right shape exists, has a one-wave-old precedent in this very sprint, and does not touch
`evalSection`.** STORY-054-06 (`a7f1c66`, w6) added **predicate #10** as a closed-set predicate at
three sites, without touching the frozen evaluator (N5):

```
readiness-predicates.ts:24        | { kind: 'task-breakdown-complete' };          <- union member
readiness-predicates.ts:135-138   if (s === 'task-breakdown-complete') { … }      <- parsePredicate
readiness-predicates.ts:189-190   case 'task-breakdown-complete': return evalTaskBreakdownComplete(doc);
readiness-predicates.ts:1147-1177 function evalTaskBreakdownComplete(doc) { … }   <- the evaluator
```

**`test-layers-declared` must be predicate #11 in exactly that shape.** It locates its section by
`headingTitleOf` (exported at `:467`), passes on absence, fails on present-but-incomplete, and —
unlike `task-breakdown-complete`, which only counts rows (`:1165`) — **must also check the reason
cell is non-empty when the count is `0`**. That last part is genuinely new logic, not a copy.

**Consequence: CR-111 adds NO `section(N)` criterion.** So S1a's `18`/`16` are unchanged, S6's `16`
is unchanged, `EXPECTED_HEADINGS` needs zero rows, and the four-site T2 edit does not apply. **This
is the cheapest possible route and it is the one to take.**

### Cross-Cutting Rule 4: NOT ENGAGED. See N6. RULING: CR-111 adds no `## ` heading to any template.

`story.md` §4.1's existing table (raw `:193-197`) gains **one row**. `CR.md` §4 and `Bug.md` §5 gain
a table placed **inside** the existing section, under a `###` sub-heading or none. `###` does not
count — `evalSection` splits on `/^(?=## )/m` (`readiness-predicates.ts:645`), which a `### ` line
does not match.

**Therefore: zero `section(N)` values move, `expected-headings.ts` gets zero edits, and
`gate-section-index-pinning` must report `18 / 18 / 0 / 0`.** `sprint-context.md`'s Cross-Cutting
Rule 4 names CR-111 as a heading-adder; **that expectation is superseded by this ruling**, and the
supersession is recorded in `## Mid-Sprint Amendments` by the orchestrator, not by rewriting Rule 4.

## Cross-item risks

Things a Developer working only on their own item would miss.

**X1 — SKILL.md is edited by four of eight items and every one of them cites it by a LIVE line
number that is wrong for the tree they must commit.** N2. The offsets (+9 / +10) are stable only
until the first M4 edit lands; after that they compound. **Locate by heading text, always.** Four
Developers, four waves, one file.

**X2 — the SKILL.md edit ORDER shifts every later citation, and the earliest edit is the most
disruptive.** CR-110 (w12) edits §0.5 at canonical `:35-55` — **above every other M4 edit point**.
So CR-110's own §A.5 and §E.2 citations shift within its own commit (N7), and CR-111 (w13) inherits
a §C.3 that has moved twice (BUG-046's §C.2 insertion + CR-110's §0.5 insertion). No conflict; only
stale numbers.

**X3 — `state-scripts.test.mjs` is the shared acceptance harness for BUG-044 (w10) and CR-106
(w11), it has a pre-existing failure, and NOTHING RUNS IT.** N3. If BUG-044 does not fix `:90` in
commit A, CR-106 inherits a `fail 1` baseline and cannot distinguish its own regressions from it.
If neither Developer runs it by hand, both items ship with an untested acceptance criterion. **The
orchestrator must require the three-number line in both reports.**

**X4 — BUG-044 and CR-106 both rewrite the writer of the file this sprint is using to track itself.**
Both merge into `sprint/S-39`, and `.cleargate/scripts/**` executes from the main checkout, which is
on `sprint/S-39`. From w10 onward the lock guards this sprint's own transitions; from w11 onward the
event log **is** this sprint's state writer. Zero soak time, no automatic test. **BUG-044 is
acceptable** (additive, ~40 lines, `git revert -m 1`). **CR-106 is flagged — OD-1.**

**X5 — BUG-045 (w10) is CR-108's (w12) hard predecessor, and CR-108 will inherit whichever mtime
semantics BUG-045 ships.** If BUG-045 copies `countActiveHotfixes`'s 7-day filter (its R6 mutant),
CR-108 generalises a *still-broken* allocator to nine types — the precise failure §Open Questions
was written to prevent. **BUG-045's R6 is therefore a CR-108 blocker, not just a BUG-045 test.**

**X6 — BUG-046 (w10) adds `dep_predecessors` to `story.md`/`CR.md`/`Bug.md`; CR-108 (w12)
normalises placeholders in the same three files; CR-111 (w13) adds table rows to the same three
files.** Three items, three waves, same six files (two trees). All disjoint regions — frontmatter
vs `{Token}` sites vs §4/§4.1/§5 body — but **each lands a diff on files the next one must re-read.
None may rely on a line number measured before its predecessor merged** (N7).

**X7 — `cleargate-enforcement.md` is edited by BUG-046 (w10, §1.3 `:85-90`) and CR-107 (w11, §2
`:108-138`).** Disjoint, sequential. But both are two-tree byte-identical obligations under
Cross-Cutting Rule 1, and the trees are byte-identical **today** — a divergence introduced by
BUG-046 makes CR-107's parity check fail for a reason CR-107 did not cause.

**X8 — `init_sprint.mjs` is edited by CR-106 (w11, `:220-233` state seed) and CR-110 (w12,
`:239-289` context render).** Disjoint, sequential. CR-106 also **collapses the duplicated
tmp+rename idiom at `:231-233`**, which shifts every line below it — so CR-110's `:239-289` region
will have moved by the time it runs.

**X9 — `.claude/**` is untracked and FOUR items name live paths as their primary surface.** N1.
BUG-046, CR-107, CR-110 and CR-111. A Developer who edits only the live copy produces a commit
containing nothing for those files and **no test will notice**, because every one of those files is
prose. The kick-back for all four is the same:
`git diff --name-only` must contain **zero** `.claude/` paths.

**X10 — the surface gate is INERT for all eight items.** N4 (BUG-059). The mechanism that caught
STORY-054-06's and STORY-054-07's undeclared surfaces cannot fire once in M4. Combined with X9,
this means **the two controls that would independently catch a `.claude/**` inversion are both
absent.** QA-Verify's diff read is the only one left.

**X11 — three items are user-visible and only two need a CHANGELOG entry.** §Q5-C. BUG-045 and
CR-108 touch `cleargate-cli/src` and must append **under the existing `## Unreleased`**. CR-107
does not touch cli source and must not. **If both BUG-045 and CR-108 append, there must still be
exactly one `## Unreleased` heading** — `grep -c '^## Unreleased' cleargate-cli/CHANGELOG.md` must
return 1 at the end of M4.

**X12 — `gate-section-index-pinning` must read `18 / 18 / 0 / 0` after every one of the eight
merges, and no M4 item may open `expected-headings.ts`.** N6. Three items touch templates and one
touches `readiness-gates.md`; all four have this assertion in their kick-back list. A Developer who
"keeps the fixture in sync" is one keystroke from the tampering Cross-Cutting Rule 4 forbids, and
the STORY-054-05 post-flight already recorded that nine of the twelve original fixture rows are
**single-witness** — editing one to match a drifted tree goes green.

**X13 — CR-111 (w13) needs `readiness-predicates.ts`, which is in the CLI repo, while its other six
files are in the outer repo.** F-omission in its §3. That makes CR-111 a **cross-repo** item, which
neither its §3 nor `waves.json`'s w13 rationale states. Cross-Cutting Rule 6 applies to its cli half.

**X14 — CR-108 and CR-111 both edit the CLI repo in consecutive waves, and CR-108's branch point is
"cli `main` after BUG-045 merges", not `e4cb49f`.** The dispatch's stated cli baseline
(`e4cb49f`) is correct **only for BUG-045**. CR-108 and CR-111 must branch from whatever cli `main`
is at their turn, and their full-suite baselines will not be `2576 / 2574 / 1 / 1`.

## Open decisions for the orchestrator

**OD-1 — Should CR-106 ship this sprint? I recommend NO.** *(This is the one item I would move.)*

The case for deferring:
- It **replaces the write path** for `state.json` while `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/state.json`
  is live and being written by the running sprint, from a main checkout sitting on `sprint/S-39`.
  Waves 12 and 13 would be the soak (X4).
- Its acceptance harness has **no automatic runner and had a pre-existing failure** (N3). Every
  other item in M4 is adjudicated by a suite something runs.
- BUG-044 (w10) already closes the defect. CR-106 is the *architectural successor*, and both the
  item's own §Open Questions and BUG-044's confirm the two are deliberately independent: *"Keep
  both; test carries forward."*
- Three of its findings above (the `PIPE_BUF` premise, the two unguarded migration writes at
  `update_state.mjs:116`/`:122`, and the `fold()`-must-not-read mutant E8) each change the design.
  None was in the item.
- Its blast radius is 27 readers and every transition in every sprint, with no CI anywhere
  (`ls .github/workflows` absent in all three repos).

The case for shipping: BUG-044's lock is a stopgap the item itself calls temporary, and EPIC-055 is
blocked on CR-106. Neither is urgent inside SPRINT-39.

**If it ships anyway,** the mitigations are E7 (byte-compatibility against the current writer), E8
(the fold reads no `state.json`), E9 (both eviction greps), and a named rollback:
`git revert -m 1 <merge commit>` in the outer repo, never `git reset --hard`. **Decision is the
orchestrator's and the human's; I am surfacing it, not making it.**

**OD-2 — BUG-046 is four defects in one item and is CR-108-sized. Split, or accept?**
Reachability (§1) + `dep_predecessors` (§3.5a) + parser over-reporting (§3.5b/c) + label
unification (§3.5d), across **thirteen** files in two trees with **thirteen** verification cases.
§3.5(a) is independently valuable and independently testable — it is the one the item itself calls
*"the most consequential of the three"*, and it is the only one that fails toward a **real
collision** rather than toward over-serialisation. A split would be `BUG-046` (reachability +
refusal, cases C1–C7, C12, C13) and a new item (`dep_predecessors` + parser unification, cases
C8–C11). **Not my call. Flagging the size, and noting the split line is clean.**

**OD-3 — Does `cleargate new` strip `<instructions>` blocks?** CR-108 F7. All ten templates carry
one; the shipping renderer strips nothing; the one real scaffolded artefact in the repo
(`archive/HOTFIX-001_*.md`) carries none, so the current workflow is hand-cleaning. Stripping is a
behaviour change to `hotfix new` (§4 case 7 demands byte-identical regression). **The CR does not
decide this and it must be decided before dispatch.**

**OD-4 — Does the sprint-goal verdict get recorded in `sprint_report.md`, or only spoken in the
Brief?** CR-110 F3. The report template has zero goal/verdict content today. Adding a section
interacts with `close_sprint.mjs:397-409`'s required-row regex list, which CR-110 explicitly puts
in Do-NOT-modify. **Recommend: not this sprint** — the `GOAL_RELATION` line (§Q5-B) gives the
Reporter what it needs without touching the close gate.

**OD-5 — The `## Task Breakdown` sections.** N8. All eight items lack the section and all eight pass
their gate on the absence grace. My role brief requires the rows in the item files; this dispatch
forbids editing them. **The rows are in this plan.** If they are to reach the item files, the
orchestrator writes them and commits **before** the worktrees are cut. N4 means the gate will not
check them either way.

**OD-6 — Three of eight items carry a `## Prior work` or `## Existing Surfaces` claim that this plan
measures as FALSE.** CR-108's *"No overlap"* (§Q5-A F1), CR-110's `reporter.md` verdict claim
(F1), CR-111's *"existing section-predicate machinery"* claim (F6). All three are **pre-dispatch
orchestrator amendments** to the item files — the same class as M1's `STORY-054-02 §3.1 AMENDED`
and M2's `STORY-054-06 §3.1` corrections. **Per the CR-105 rule, all three REPLACE a justification
and none deletes a row.** All three rows name files that are genuinely needed.

**OD-7 — CR-111's cli half is undeclared, which makes it cross-repo.** X13. If the orchestrator
would rather keep w13 single-repo, the alternative is to ship CR-111's template + doc half only and
defer the gate — which is exactly what CR-111 §2's own escape clause proposes (*"ship the template
rows first and the gate as a follow-up"*). F7 measured the trigger for that clause as **fired** for
the *version guard*; F8 and F9 then show a route that does not need it. **Both routes are viable;
the cheap one is cross-repo.**

**OD-8 — CR-107's squash-merge and stale-local-`main` gaps (F2a, F2b) are not in the item's §2 and
are the actual work.** The item asks for a change to Step 2.8 that measurement shows is largely
unnecessary, and does not ask for the two changes measurement shows are necessary. **This is a
scope correction, not a scope increase** — but it should be recorded as an amendment before
dispatch so the Developer is not asked to build what §2 describes.

## Version check (CR-037)

**No M4 item adds a package to any `package.json`.** Verified: `grep -n "package.json"` across the
eight items returns hits only in Do-NOT-modify lists (BUG-044 §4, CR-105-inherited discipline) and
in CR-108's `stamp-frontmatter`/`cli.ts` context, none of them a dependency addition.

CR-107 introduces a **runtime binary** dependency, not a package dependency: the `gh` CLI. It is
config-gated (`vcs.sprint_pr`, default `false`) and its absence must be a named refusal (P2), so no
`npm view` check applies. **`npm view` was therefore not required and not skipped** — the CR-037
trigger (a declared package + version) never fires in M4.

Locked versions from `sprint-context.md` §Locked Versions are unchanged: Node `>=24.0.0`,
TypeScript `^5.8.0`, `js-yaml` as pinned. No M4 item upgrades any of them.

`.cleargate/FLASHCARD.md` is on this dispatch's forbidden-write list. Append at Gate 4, newest on
top, after a dupe grep.

- `2026-08-29 · #dogfood-split #citations #danger · .claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md has DRIFTED live-vs-canonical (777 vs 787) because STORY-054-03's Gate-4 re-sync never ran, so EVERY live line number in an unexecuted work item is wrong by +9/+10 for the tree you must commit. Four SPRINT-39 M4 items cite it. Locate by heading text; treat a "byte-identical today" precondition as expiring. [SPRINT-39 M4]`
- `2026-08-29 · #test-harness #danger · .cleargate/scripts/state-scripts.test.mjs is invoked by NOTHING — not the precommit gate (which runs the cli suite), not CI (there is none) — and it has carried a stale `schema_version === 1` assertion since STORY-070-01 bumped the schema to 3. Two M4 items name it as their acceptance harness. Before trusting a test file, grep for its runner. [SPRINT-39 M4]`
- `2026-08-29 · #gate #readiness-gates #danger · A `section(N) has ≥N declared-item` criterion CANNOT express "this table is populated" for story.md: §4 Quality Gates already scores 6 declared-item unedited, so every threshold ≤6 is vacuous and ≥7 fails honest stories. The closed-set predicate shape (task-breakdown-complete, readiness-predicates.ts:1147) is the only non-vacuous route — and its absence-passes branch IS the grandfathering mechanism, so no version guard is needed. [SPRINT-39 CR-111]`
- `2026-08-29 · #gate #danger · There is no version-guard predicate and there cannot be one: compareValues implements >= / <= as Number(a) >= Number(expected) (readiness-predicates.ts:290-291), so `created_at_version >= 'cleargate@0.25.0'` is NaN >= NaN — false, always. Any grandfathering plan that assumes a version comparison is unbuildable. [SPRINT-39 CR-111]`
- `2026-08-29 · #id-parsing #danger · `{ID}` is not one placeholder, it is three: bare number (bug_id: "BUG-{ID}"), full id (hotfix_id: "{ID}", spike_id: "{ID}"), and the PARENT's number (story.md:67 parent_epic_ref: "EPIC-{ID}"). A "normalize the token" refactor that does not first pick a semantic renders BUG-BUG-044. And pad width is per-type: SPRINT is 2-digit unpadded, everything else 3-digit. [SPRINT-39 CR-108]`
- `2026-08-29 · #test-harness #regex #danger · A CLAUDE.md `block-equal` check written with an UNANCHORED marker regex returns a confident `true` about the WRONG block — CLAUDE.md:50 mentions the markers inline INSIDE the block, so the non-greedy match ends 8 lines early and both files agree at 10606 instead of 11762. BUG-043's defect, reproduced inside the check meant to guard against it, passing. Anchor with ^…$/m and assert the LENGTH too. [SPRINT-39 M4 / CR-108]`
- `2026-08-29 · #test-harness #danger · An allocator and a rolling-window cap can read the same directory for opposite reasons: hotfix.ts:106-108 filters archive/ by a 7-day mtime for the CAP, and a Developer fixing the archive-blind ALLOCATOR 60 lines away will copy it — passing every id test and re-opening the bug for any archive older than a week. Test the backdated-mtime case explicitly, in both directions. [SPRINT-39 BUG-045]`
- `2026-08-29 · #process #danger · process.exit() does NOT run finally blocks. update_state.mjs exits 13 times, including :229's idempotency no-op — the commonest path in normal operation — so a lockfile released in a finally leaks on the second identical invocation and the tool self-deadlocks. Register the release as process.on('exit'). [SPRINT-39 BUG-044]`
- `2026-08-29 · #surface-declaration · Two SPRINT-39 M4 items repeat CR-105's "right row, wrong reason" mode in a single milestone: CR-110 says reporter.md holds the met|partial|missed verdict (it holds zero of those words; SKILL.md does) and CR-111 says section-predicate machinery can express test-layers-declared (measured: it cannot). Both files are still genuinely needed. Replace the justification; never delete the row. [SPRINT-39 M4]`
- `2026-08-29 · #gate #danger · file_surface_diff.sh globs STORY-<num>_*.md only (BUG-059), so an ENTIRE milestone of BUG-/CR- items runs with the surface gate inert — SPRINT-39 M4 is 8 for 8. When a milestone has no Story-prefixed items, the Architect plan and QA-Verify's diff read are the ONLY surface controls that exist. [SPRINT-39 M4]`
- `2026-08-29 · #process · PIPE_BUF governs pipes, not regular files, and on darwin it is 512 — not the 4096 an event-log design cited. The property an O_APPEND JSONL log actually relies on is that seek-to-end + write is atomic w.r.t. other writers on a regular file. A correct design can rest on a wrong reason; record the real guarantee or the next reader sizes records against a meaningless number. [SPRINT-39 CR-106]`

> **TPV RULING — BUG-046 (2026-08-29).** Mutation gate between QA-Red (`f5d587a4`) and Developer.
> Verdict **`TPV: rulings-required`**. Full measurement, kill matrix and the patch:
> `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/BUG-046-tpv.md`. Binds the BUG-046 Developer dispatch and
> QA-Verify. Nothing here overrides N1-N10 or R1-R8.
>
> **T1 — Seven mutants survive the shipped baseline at 14/14 with no witness.** `M1b` the
> `existsSync` hybrid (`nested OR (untracked AND on-disk)`, label-blind); `M3` serialize-instead-of-
> refuse whose prose contains the word "refuse"; `M4b` a dispatch-time refusal placed in a *new*
> `.cleargate/scripts/*.mjs`; `M5` fixing two of the three doctrine sites; `M7` nested-repo
> detection by hardcoded `mcp/`/`cleargate-cli/`/`admin/` prefixes; `M8` deleting the two doctrine
> sentences instead of correcting them; `M10` dropping `collision_surface.sh`'s stdout surface
> entirely. Four of the seven are the named mutant of the case written to guard against them.
>
> **T2 — The baseline IS satisfiable and IS well-formed. This is a coverage ruling, not a rejection.**
> A reference implementation scores **14/14** (`git ls-files` + walk-up `.git` probe + guarded
> `check-ignore` + create-row exemption + doc edits). All six reds are red because the feature is
> absent, not because the harness is broken — verified individually. The split held: the diff is two
> files, both `test_file_surface.sh`, byte-identical (`md5 b2062a26…`), zero BUG-062 scope, zero
> live `.claude/**` paths, and `.claude/` does not exist in the worktree at all (N1 satisfied).
>
> **T3 — C3 has no unique kill and does not stop the mutant it names.** C3 fires only against the
> *pure* `existsSync` classifier, which C1 and C4 already kill. The variant a Developer would write
> (`M1b`) passes C3 because C3's path happens not to exist on disk, and passes C4 because C4's path
> happens to exist. Cause: **C1's fixture creates `vendor/lib.ts` on disk (`test_file_surface.sh:300`).**
> Consequence if shipped: on any checkout without the gitignored siblings — a fresh clone, CI — `M1b`
> flags **nothing**, which is precisely the blindness BUG-046 exists to remove.
>
> **T4 — C6 cannot distinguish refuse from serialize.** It is two *independent whole-file* greps
> (`refus` anywhere ∧ `unreachable` anywhere), so a serializing `architect-synth.md` that says
> *"we do not **refuse** stories for metadata reasons"* passes. Measured: the serialize mutant scores
> 14/14; it only dies (13/14) if its prose omits the string `refus` entirely. The human's 2026-08-26
> decision is **refuse**, and the baseline does not enforce it.
>
> **T5 — C13 is itself the mutant it was written to kill. There are THREE live occurrences, not two.**
> C13's roots are `.cleargate/knowledge` + `cleargate-planning/.claude`. The third occurrence is
> `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-enforcement.md:89` — the Cross-Cutting-Rule-1
> byte-identical mirror — outside both roots. Measured: fixing the two visible sites scores **14/14
> while the false claim survives and Rule 1 is violated**. C13 also asserts only *absence*, so
> deleting both sentences goes green (`M8`), and — the inverse trap — the most natural correct
> rewording, negating the claim in place (*"are NOT visible … as a subdirectory"*), goes **red**.
> C13 bans a phrasing, not a claim; a green C13 is not evidence the doc is now correct.
>
> **T6 — C2's fixture pins the implementation technique and admits `M7`.** `mkdir -p "${TMPDIR6}/mcp/.git"`
> (`:320`) is not a git repository: measured, `git -C …/mcp rev-parse --show-toplevel` returns the
> **outer** root. So the git-native nested probe scores **13/14 — a correct implementation rejected** —
> while a hardcoded prefix list scores 14/14. One line fixes both: `cs_init_repo "${TMPDIR6}/mcp"`.
>
> **T7 — C5's stated rationale is false; C12's file pin is too narrow; C4 is the real `set -e` witness.**
> `set -e` is **suspended inside `if` conditions**, so `if is_ignored "$P"` needs no guard at all; only
> a bare-statement `IGN="$(git check-ignore …)"` in the loop body dies, and **C4** catches that, not C5
> — C5's fixture declares a *tracked* path and short-circuits before any `check-ignore` call. C12's
> `launch_wave.mjs` `reachab`-count pin is correct (baseline verified = 1, at `:59`) but pins one file
> by name: a dispatch-time check in any other script is invisible. This is the sub-check with teeth
> beyond the test — CR-108 (w12) declares `cleargate-cli/src/**` paths, so a mis-scoped refusal
> retroactively voids waves 11-13.
>
> **T8 — The wire format QA-Red pinned is upheld, with one correction and one addition.** `CLEARGATE_REPO_ROOT`
> (`file_surface_diff.sh:29` precedent — `collision_surface.sh` reads it **nowhere** today, so honouring
> it is task row #1), the `unreachable` and `nested` markers, and the `New Files Needed` create-row
> exemption (`architect-reader.md:33`, general rule at `:45`) are **BINDING**. Exit code is **FREE** —
> `exit 0`-with-annotation and `exit 3`-on-flag both measured at 14/14. **Addition:** stdout must keep
> emitting the bare surface one path per line (`collision_surface.sh:12-18`; architect-synth's only
> input) — nothing in the baseline asserts this today, hence `M10`.
>
> **T9 — Required amendment P1-P7, applied by QA-Red on `story/BUG-046`, test files only, both trees
> byte-identical.** P1 stop creating C1's file on disk (kills M1b) · P2 real nested repo + new **C2b**
> under a non-`mcp` name (kills M7, admits both probes) · P3 add the canonical `cleargate-planning/.cleargate/knowledge`
> root + broaden the verb alternation (kills M5) · P4 same-line `unreachab`∧`refus` coupling with a
> `serializ` exclusion, plus a `.cleargate/scripts/` reachability-vocabulary census against the measured
> baseline set `{collision_surface.sh, launch_wave.mjs, assert_story_files.mjs}` (kills M3, M4b) · P5
> two-row C5 fixture so the ignored-probe is reached (C5 finally fires under its own mutant) · P6 new
> **C13b**, all three doctrine files must couple `worktree` to `tracked` (kills M8) · P7 separate
> `CS_STDOUT` and assert the surface contract (kills M10). Patch verified at
> `$SCRATCH/BUG-046-qa-red-amendment.patch`, 167 lines. **The Developer does not apply this** — a
> Developer amending its own acceptance test is the tampering shape.
>
> **T10 — Numbers for the Developer dispatch. Measured, not predicted.**
> `bash .cleargate/scripts/test/test_file_surface.sh` (live path only; the canonical mirror is a
> `diff`-parity artefact, correctly not independently executed).
> Clean pre-QA-Red `9c1ba35f`: **`6/6 passed, 0 failed`**. QA-Red as shipped `f5d587a4`:
> **`8/14 passed, 6 failed`** (3× in the worktree + 1× in an isolated sandbox, byte-identical).
> **After P1-P7, before the Developer: `8/16 passed, 8 failed`.**
> **Developer acceptance line: `Results: 16/16 passed, 0 failed`, exit 0** — measured against two
> independent correct implementations. Flip RED→GREEN: **C1 C2 C2b C4 C6 C12 C13 C13b**. Stay GREEN:
> the six `file_surface_diff.sh` assertions (C7), **C3**, **C5** (which now carries C5b).
> *If the amendment is skipped, the acceptance line is `14/14 passed, 0 failed` and all seven T1
> mutants ship green* — then they must be carried into the Developer dispatch as explicit

> **TPV RULING — BUG-045 (2026-08-29).** Mutation-testing gate between QA-Red and Developer, wave 10.
> Verdict **`TPV: rulings-required`** — not a QA-Red bounce; the wiring is sound and the four authored
> reds are genuine, so **`arch_bounces` must not increment**. Full measurement:
> `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/BUG-045-tpv.md`. All mutants were built and run **out of tree**
> (`<scratchpad>/mut/`, `BASE` verified `diff`-identical to the shipped `hotfix.ts`); nothing under
> `cleargate-cli/src`, `cleargate-cli/test` or the outer repo was written. Control reproduced exactly:
> `BASE` → `pass 3 · fail 4`, failing **R1 R2 R4 R6**.
>
> **T1 — FOUR mutants survive the authored R1–R7 baseline.** 17 variants measured.
> - **M9** — the `classifyType(id) === 'HOTFIX'` filter dropped while widening the helper. Passes
>   R1–R7 *and* the item's real-tree collision argument: on this repo it allocates **`HOTFIX-115`**
>   (it tracks the CR/EPIC/STORY numbering), colliding with nothing. **Collision-freedom is not a
>   sufficient acceptance criterion for this fix.** Killed only by new **R14**.
> - **M6b / M6c** — one `try/catch` around the whole directory loop instead of one per directory.
>   R3 cannot reach either: its fixture leaves `pending-sync/` present-and-empty, so the directory
>   that throws is also the one with no data to lose. Realistic because **git tracks no empty
>   directories**. Killed only by new **R9** (both) and **R10** (M6b).
> - **M3b** — a hand-rolled, case-*sensitive*, underscore-only regex `/^HOTFIX-(\d+)_/`. R4 exercises
>   only the case-*in*sensitivity direction. This is verbatim the pattern `hotfix.ts:41-42` records as
>   having already shipped once. Killed only by new **R12** / **R13**.
> - **M5d** — pad width inherited from the widest scanned stem (default 3). Every id in R1–R7 is
>   already 3 digits. Load-bearing for **CR-108 F4** (`SPRINT` is 2-digit). Killed only by new **R11**.
>
> With R9–R15 ruled in, **zero mutants survive**: 17 built, 17 killed, `FIX` green at 14/14.
>
> **T2 — the item's §2 repro is DIAGNOSTIC; it is §5 case 2 that is decorative.** Measured, and this
> corrects the dispatch premise. §2 executed verbatim (create → `mv` to archive → create) is **RED at

> archive-blind max is already `2`, so the unfixed code is right by coincidence. QA-Red's substituted

> comment calls it "orthogonal to the archive-scan defect", and that isolation is exactly what makes

> M5c, but not M6b/M6c. **R7** is correct and correctly scoped — keep verbatim.

> exported helpers `hotfix.ts:17` imports: `pending-sync/` holds no `HOTFIX-*`; `archive/` holds
