## TPV RULING — BUG-044 (2026-08-29)

> **TPV: rulings-required.** Full battery, method and measurements:
> `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/BUG-044-tpv.md`. Subject: `.worktrees/BUG-044` @ `e55431ec`.
> Ten mutants built out-of-tree from the committed baseline, applied, measured, destroyed.
> Eight killed deterministically (3/3 each). **Two survivors.** Baseline determinism re-measured
> **12/12 identical** (`tests 14 · suites 12 · pass 10 · fail 4 · skipped 0`) — with QA-Red's 18/18,
> **30/30**. Where this conflicts with the BUG-044 plan body, this wins; nothing here overrides N1-N10
> or R1-R8.

**Survivors, first:**

- **M6 — release registered only on the happy paths (no `process.on('exit')`) survives 3/3 at
  `pass 14 · fail 0`.** Nine non-zero `process.exit` sites fire inside the critical section
  (`update_state.mjs:102,:110,:130,:135,:146,:166,:184,:201,:223`) and **none** is covered; S3 covers
  only `:229`. Demonstrated leaking on three of them. `state.json.lock` is **not gitignored**, and
  `validate_bounce_readiness.mjs:98-101` hard-fails on any dirty tree — so the leak halts the next
  story in the sprint with a diagnostic that never names `update_state`.
- **M3 — lock released between `writeFileSync(tmp)` and `renameSync` survives 6 of 10 runs.**
  **S2, the scenario this plan assigns to exactly that mutant, was green 10/10 against it.** S2 killed
  zero mutants in the battery.

**T1 — BLOCKING, routes to QA-Red.** Add one deterministic case: after an error exit
(`--qa-bounce` on an already-`Escalated` story, `:184`; plus story-not-found, `:135`), assert
`!fs.existsSync(stateFile + '.lock')`. ~15 lines, no concurrency. It is **green at baseline** and red
only under M6 — a regression guard of S3's class; do not reshape it to be red. Harness after T1:
`tests 15 · suites 13 · pass 11 · fail 4` at QA-Red HEAD, `tests 15 · suites 13 · pass 15 · fail 0 ·
skipped 0` after the fix.

**T2 — S2's mutant claim is struck.** S2 stays as §5-case-2 acceptance coverage ("file stays valid
JSON"), not as a release-before-`renameSync` guard. **M3 is an accepted documented residual.**
Compensating control: **QA-Verify MUST read the `update_state.mjs` diff and confirm release happens
strictly after `renameSync` returns.** No test enforces it. Added to the kick-back list below.

**T3 — BLOCKING on the Developer. The harness over-constrains the retry budget; remove the coupling.**
The barrier shim arms on the first read of the state file, which under this plan's own Gotcha 2 sits
**inside** the lock — so every serialized holder pays its 300ms inactivity window, and S1's twenty
holders serialize into ~6s. Measured, correct implementations differing **only** in
`LOCK_RETRY_BUDGET_MS`: **2000 → 12/14 (S1 + addendum red, 3/3); 5000 → 13/14 (S1 red, 3/3); 7000 →
14/14 (3/3); 9000 → 14/14 (3/3).** The failure is `every invocation should exit 0` at `:490` with
stderr `could not acquire lock … held by pid N` — the lock is correct, the budget is short, and the
message one line lower blames three things that are all false. Upper bound ~9800ms is fixed by S5
(`elapsedMs < 10000`, `spawnSync timeout: 10000`); lower bound ~6.5-7s scales with machine speed.
**Ruling: implement the budget PER-HOLDER — record the holder's `pid`+`at` and reset the deadline
whenever the holder changes.** Measured `LOCK_RETRY_BUDGET_MS = 2000` with progress-reset →
**14/14/0 in 4/4 runs, ~14s.** If a flat budget is used instead it MUST be **8000ms**, with a code
comment stating the lower bound is a test-harness artifact.

**T4 — R1 is NOT satisfied by BUG-044 as committed, and its cost figure is wrong.**
`command grep -rn "state-scripts"` still returns exactly two hits, both in the file's own docstring
(`:2`, `:6`), after four QA-Red commits; `config.yml` `gates.precommit` runs the **cli** suite only.
Adding a runner is outside BUG-044's three-row surface and would trip its own kick-back criterion 7 —
so BUG-044 owes CR-106 the **green**, and CR-106/the orchestrator owes the **runner**. Three corrected
facts for that wiring: post-fix wall-clock is **14-22s** (not QA-Red's 5-10s; baseline is ~4s); the
file spawns **32 real node child processes** per run; and it must run **single-concurrency** — S1's
lock-serialization already sits within ~3s of S5's hard 10s ceiling.

**T5 — `state-scripts.test.mjs:186`'s title still says `schema_version=1`** while its body asserts 3.
Stale prose left by the commit that removed a stale assertion. Fix in the Developer's commit.

**T6 — do NOT add a `.gitignore` rule for `state.json.lock`.** Ruled here so nobody spends a dispatch
on it: it would add a fourth row to a three-row surface and hide a real signal. The mitigation is the
`process.on('exit')` release registered at acquire (measured clean on all three error paths) plus the
liveness steal. **Orchestrator operational note:** if any wave-11/12/13 transition reports "git working
tree is dirty", check for `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-39/state.json.lock` and `rm` it first.

**Accepted without change:** commit A is correct — `3` is witnessed by `constants.mjs:31`,
`state.schema.json:17-21` (`"const": 3`), `init_sprint.mjs:221`, `_migrate-schema-v3.mjs:39-40` and
the live SPRINT-39 `state.json`; the two-assertion form is exactly N3 part 2 and masks nothing. All
four reds (S1 `:473`, S4 `:627`, S5 `:667`, addendum `:719`) are accepted as structurally real and
deterministic. **The v1-seeded addendum is load-bearing, not an extra: M1b — lock acquired
post-migration with an authoritative re-read inside it — is green on S1 3/3 and red on the addendum
3/3, and it runs FASTER than a correct fix.** A Developer who lands M1b sees a fast green S1.
S3 is confirmed the highest-value case in the milestone: it is the sole killer of M7
(`finally`-only release), via the residual-lock assertion at `:594`, not the third-invocation hang.

**Correction to the QA-Red report before anyone quotes its figures:** S1 does not "lose 1-5 of 20" —
it **keeps** 2-5 of 20 (6 runs kept 2, 3 kept 3, 1 kept 4, 2 kept 5); the addendum keeps 1-3 of 10.
**Correction to this plan:** `update_state.mjs` has **twelve** `process.exit()` sites, not thirteen.

**BUG-044 kick-back criteria — amended. The plan's eight stand; four are added:**

9. The lock is released before `renameSync` returns (M3 — no test catches this; QA-Verify reads the diff).
10. Release is not registered as `process.on('exit')` at acquire, so any of the ten in-lock exit sites leaks (M6).
11. A flat retry budget outside `[7000, 9800]`ms, or a per-holder budget without the holder-change reset (T3).
12. T1's error-exit case is absent, or was made red by reshaping instead of reported as a green-at-baseline guard.

**Task rows — amended (supersede the BUG-044 `### Task rows` block above):**

```
- [ ] QA-Red re-entry (T1): add the error-exit no-lock-leak case; expect 15/11/4
- [ ] Implement the lock: 'wx', acquire after :95 before the :99 read, section spans :116/:122
- [ ] Release via process.on('exit') registered at acquire; verify all 10 in-lock exits leave no lock
- [ ] Release strictly AFTER renameSync returns (M3 — unguarded by tests)
- [ ] Steal guarded by pid liveness (EPERM = ALIVE) AND an age ceiling; never unconditional
- [ ] Retry budget PER-HOLDER, 2000ms, deadline resets on holder change (T3)
- [ ] Fix the stale test title at state-scripts.test.mjs:186 (T5)
- [ ] Mirror byte-identically into cleargate-planning/.cleargate/scripts/update_state.mjs
- [ ] Commit C with both trees; run node --test; record `tests 15 · suites 13 · pass 15 · fail 0 · skipped 0` verbatim
- [ ] Verify diff between trees is empty; no file outside the three-row surface staged; no .gitignore edit (T6)
```

## ORCHESTRATOR RULING — OD-3 and OD-4 (2026-08-29, pre-w12)

Both were flagged by the M4 Architect as "must be decided before dispatch". Decided here, on
measurement, so wave 12 dispatches without a planning halt.

### OD-3 — `cleargate new <type>` does NOT strip `<instructions>` blocks. Ruled: **no stripping.**

Measured directly, not inferred:

- Eight of ten templates carry exactly one `<instructions>` block (`Bug.md`, `CR.md`,
  `Sprint Plan Template.md`, `epic.md`, `hotfix.md`, `initiative.md`, `spike.md`, `story.md`);
  `sprint_context.md` and `sprint_report.md` carry none. **`hotfix.md` is in the carrying set.**
- The shipping renderer strips nothing. `hotfixNewHandler` reads the template and performs
  exactly three substitutions — `cleargate-cli/src/commands/hotfix.ts:188-191`:
  `.replace(/\{ID\}/g, idStr).replace(/\{SLUG\}/g, opts.slug).replace(/\{ISO\}/g, now)`.
  `grep -n 'instructions' src/commands/hotfix.ts` returns **nothing**.
- The one real scaffolded artefact, `archive/HOTFIX-001_init_skip_strips_exec_bit.md`, carries
  **zero** `<instructions>` blocks while its own template carries one. Since the renderer cannot
  have removed it, **it was hand-cleaned after scaffolding.** That is the actual current workflow,
  and it is a human editorial step, not a renderer behaviour.

**Ruling — three reasons, in order of weight:**

1. **CR-108 §4 case 7 demands `cleargate new hotfix` be byte-identical to today's `hotfix new`.**
   Stripping violates that by construction: `hotfix.md` carries a block, so a stripping renderer
   cannot reproduce the current output. The two requirements are mutually exclusive as written, and
   byte-identical regression is the one that protects a command that already ships to users.
2. **The block is the drafting agent's contract.** Every work-item template's `<instructions>`
   block is what tells the agent to render a Brief and halt — the universal pre-push handshake in
   CLAUDE.md. Removing it at scaffold time deletes the instruction from the artefact the agent
   subsequently reads.
3. **Stripping is separable.** If hand-cleaning proves a real burden across nine types, it is its
   own CR with its own regression case. It is not a precondition of "one scaffolder for every type",
   and folding it in would make case 7 unverifiable.

CR-108's Developer implements substitution-only rendering, matching `hotfix.ts:188-191`. Any
`<instructions>` handling is **out of scope** and a kick-back if it appears.

### OD-4 — the sprint-goal verdict is spoken in the Brief, NOT written into `sprint_report.md`. Ruled: **not this sprint.**

Adopting the Architect's own recommendation, for its stated reason: the report template carries zero
goal/verdict content today, and adding a section interacts with `close_sprint.mjs:397-409`'s
required-row regex list — which CR-110 itself places in Do-NOT-modify. The `GOAL_RELATION` line
(§Q5-B) already gives the Reporter what it needs without touching the close gate. CR-110 ships the
acceptance check; it does not reshape the report.

### Status of the remaining open decisions

| OD-1 | **Ship CR-106 with the mitigations** — E7, E8, E9 and the named rollback (`git revert -m 1 <merge commit>`, never `git reset --hard`) | human decision at the M4 planning halt, against the Architect's recommendation to defer |
| OD-2 | **Split** — BUG-046 narrowed to C1-C7/C12/C13; C8-C11 filed as BUG-062 (unapproved, out of sprint) | human decision at the M4 planning halt |
| OD-3 | **No stripping** | this ruling |
| OD-4 | **Brief only, not the report** | this ruling |
| OD-5 | **Rows written into all nine item files before any worktree was cut** | orchestrator, done pre-w10 |
| OD-6 | **All three amendments applied** — CR-108 §Q5-A F1, CR-110 F1, CR-111 F6; each REPLACED a justification, none deleted a row | orchestrator, done pre-dispatch |
| OD-7 | **CLOSED by R7 — CR-111 ships FULL, cross-repo.** F6 removed the `section(N)` route; F8/F9 supply one needing no version guard. w13 is single-item, so cross-repo costs no parallelism. The §2 escape clause stays unused. **X14 rider:** CR-111's cli branch point is whatever cli `main` is *after CR-108 merges* — not `e4cb49f`. | R7, M4 pre-dispatch ruling |
